Multivariable ( φ , 𝒪 K × ) (\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}) -modules and local-global compatibility
Christophe BreuilaFlorian HerzigbYongquan Huc, Stefano Morradf, Benjamin Schraenef

Abstract
Let p p be a prime number, K K a finite unramified extension of ℚ p \mathbb{Q}_{p} and 𝔽 \mathbb{F} a finite extension of 𝔽 p \mathbb{F}_{p} . Using perfectoid spaces we associate to any finite-dimensional continuous representation ρ ¯ \overline{\rho} of Gal ⁡ ( K ¯ / K ) {\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K) over 𝔽 \mathbb{F} an étale ( φ , 𝒪 K × ) (\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}) -module D A ⊗ ​ ( ρ ¯ ) D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho}) over a completed localization A A of 𝔽 ​ ⟦ 𝒪 K ⟧ \mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket . We conjecture that one can also associate an étale ( φ , 𝒪 K × ) (\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}) -module D A ​ ( π ) D_{A}(\pi) to any smooth representation π \pi of GL 2 ⁡ ( K ) \GL_{2}(K) occurring in some Hecke eigenspace of the mod p p cohomology of a Shimura curve, and that moreover D A ​ ( π ) D_{A}(\pi) is isomorphic (up to twist) to D A ⊗ ​ ( ρ ¯ ) D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho}) , where ρ ¯ \overline{\rho} is the underlying 2 2 -dimensional representation of Gal ⁡ ( K ¯ / K ) {\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K) . Using previous work of the same authors, we prove this conjecture when ρ ¯ \overline{\rho} is semi-simple and sufficiently generic.



1
Introduction

Let
p
be a prime number. The main motivation of this work
is the investigation of the (hoped for) mod
p
Langlands
correspondence for
\GL_{2}(K)
, where
K
is a finite unramified
extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The case
K=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is now well known
(
[
Bre03
]
,
[
Col10a
]
,
[
Eme
]
), whereas the case
K\neq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is
still resisting after more than 10 years (
[
BP12
]
). An important
aspect of the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-case is the construction by Colmez in

loc. cit.
of an exact functor from the category of admissible finite length mod

p
representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
to the category of finite-dimensional continuous mod
p
representations of

\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. The construction of this functor uses, as an intermediate step, Fontaine’s category of

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. In a previous article (
[
BHH+
]
), we
constructed an exact functor
D_{A}^{\acute{\mathrm{e}}\mathrm{t}}
from a “good”
subcategory of admissible mod
p
representations of
\GL_{2}(K)
to a
category of étale multivariable
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules. These multivariable

(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules are
A
-modules with additional
structures, where
A
is a ring obtained as a completed localization
of the Iwasawa algebra of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. In this work we propose a
construction of a functor
D_{A}^{\otimes}
from the category of
continuous mod
p
representations of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
to the
category of étale multivariable

(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules. This construction is based on the
equivalence, also due to Fontaine (
[
Fon90
]
), between mod
p

representations of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
and Lubin–Tate étale

(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules. One of the main obstructions to pass from
Lubin–Tate
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules to multivariable

(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
lies in the comparison
between the
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
-action on
A
and the

\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
-action on (some tensor power of) the structural ring
of the Lubin–Tate group. To solve this problem, we need to work at a perfectoid level and
use the “Abel–Jacobi map” considered by Fargues in
[
Far20
]
. We then
prove, under some conditions, that the two functors
D_{A}^{\acute{\mathrm{e}}\mathrm{t}}
and

D_{A}^{\otimes}
satisfy a local-global compatibility property in
the completed cohomology of a tower of Shimura curves.

We now describe in more detail the content of this article.

Let
F
be a totally real number field and let
X_{U}
be the smooth projective Shimura curve over
F
associated to a quaternion algebra
D
of center
F
(which splits at one infinite place) and to a compact open subgroup
U
of
(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty})^{\times}
. For
v
a place of
F
above
p
which splits
D
and
\mathbb{F}
a finite extension of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
(“sufficiently large”, as usual), consider the admissible smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(F_{v})
over
\mathbb{F}

\pi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varinjlim_{U_{v}}\Hom_{{\rm Gal}(\overline{F}/F)}\!\big(\overline{r},H^{1}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(X_{U^{v}U_{v}}\times_{F}\overline{F},\mathbb{F})\big),

(1)

where
U^{v}
is a fixed compact open subgroup of
(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty,v})^{\times}
,
\overline{r}:{\rm Gal}(\overline{F}/F)\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
is an absolutely irreducible continuous Galois representation such that
\pi\neq 0
, and where the inductive limit runs over compact open subgroups
U_{v}
of
(D\otimes_{F}F_{v})^{\times}\cong\GL_{2}(F_{v})
. In this introduction, we moreover assume for simplicity that
v
is the only
p
-adic place of
F
and that we are in a “multiplicity
1
” situation, which then roughly means that
U^{v}
is “as big as possible” (in general, one needs to take into account the action of certain operators, which requires mild assumptions on
F
,
D
and
\overline{r}
, see (
65
)).

We know that the isomorphism class of
\pi
always determines the one of
\overline{r}_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\overline{r}|_{{\rm Gal}(\overline{F}_{v}/F_{v})}
, see
[
BD14
]
,
[
Sch18
]
. We also expect that
\pi
is always of finite length, which is known in several cases, see
[
HW22
]
,
[
BHH+
]
. However, the representation
\pi
is still not understood when
F_{v}\neq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, in particular we have the key question:

Question 1.1
.

Assume
F_{v}\neq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, does
\pi
only depend on
\overline{r}_{v}
?

Question
1.1
, as routine as it may seem at first, has
unfortunately proven to be surprisingly difficult, and there is not
one single instance of a
\pi
as in (
1
) for which we know
the answer. For instance the mod
p
étale cohomology of the
Drinfeld tower in dimension
1
, which provides a smooth
representation of
\GL_{2}(F_{v})
only depending on
\overline{r}_{v}
, cannot
give rise to representations like
\pi
as soon as
F_{v}\neq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, see

[
CDN23
]
(together with
[
Sch15
]
,
[
Wu21
]
). On the other hand,
we know that, for
F_{v}
unramified and most
\overline{r}_{v}
, the diagram

(\pi^{I_{1}}\hookrightarrow\pi^{K_{1}})
(where
K_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}1+pM_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})\subseteq I_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}
pro-
p
-Iwahori) only depends on

\overline{r}_{v}
, and this is a really non-trivial fact, see
[
DL21
]
. We
do not answer Question
1.1
in this work, but we provide one
further step towards the understanding of the representation
\pi
,
and certainly Question
1.1
was a motivation. More precisely, we
completely describe the multivariable étale

(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}(\pi)
associated to
\pi
in

[
BHH+
, §3]
when
F_{v}
is unramified and
\overline{r}_{v}
is
semi-simple sufficiently generic, in particular we prove that it only
depends on
\overline{r}_{v}
, and we provide a precise conjecture on what

D_{A}(\pi)
should be for all
\overline{r}_{v}
(and
F_{v}
unramified),
crucially using perfectoid spaces. As an intermediate result, we
construct a new fully faithful functor from continuous representations
of
{\rm Gal}(\overline{F}_{v}/F_{v})
over
\mathbb{F}
to a certain category of
multivariable étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}^{\times})
-modules: this is
the functor
D_{A}^{(0)}
constructed in Corollary
2.6.7
.

Let us first recall the definition of these modules. Let
K
be a finite unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of degree
f\geq 1
, then we can write the Iwasawa algebra
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
as
\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma},\ \sigma\!:\!\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}\rrbracket
for
Y_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\sigma(\lambda)^{-1}[\lambda]\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
, where
q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}p^{f}
and
[\lambda]\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
is the multiplicative representative of
\lambda
(seen in
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
). We then define
A
to be the completion of
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket[1/Y_{\sigma},\ \sigma\!:\!\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}]
for the
(Y_{\sigma})_{\sigma}
-adic topology (in a suitable sense), see (
16
) for the precise definition. In fact
A
is isomorphic to the Tate algebra
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(Y_{\sigma})\mkern-3.0mu)\langle(Y_{\sigma^{\prime}}/Y_{\sigma})^{\pm 1},\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma\rangle
for any choice of
\sigma
, see Lemma
2.6.1
. It is endowed with an
\mathbb{F}
-linear Frobenius
\varphi
coming from the multiplication by
p
on
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and with a commuting continuous action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
coming from its action on
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
(by multiplication on
\mathcal{O}_{K}
). Then an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
is by definition a finite free
A
-module endowed with a semi-linear Frobenius
\varphi
whose image generates everything and a commuting continuous semi-linear action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
. Replacing
\varphi
on
A
by
\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi^{f}
, we define in the same way étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
. When
f=1
, the two definitions recover Fontaine’s classical
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-modules (or
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules) in characteristic
p
.

Now let
\pi
be an admissible smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
over
\mathbb{F}
. We endow
\pi^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}(\pi,\mathbb{F})
with the
\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}
-adic topology, where
\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}
is the maximal ideal of the Iwasawa algebra
\mathbb{F}\llbracket I_{1}\rrbracket
. In particular we can see
\pi^{\vee}
as an
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
-module via
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket\cong\mathbb{F}\llbracket\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{1}&{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\\
{0}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\rrbracket\subseteq\mathbb{F}\llbracket I_{1}\rrbracket
. We define

D_{A}(\pi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\big(\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket[1/Y_{\sigma},\sigma\!:\!\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket}\pi^{\vee}\big)^{\wedge},

where the completion is for the tensor product topology, see
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
or §
3.1
. Even though
D_{A}(\pi)
is an
A
-module endowed with a semi-linear action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
(coming from the action of
\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}&{0}\\
{0}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
on
\pi^{\vee}
), it is not clear if it has good properties in general (it might not have a Frobenius
\varphi
, it might not be of finite type, etc.). But we know that
D_{A}(\pi)
is an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module of rank
2^{f}
for some of the
\pi
in (
1
) when
K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}F_{v}
is unramified, see
[
BHH+
, §1.3]
1
1

1

Note that, with the notation of
[
BHH+
, §3.1.2]
,
D_{A}(\pi)
is equal to its étale quotient
D_{A}(\pi)^{\acute{\mathrm{e}}\mathrm{t}}
in our case, see
[
BHH+
, Rem. 3.3.5.4(ii)]
.
together with Remark
2.6.2
. In fact we conjecture in this paper that
D_{A}(\pi)
is always an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
(hence equal to
D_{A}(\pi)^{\acute{\mathrm{e}}\mathrm{t}}
) of rank
2^{f}
for all representations
\pi
in (
1
) (when
F_{v}
is unramified).

On the Galois side, for
\overline{\rho}:{\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K)\!\rightarrow\GL_{n}(\mathbb{F})
(
n\geq 1
) a continuous representation
and
\sigma\!:\!\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
we can associate to
\overline{\rho}
a
Lubin–Tate
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module. Recall that it is an

n
-dimensional
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma})\mkern-3.0mu)
-vector space

D_{K,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
equipped with a semi-linear endomorphism

\varphi_{q}
whose image generates
D_{K,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
and a
commuting continuous action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
. Here
\varphi_{q}
is

\mathbb{F}
-linear and satisfies
\varphi_{q}(T_{K,\sigma})=T_{K,\sigma}^{q}
,
and the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma})\mkern-3.0mu)
is given
by the Lubin–Tate power series associated to the choice of logarithm

\sum_{n\geq 0}p^{-n}T_{K,\sigma}^{q^{n}}
composed with

\sigma\!:\!\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
on the coefficients. Recall we have

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}~D_{K,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}~D_{K,\sigma}(\overline{\rho}).

Assume now that
\overline{\rho}
is a direct sum of absolutely irreducible representations and define

D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}A\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]},

(2)

where the embedding
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow A
sends
T_{K,\sigma}^{q-1}
to
\varphi(Y_{\sigma})/Y_{\sigma}\in A
. We endow
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
with
\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi^{f}\otimes\varphi_{q}
. The embedding
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow A
does not commute with
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
, but one easily checks that, when
\overline{\rho}
is a direct sum of absolutely irreducible representations, there exists a unique (in a certain sense) continuous semi-linear action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
which commutes with
\varphi_{q}
and makes
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
of rank
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}
, see Lemma
2.2.2
. Moreover there is a canonical isomorphism
\id\otimes\varphi:A\otimes_{\varphi,A}D_{A,\sigma\circ\varphi}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
of étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
, where
\sigma\circ\varphi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sigma((-)^{p})
. We then define:

D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigotimes_{A,\sigma:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}}D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})

(3)

endowed with the “diagonal” action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
. Using the isomorphism
\id\otimes\varphi
, we can define a canonical endomorphism
\varphi:D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\rightarrow D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
which cyclically permutes the factors
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
, is semi-linear with respect to
\varphi
on
A
and is such that
\varphi^{f}=\varphi_{q}\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi_{q}
. It is then clear that
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
is an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
of rank
(\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho})^{f}
. The following theorem is our main result:

Theorem 1.2

(Corollary
3.1.4
)
.

Assume that
\overline{r}_{v}
is semi-simple and sufficiently generic
(
see (
68
)
)
, and assume standard technical assumptions on the global setting
(
see §
3.1
for precise statements
)
. Then there is an isomorphism of étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
over
A
, where
\overline{r}_{v}(1)
is the usual Tate twist of
\overline{r}_{v}
.

The proof of Theorem
1.2
is a long explicit computation of the dual étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
\Hom_{A}(D_{A}(\pi),A)
. Let us briefly indicate the various steps. We first describe
\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}(D_{A}(\pi),\mathbb{F})
, which is not so hard, see Proposition
3.2.3
. We then prove that there is a canonical injection

\Hom_{A}(D_{A}(\pi),A)\hookrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}(D_{A}(\pi),\mathbb{F})

induced by a nonzero continuous morphism
\mu:A\rightarrow\mathbb{F}
uniquely determined (up to scalar in
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
) by the condition
\mu\circ\psi\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}\mu
, where
\psi:A\rightarrow A
is a certain canonical left inverse of
\varphi
, see Lemma
3.2.1
, Proposition
3.3.1
and (
84
). To each Serre weight
\sigma
of
\overline{r}_{v}^{\vee}
we then associate in (
102
) a certain projective system
x_{\sigma}=(x_{\sigma,k})_{k\geq 0}
, where
x_{\sigma,k}\in\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{kf+1}]
, and we prove via Proposition
3.2.3
that
x_{\sigma}
lies in
\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}(D_{A}(\pi),\mathbb{F})
, see Lemma
3.4.10
and Proposition
3.5.1
. Then the key calculation is to prove that
x_{\sigma}
actually also lies in the submodule
\Hom_{A}(D_{A}(\pi),A)
, and that the
2^{f}
-tuple
(x_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in W(\overline{r}_{v}^{\vee})}
even forms an
A
-basis of the free
A
-module
\Hom_{A}(D_{A}(\pi),A)
, see Theorem
3.7.1
. For that we prove a crucial finiteness result (Proposition
3.6.1
) using the technical – but important – computations in
[
BHH+
, §3.2]
that we need to strengthen, see §
3.4
. Once all this is done, it is easy to derive the explicit actions of
\varphi
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\Hom_{A}(D_{A}(\pi),A)
, see Propositions
3.8.1
and
3.8.2
. We can then at last compare the two
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
D_{A}(\pi)
and
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
and prove that they are isomorphic, see Theorem
3.9.1
. The same proof works verbatim for quaternion algebras
D
which are definite at all infinite places (and split at
v
) and the representations
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(K)=\GL_{2}(F_{v})
defined analogously to (
1
).

There is no doubt to us that there should exist a more conceptual proof of Theorem
1.2
which will hopefully avoid both the genericity assumptions on
\overline{r}_{v}
and the technical computations. At present however, we do not know how to do this. But the first issue is to find a more conceptual definition of
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
and of
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
. Indeed, when
\overline{\rho}
is not semi-simple, the recipe (
2
) does not work in general because there might not always exist a continuous semi-linear action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
A\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
which commutes with
\varphi^{f}\otimes\varphi_{q}
(or such an action might not be unique) , see for instance
[
Wana
, §4]
. Using perfectoid spaces we give below a functorial construction of an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
, and subsequently of an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
, which works for all
\overline{\rho}
.

The first step is to replace the ring
A
by its perfectoid version

A_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(Y_{\sigma}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\left\langle(Y_{\sigma^{\prime}}/Y_{\sigma})^{\pm{1/p^{\infty}}},\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma\right\rangle

(4)

which is a perfectoid Tate algebra over the perfectoid field

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(Y_{\sigma}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
(for any
\sigma
). Using the
equivalence between finite étale
A
-algebras and finite étale

A_{\infty}
-algebras together with the equivalence between locally constant étale sheaves of finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector spaces on
\Spec(R)
and finite projective
R
-modules with an action of Frobenius for perfect rings
R
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
,
it is not hard to check that the extension of scalars
(-)\mapsto(-)\otimes_{A}A_{\infty}
induces an equivalence of categories between étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
and étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A_{\infty}
, and similarly with
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
instead of
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
, see Corollary
2.6.6
. Hence we may as well look for a definition of
D_{A_{\infty},\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
and
D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
.

It is now convenient to fix an embedding
\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
and set
\sigma_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sigma_{0}\circ\varphi^{i}
for
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The second step is to consider the two perfectoid spaces

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{LT}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Spa\big(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket\big)\times_{\Spa(\mathbb{F})}\cdots\times_{\Spa(\mathbb{F})}\Spa\big(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket\big)}_{f\mathrm{\ times}}

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\Spa\Big(\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket,\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket\Big)\setminus V(Y_{\sigma_{0}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}),

where
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
is endowed with an obvious action of
(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
(
p\in K^{\times}
acting via
\varphi_{q}
which is now bijective) and
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
is endowed with an action of
K^{\times}
(
p
acting via
\varphi
). It turns out that there is a morphism of perfectoid spaces (see the beginning of §
2.4
)

m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\longrightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}

such that
m\circ((a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1}),w)=(\prod_{i}a_{i})\circ m
for
a_{i}\in K^{\times}
and
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
, a crucial fact that we learnt from
[
Far20
]
. Indeed, the sheaf on the perfectoid
v
-site over
\mathbb{F}
represented by
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
sends a perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
to a subset of
(\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p})^{f}
stable under multiplication, where
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
is the (relative version of the) ring defined in
[
FF18
, §1.10]
(a certain completion of
W(R^{\circ})[1/p]
, where
R^{\circ}\subseteq R
is the subring of power-bounded elements). Likewise, the sheaf represented by
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
sends
R
to a subset of
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
stable under multiplication, see §
2.3
. The map
m
then is induced by the product map
(\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p})^{f}\rightarrow\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
in the ring
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
, which satisfies the above relation with respect to the various group actions.

Note that
\Spa(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ})
is an affinoid open subspace of
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
by (
4
). Let
\Delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\{(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in(K^{\times})^{f},\ \prod a_{i}=1\}
and
\Delta_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\Delta\cap(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}
. The third step is to prove that the morphism
m
induces a commutative diagram of perfectoid spaces over
\mathbb{F}
:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.35768pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.02432pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.02432pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.35768pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.285pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{m}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-25.2367pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.35768pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\ m^{-1}(\Spa(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\cong}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.78258pt\raise-16.285pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{m}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.78258pt\raise-24.57pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 162.20747pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\underline{(\Delta/\Delta_{1})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\times\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 114.4232pt\raise-24.57pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-13.35768pt\raise-32.57pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.3577pt\raise-32.57pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.97694pt\raise-32.57pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.97694pt\raise-32.57pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\ \Spa(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,\end{gathered}

where the middle vertical morphism is a pro-étale
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-torsor and where
\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ})
is an explicit affinoid open subspace of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
preserved by the action of
\Delta_{1}
which is itself a pro-étale
\Delta_{1}
-torsor over
\Spa(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ})
, see Proposition
2.4.4
, Corollary
2.4.5
and Lemma
2.4.7
.

Now let
\overline{\rho}
be any finite-dimensional continuous representation of
{\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
, then
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
is the space of global sections of a
K^{\times}
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
on

\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)
. For
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
we define
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
, where
\mathrm{pr}_{i}:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\rightarrow\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)
is the
i
-th projection. Then
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}
is a
(K^{\times})^{f}
-equivariant vector bundle on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
, and thus
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}|_{\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ})}
is a
\Delta_{1}
-equivariant vector bundle on
\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ})
. By the third step and using
[
SW20
, Lemma 17.1.8]
, we deduce that
\Gamma(\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ}),\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)})^{\Delta_{1}}
is an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
of rank
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}
, see Theorem
2.5.1
and §
2.6
. Hence by the first step
\Gamma(\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ}),\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)})^{\Delta_{1}}
is the extension of scalars of a unique étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
over
A
of rank
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}
.

The following theorem sums up the main properties of the functor
\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
.

(i)

There is a functorial
A
-linear isomorphism
\phi_{i}:A\otimes_{\varphi,A}D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A}^{(i+1)}(\overline{\rho})
which commutes with
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
and is such that
\phi_{f-1}\circ\phi_{f-2}\circ\cdots\circ\phi_{0}:A\otimes_{\varphi^{f},A}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
is
\id\otimes\varphi_{q}
, see Corollary
2.6.7
.

(ii)

The functor
\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
from finite-dimensional continuous representations of
{\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
to étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
is exact and fully faithful, see Corollary
2.8.4
.

(iii)

There is
d\in\{0,\cdots,f-1\}
such that the surjection
A\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
induced by the trace
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rrbracket\cong\mathbb{F}\llbracket T\rrbracket
gives a functorial isomorphism of
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-modules

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})\cong D_{\sigma_{d-i}}(\overline{\rho}),

where
D_{\sigma_{d-i}}(\overline{\rho})
is the usual
(
cyclotomic
)

(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
associated to
\overline{\rho}
using
\sigma_{d-i}
to embed
\mathbb{F}_{q}
into
\mathbb{F}
, see Proposition
2.8.1
and Remark
2.8.2
.

(iv)

If
\overline{\rho}
is a direct sum of absolutely irreducible representations then there is an isomorphism of
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A

D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})\cong D_{A,\sigma_{f-i}}(\overline{\rho}),

where
D_{A,\sigma_{f-i}}(\overline{\rho})
is as in (
2
), see Theorem
2.9.4
.

Because of Theorem
1.3
(iv) it is natural to rename
D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
as
D_{A,\sigma_{f-i}}(\overline{\rho})
for any
\overline{\rho}
. Using Theorem
1.3
(i) we can then associate to any
\overline{\rho}
an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
over
A
of rank
(\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho})^{f}
by exactly the same formula as in (
3
). Note that by Theorem
1.3
(iii)
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
can be identified with the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of the tensor induction from
K
to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of
\overline{\rho}
.

We can now state our conjecture:

Conjecture 1.4

(Conjecture
3.1.2
)
.

For any
\pi
as in (
1
)
(
with
F_{v}=K
unramified
)
there is an isomorphism of étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
over
A
.

By Theorem
1.3
(iv) we see that Theorem
1.2
proves special cases of Conjecture
1.4
(but recall that our somewhat technical proof of Theorem
1.2
does not use perfectoids). Note that Conjecture
1.4
implies (the analogue of)
[
BHH+
, Conjecture 1.2.5]
for the representations
\pi
in (
1
). It is also reminiscent of the plectic structure of the local Galois action at
p
on the
\ell
-adic cohomology (
\ell\neq p
) of certain Shimura varieties recently proven in
[
LH
]
, where the above map
m
also plays a key role.

We finish this introduction by going back to Question
1.1
assuming Conjecture
1.4
. The image of the natural map
\pi^{\vee}\rightarrow D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
is a compact
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
-submodule
D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}
which generates
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
over
A
and is preserved by
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
and the operator
\psi
, with moreover
\psi:D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}\twoheadrightarrow D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}
surjective. Assuming there is an admissible smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(K)
naturally associated to
\overline{r}_{v}
, and that this representation is
\pi
(as is the case when
K=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
), one could hope to “guess” what
D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}
is inside
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
, as the latter is pretty explicit, at least when
\overline{r}_{v}
is semi-simple and sufficiently generic. However, even in the simplest case where
K
is quadratic (unramified) and
\overline{r}_{v}
is the direct sum of two characters, where we know that
\pi
is semi-simple (
[
BHH+
]
), it seems impossible to find
D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}
“by hand” (there exists a natural explicit generating compact
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
-submodule in
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
which is preserved by
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
and
\psi
with
\psi
surjective, but we can prove that it cannot be
D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}
). Going back to perfectoids, one could hope to find instead a natural
\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket
-submodule
D_{A_{\infty}}(\pi)^{\natural}
inside
D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))=A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))
and from there go to
D_{A}(\pi)^{\natural}
in a similar way as what was done by Colmez when
K=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
[
Col10b
, §IV.2]
. However, even though there is a natural candidate, namely the
\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket
-submodule

\Gamma\big(Z_{\mathrm{LT}},\mathcal{V}_{\overline{r}_{v}(1)}^{(0)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{r}_{v}(1)}^{(f-1)}\big)^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\\
\subseteq\Gamma\big(m^{-1}(\Spa(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ})),\mathcal{V}_{\overline{r}_{v}(1)}^{(0)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{r}_{v}(1)}^{(f-1)}\big)^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\cong D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1)),

computations for
f=2
show no evidence for this submodule to be large enough (or even nonzero when
\overline{r}_{v}
is irreducible).

We fix some general notation (most of which has already been introduced above, but we remind the reader). We fix
K
a finite unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of residue field
\mathbb{F}_{q}=\mathbb{F}_{p^{f}}
, so
\mathcal{O}_{K}=W(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and
K=\mathcal{O}_{K}[1/p]
. We normalize the local reciprocity map so that it sends
p\in K^{\times}
to (the image of) the geometric Frobenius
x\mapsto x^{-q}
. We fix an algebraic closure
\overline{K}
of
K
with ring of integers
{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{K}}
and maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\overline{K}}
. We denote by
\mathbb{F}
the coefficients, which is a finite extension of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
that we always tacitly assume to be “large enough”. We fix an embedding
\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
(which is sometimes omitted from the notation when the context is clear) and we let
\sigma_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sigma_{0}\circ\varphi^{i}
for
\varphi
the Frobenius on
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(i.e.
\varphi(x)=x^{p}
) and
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

.

2
Étale
(\varphi,\!{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules and Galois representations

In this section we functorially associate to any finite-dimensional continuous representation of
{\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
an étale
(\varphi_{q},\!{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
of rank
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}
over the ring
A
of
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
(depending on an embedding
\sigma:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
) and an étale
(\varphi,\!{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
of rank
(\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho})^{f}
over
A
. We prove various properties of these modules and we make them explicit when
\overline{\rho}
is a direct sum of absolutely irreducible representations.

If
X
is an adic space over
\mathbb{F}
, we denote by
h_{X}
the functor
\Hom_{\Spa(\mathbb{F},\mathbb{F})}(-,X)
from the category of adic spaces over
\mathbb{F}
to the category of sets. If
R
is an adic Huber ring, i.e. a topological ring whose topology is
I
-adic for a finitely generated ideal
I
(see for instance
[
SW20
, §2.2]
), we use the shorthand
\Spa(R)
for the adic spectrum
\Spa(R,R)
. We denote by
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
the category of perfectoid spaces over
\mathbb{F}
. For background on adic spaces or perfectoid spaces we refer (mostly without comment) to
[
Hub96
]
,
[
Sch12
]
or
[
SW20
]
.

Let
A
be a (commutative) ring and let
\varphi
be a ring
endomorphism of
A
. We
define a
\varphi
-module over
A
as a finite free

A
-module
D
endowed with a
\varphi
-semi-linear map

\varphi:D\rightarrow D
. We say that a
\varphi
-module over
A
is

étale
if the
A
-linear map

\id_{A}\otimes\varphi:A\otimes_{\varphi,A}D\rightarrow D
is an
isomorphism. Assume moreover that
A
is a topological ring and that
there exists a continuous action of an abelian topological group

\Gamma
on
A
via endomorphisms commuting with
\varphi
. We
define a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
A
as a
\varphi
-module

D
over
A
endowed with a continuous semi-linear action of

\Gamma
such that, for
a\in A
,
v\in D
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
:

\varphi(\gamma(v))=\gamma(\varphi(v)).

Moreover we say that a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module is

étale
if its underlying
\varphi
-module over
A
is so.

Let
\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
denote the category of
\mathbb{F}
-linear continuous
representations of the topological group
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
on finite-dimensional

\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces.

2.1
Review of Lubin–Tate and classical
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

We review Lubin–Tate and classical
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
associated to an object of
\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
be the
p
-adic completion of

{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{K}}
and let
\mathbb{C}_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}[1/p]
. Set

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\displaystyle\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{x\mapsto x^{p}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}/(p)\cong\displaystyle\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{x\mapsto x^{p}}}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{K}}/(p)\cong\displaystyle\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{x\mapsto x^{q}}}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{K}}/(p)

(which Fontaine used to denote by
R
in
[
Fon82
, §2.1]
). Note that there is an isomorphism of (multiplicative) monoids
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\displaystyle\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{x\mapsto x^{p}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
. This allows us to define a map
v:\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}\cup\{+\infty\}
by
v((x_{m})_{m\geq 1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{val}(x_{1})
, where
x_{m}\in\mathbb{C}_{p}
,
x_{m}^{p}=x_{m-1}
(for

m>1
)
and val is the usual
p
-adic valuation on
\mathbb{C}_{p}
normalized by

\mathrm{val}(p)=1
. Then
v
is a valuation on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
and extends
therefore to a valuation on
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\displaystyle\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{x\mapsto x^{p}}}}\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Then
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}
is an
algebraically closed field of characteristic
p
which is complete
with respect to the valuation
v
. Moreover its ring of integers

\{x\in\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat},\ v(x)\geq 0\}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
and
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}\cong{\rm Frac}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat})
. There is an action of
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, hence of
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
, on
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}
which preserves
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
.

We denote by
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
the unique (up to isomorphism) Lubin–Tate
formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-module over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
associated to the uniformizer

p
. Let
T_{K}
be a formal variable of
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
. The structure of

\mathcal{O}_{K}
-module on
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
is given by power series:

a_{\rm LT}(T_{K})\in aT_{K}+T_{K}^{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{K}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket\ {\rm for}\ a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K},

and recall that
p_{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K})\in T_{K}^{q}+p\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
. Let

T_{p}G_{\mathrm{LT}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}G_{\mathrm{LT}}[p^{m}](\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}})

be the Tate module of
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
, which is a free
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-module
of rank
1
. Let
u
be a generator of the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-module
T_{p}G_{\mathrm{LT}}
.
We can write
u=(u_{m})_{m\geq 1}
with

u_{m}\in G_{\mathrm{LT}}[p^{m}](\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
for
m\geq 1
,
where we embed
G_{\mathrm{LT}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})
into
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
using
T_{K}
. For
m\geq 1

let
\overline{u}_{m}
be the image of
u_{m}
in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}/(p)
and

\overline{u}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(\overline{u}_{m})_{m\geq 1}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
. The map

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
sending
T_{K}
to
\overline{u}

is injective, and we use it to identify
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
with a subring of

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
.

We denote by
K_{\infty}
the abelian extension of
K
generated by all
the elements
u_{m}
and recall that we have the commutative diagram:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 268.11984pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

(5)

where the left vertical injection is the local reciprocity map, the
bottom left horizontal surjection is the projection sending
p
to

1
, and the bottom right horizontal surjection is the norm
map.

We endow the topological ring
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
with a continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear endomorphism
\varphi
and a continuous

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
commuting with
\varphi
and satisfying the following conditions for
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}
,
f\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
, and
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\varphi(\lambda\otimes f)=\lambda\otimes f^{p},\\
a(\lambda\otimes f)=\lambda\otimes(f\circ a_{\rm LT}),\end{array}\right.

(6)

where we still denote by

a_{\rm LT}(T_{K})\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
the reduction mod
p
of

a_{\rm LT}(T_{K})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
. Lubin–Tate Theory implies that
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
is
stable under the action of
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
, and moreover that the action of
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
on

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
factors through
\Gal(K_{\infty}/K)
and coincides with
action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
in (
6
) via the local reciprocity map.

Denote by
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}
the separable closure of

\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
in
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}
. If
\overline{\rho}\in\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
, define

D_{K}(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\big(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\overline{\rho}\big)^{\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})}.

Then
D_{K}(\overline{\rho})
is an étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
and it follows from
Fontaine’s theory of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules (
[
Fon90
]
) that
D_{K}

is a (covariant) rank-preserving
\otimes
-equivalence of categories between
\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
and the category of
étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
. Note that the injectivity of

\id_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)}\otimes\varphi

implies that the endomorphism

\varphi
of an étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
is automatically injective.

The isomorphism

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)&\!\!\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}&\!\!\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)\times\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{1}})\mkern-3.0mu)\times\cdots\times\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{f-1}})\mkern-3.0mu)\\
\lambda\otimes(\sum_{n\gg-\infty}c_{n}T_{K}^{n})&\!\!\longmapsto&\!\!\big(\sum_{n\gg-\infty}\lambda\sigma_{0}(c_{n})T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{n},\dots,\sum_{n\gg-\infty}\lambda\sigma_{f-1}(c_{n})T_{K,\sigma_{f-1}}^{n}\big)\end{array}

(7)

induces an analogous decomposition for any
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
-module
D_{K}
:

D_{K}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}\times\cdots\times D_{K,\sigma_{f-1}}.

If
D_{K}
is an étale
\varphi
-module over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
, then
\varphi
induces a morphism
(still denoted by)
\varphi:D_{K,\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow D_{K,\sigma_{i-1}}

such that

\varphi(\sum_{n\gg-\infty}c_{n}T_{K,\sigma_{i}}^{n}v)=\sum_{n\gg-\infty}c_{n}T_{K,\sigma_{i-1}}^{pn}\varphi(v)

for
c_{n}\in\mathbb{F}
and
v\in D_{K,\sigma_{i}}
. By a standard argument, the
functor
D_{K}\longmapsto D_{K,\sigma_{0}}
induces an equivalence of
categories (compatible with tensor products) between the category of
étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
and the category of étale

(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
, where

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
is endowed with a continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear endomorphism

\varphi_{q}\ (=\varphi^{f})
and a continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
that commutes with
\varphi_{q}
and satisfies the following conditions for
f\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\rrbracket
and
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\varphi_{q}(f(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}))=f(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q}),\\
a(f(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}))=f(a_{\rm LT}(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})).\end{array}\right.

To be precise, in the last formula,

a_{\rm LT}(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sigma_{0}(a_{\rm LT}(T_{K}))\in\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})T_{K,\sigma_{0}}+T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{2}\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\rrbracket,

where
\sigma_{0}(a_{\rm LT}(T_{K}))
is the image of
a_{\rm LT}(T_{K})\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
via

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\rrbracket,\sum_{n\gg-\infty}c_{n}T_{K}^{n}\mapsto\sum_{n\gg-\infty}\sigma_{0}(c_{n})T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{n}.

If one chooses the embedding
\sigma_{i}
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,f-1\}
instead of
\sigma_{0}
, one goes from
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}
to
D_{K,\sigma_{i}}
by the isomorphism

\Id\otimes\varphi^{f-i}:\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{i}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\varphi^{f-i},\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\rrbracket}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{K,\sigma_{i}}.

We can also work with the infinite Galois extension

K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1})
instead of
K_{\infty}
(see (
5
)). Let

T
be a coordinate of the formal group
{\mathbb{G}}_{\rm m}
. We
endow the topological ring
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
with a continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear endomorphism
\varphi
and a continuous

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
commuting with
\varphi
and satisfying the following conditions for
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}
,
f\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T\rrbracket
, and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\varphi(\lambda\otimes f)=\lambda\otimes f^{p},\\
a(\lambda\otimes f)=\lambda\otimes(f\circ a).\end{array}\right.

(8)

The choice of a generator of the Tate module of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and the
choice of
T
induce an embedding
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T\rrbracket\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}

whose image is stable under
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
and on which the action of
{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)

factors through
\Gal(K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1})/K)
with action given by (
8
) (via local class field theory).

If
\overline{\rho}\in\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
, define

D(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\big(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\overline{\rho}\big)^{\Gal(\overline{K}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))}.

The functor
D
is, as before, a
(covariant) rank-preserving
\otimes
-equivalence of categories between the category
\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}({\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K))
and the category
of étale
(\varphi,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times})
-modules over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
.

Here a standard choice is to take
T
such that
a(T)\in\overline{a}T+T^{2}\mathbb{F}_{p}\llbracket T\rrbracket\subseteq\overline{a}T+T^{2}\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T\rrbracket

is the reduction mod
p
of
(1+T)^{a}-1\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T\rrbracket
. Using a
decomposition analogous to (
7
) and choosing the embedding
\sigma_{0}
, we again have an
equivalence (compatible with tensor products)
D\longmapsto D_{\sigma_{0}}
between the category of
étale
(\varphi,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times})
-modules over
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
and the category of étale

(\varphi_{q},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times})
-modules over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
, where

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
is endowed with an
\mathbb{F}
-linear endomorphism
\varphi_{q}\ (=\varphi^{f})
and a continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
that commutes with

\varphi_{q}
and satisfies the following conditions for
f\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket T\rrbracket
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\varphi_{q}(f(T))=f(T^{q}),\\
a(f(T))=f(a(T)).\end{array}\right.

(9)

We will mostly use
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\!{{}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\!(D_{K}(\overline{\rho}))_{\sigma_{0}}}
, an étale
(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
, and
D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}){{}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(D(\overline{\rho}))_{\sigma_{0}}}
, an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
, in the sequel.

We now relate
D_{K}(\overline{\rho})
and
D(\overline{\rho})
,
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
and
D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
. In order to do so, we have to use the perfectoid versions of
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)
,
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
, etc.

We let
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket
be the completion of the perfection
\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-n}}\rrbracket
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket
with respect to the
T_{K}
-adic topology and
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
the fraction field of
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket
. Concretely:

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\cong\left\{\sum_{n\geq 0}c_{n}T_{K}^{\frac{d_{n}}{p^{n}}},\ c_{n}\in\mathbb{F}_{q},\ d_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0},\ \frac{d_{n}}{p^{n}}\rightarrow+\infty\textrm{ in $\mathbb{Q}$ when }n\rightarrow+\infty\right\}

and
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)=\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket[\frac{1}{T_{K}}]
. We define in a similar way
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket
and
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
.

As
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}
is perfect and complete for the
T_{K}
-adic
(resp.
T
-adic) topology, we
have morphisms of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebras

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat},\qquad\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}.

(10)

The following well-known theorem follows from the work of Wintenberger (
[
Win83
]
) and the Ax–Sen–Tate Theorem, see for instance
[
CE14
, Cor. 3.4]
:

Theorem 2.1.1
.

The morphisms (
10
) induce isomorphisms of topological rings compatible with the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}

(
via (
5
)
)
:

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\cong{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}}^{\!\!\!\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})}\textrm{ and \ }\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\cong{\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}}^{\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})}

and isomorphisms of topological rings compatible with the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

(
via (
5
)
)
:

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\cong{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\flat}}^{\!\!\!\Gal(\overline{K}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))}\textrm{ and \ }\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\cong{\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}}^{\Gal(\overline{K}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))}.

In particular,
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\cong\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket^{\Gal(K_{\infty}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket

and
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\cong\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\Gal(K_{\infty}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
.

By Theorem
2.1.1
we have in particular embeddings

\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T\rrbracket\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket
. Applying
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\sigma_{0},\mathbb{F}_{q}}(-)
to Theorem
2.1.1
, we deduce embeddings
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
and
\mathbb{F}\llbracket T\rrbracket\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket
.

Proposition 2.1.2
.

Let
\overline{\rho}\in\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
. There is a canonical
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
-linear isomorphism which commutes with the actions of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
and
\varphi
:

\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K}(\overline{\rho})

(11)

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
,
\varphi
act
diagonally on each side,

{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
acting on
D(\overline{\rho})
via the norm map
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Moreover there is a canonical
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
-linear isomorphism which commutes with the actions of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
and
\varphi_{q}
:

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
,
\varphi_{q}
act diagonally on each side,
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
acting on
D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
via the norm map
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

Proof.

From Wintenberger’s theory of the field of norms
(
[
Win83
]
), recall that we have topological isomorphisms

\Gal(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)\!)\cong\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty}),\ \Gal(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\!)\cong\Gal(\overline{K}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1})).

Since we have for any integer
n\geq 1
:

H^{1}\big(\Gal(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}/\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)\!),\GL_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep})\big)=1

as follows by taking inductive limit from
[
Ser68
, Prop. X.1.3]
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\overline{\rho}

(12)

that is compatible with the actions of
\varphi
and
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
(
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
acting on
D_{K}(\overline{\rho})
via
\Gal(K_{\infty}/K)
), and likewise with
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}
,
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
and
D(\overline{\rho})
. Tensoring (
12
) by
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}
, resp. its analogue over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)^{\rm sep}
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K}(\overline{\rho})\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\overline{\rho}\xleftarrow{\,\sim\,}\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D(\overline{\rho})

compatible with the actions of
\varphi
and
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
. Taking invariants under
\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})
, which acts trivially on
D_{K}(\overline{\rho})
,

D(\overline{\rho})
, and remembering
{\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}}^{\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
from Theorem
2.1.1
we obtain the desired isomorphism (
11
).
The last assertion follows from an analogous discussion (the details of which are left to the reader).
∎

Remark 2.1.3
.

Arguing as in the proofs of Theorem
2.6.4
and Corollary
2.6.6
below, the functor
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}\mapsto\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}
in fact still induces an equivalence of categories from the category of étale
(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
to the category of étale
(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
. Likewise with the functor
D_{\sigma_{0}}\mapsto\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{0}}
and étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-modules.

We finally recall a convenient explicit presentation of
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
for
\overline{\rho}
absolutely irreducible.

For simplicity, we now choose the formal variable
T_{K}
such that
a_{\rm LT}(T_{K})=\overline{a}T_{K}
when
a\in[\mathbb{F}_{q}]
(so
a(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})=\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})T_{K,\sigma_{0}}
for
a\in[\mathbb{F}_{q}]
); for instance, this holds if
T_{K}
is such that the logarithm of the Lubin–Tate group
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
(
[
Lan90
, ch.8 §6]
) is the series
\sum_{n\geq 0}p^{-n}T_{K}^{q^{n}}
. Note that in that case
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}=\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)
and that the commutativity of the action of
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
with
[\mathbb{F}_{q}]
implies:

a(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\in\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})T_{K,\sigma_{0}}+T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q}{\mathcal{O}}_{K}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1}\rrbracket.

(13)

We recall the following straightforward lemma.

Lemma 2.1.4
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be a finite-dimensional continuous representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
. Denote by
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
the
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)
-vector subspace of
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
fixed by
[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
. Then
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
is preserved by
\varphi_{q}
and the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
, and we have an
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
-linear isomorphism compatible with
\varphi_{q}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
:

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})

where the actions of
\varphi_{q}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
on the left-hand side are the diagonal ones.

Proof.

It is enough to prove that the morphism in the statement is an isomorphism, everything else being trivial. It is enough to prove

H^{1}\big([\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}],\GL_{n}(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu))\big)=1

for any integer
n\geq 1
. But this is again the generalization of Hilbert
90
applied to the Galois extension
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)/\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)
(which has Galois group
[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]
), see for instance
[
Ser68
, Prop. X.1.3]
.
∎

We now give explicitly
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
for an absolutely irreducible
\overline{\rho}
.

For
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
denote by
{\rm unr}(\lambda)
the unramified character of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
sending the Frobenius
x\mapsto x^{q}
to
\lambda^{-1}
. For
f^{\prime}\geq 1
denote by
\omega_{f^{\prime}}:I_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}_{p^{f^{\prime}}}^{\times}
Serre’s fundamental character of level
f^{\prime}
, where
I_{K}\subseteq\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
is the inertia subgroup. We also denote by
\omega_{f}
(instead of
\sigma_{0}\circ\omega_{f}
) the composition

I_{K}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\omega_{f}}}\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hookrightarrow}\limits^{\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{F}^{\times}

(14)

and again
\omega_{f}
its unique extension to
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
such that
\omega_{f}(p)=1
(via local class field theory). Recall that
\omega_{f}:\Gal(\overline{K}/K)\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
is the composition by
\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
of the mod
p
Lubin–Tate character of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
. For
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, it goes back to Serre that any absolutely irreducible
d
-dimensional representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
is isomorphic to
(\ind\omega_{df}^{h})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda)
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
and some positive integer
h
which is not of the form
m\frac{q^{d}-1}{q^{d^{\prime}}-1}
for some
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
and some
d^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,d-1\}
, where
\ind\omega_{df}^{h}
is the induction from
\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{d})
to
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
of the mod
p
Lubin–Tate character of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{d})
(seen with values in
\mathbb{F}
via any embedding
\mathbb{F}_{q^{d}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
lifting
\sigma_{0}
), where
K_{d}
is the unramified extension of
K
of degree
d
. Equivalently
\ind\omega_{df}^{h}
is the unique representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
with determinant
\omega_{f}^{h}\cdot{\rm unr}(-1)^{d-1}
such that
(\ind\omega_{df}^{h})|_{I_{K}}\cong\omega_{df}^{h}\oplus\omega_{df}^{qh}\oplus\cdots\oplus\omega_{df}^{q^{d-1}h}
(for any choice of embedding
\mathbb{F}_{q^{d}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
). Note that

(\ind\omega_{df}^{h})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda)\cong(\ind\omega_{df}^{h^{\prime}})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda^{\prime})

if and only if
h^{\prime}\equiv q^{i}h\!\!\mod q^{d}-1
for some
i\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
and
\lambda^{d}=\lambda^{\prime d}
.

For
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
, we set:

f_{a}^{\rm LT}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}f_{a}^{\rm LT}(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\frac{\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})T_{K,\sigma_{0}}}{a(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})}\in 1+T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\rrbracket.

Note that
f_{a}^{\rm LT}=1
if
a\in[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]
and that (
13
) implies

f_{a}^{\rm LT}\in 1+T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1}\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1}\rrbracket.

Lemma 2.1.5
.

Let
\overline{\rho}\in\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
and write
\overline{\rho}=(\ind\omega_{df}^{h})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda)
for some
d,h,\lambda
as above. Then
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\cong\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}

(
Lemma
2.1.4
)
, where
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
is explicitly described as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cll}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}&=&\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d-1}\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)e_{i}\\
\varphi_{q}(e_{i})&=&e_{i+1},\ i<d-1\\
\varphi_{q}(e_{d-1})&=&\displaystyle{\frac{\lambda^{d}}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{h(q-1)}}e_{0}}\\
a(e_{i})&=&{{\left(f_{a}^{\rm LT}\right)}^{\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}e_{i},\ a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}.}\\
\end{array}\right.

(15)

Moreover a basis
(e_{0},\dots,e_{d-1})=(e_{0},\varphi_{q}(e_{0}),\dots,\varphi_{q}^{d-1}(e_{0}))
as in (
15
) is uniquely determined up to a scalar in
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
. Finally, if
h^{\prime}=q^{j}h+m(q^{d}-1)
for some
j\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
and some
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, then the unique basis
(e^{\prime}_{i})_{i}=(e^{\prime}_{0},\varphi_{q}(e^{\prime}_{0}),\dots,\varphi_{q}^{d-1}(e^{\prime}_{0}))
in (
15
) corresponding to
h^{\prime}
is given by
e^{\prime}_{0}=\frac{1}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{m(q-1)}}e_{j}

(
up to a scalar in
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
)
.

Proof.

The first statement is
[
PS
, Cor. 10.10]
. We prove the uniqueness of the basis
(e_{i})
in (
15
) (up to scalar). Let
(f_{0},\dots,f_{d-1})
be another basis of
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
satisfying (
15
), it is enough to prove that
f_{0}\in\mathbb{F}e_{0}
. Write
f_{0}=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}x_{i}e_{i}
for some
x_{i}\in\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)
. Since
\varphi_{q}^{d}(f_{0})=\frac{\lambda^{d}}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{h(q-1)}}f_{0}
and
\varphi_{q}^{d}(e_{i})=\frac{\lambda^{d}}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q^{i}h(q-1)}}e_{i}
, we deduce that
\frac{1}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{h(q-1)}}x_{i}=\frac{1}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q^{i}h(q-1)}}\varphi_{q}^{d}(x_{i})
for
i\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
, i.e.
\varphi_{q}^{d}(x_{i})=T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{h(q-1)(q^{i}-1)}x_{i}
. This easily implies
x_{i}\in\mathbb{F}T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{m_{i}}
, where
m_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\frac{h(q-1)(q^{i}-1)}{q^{d}-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. If
x_{i}\neq 0
, since
(q-1)|m_{i}
in
{\mathbb{Z}}
, we obtain
h=\frac{m_{i}}{q-1}\frac{q^{d}-1}{q^{i}-1}
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,d-1\}
which contradicts the assumption on
h
. Hence
x_{i}=0
for all
i\neq 0
and thus
f_{0}\in\mathbb{F}e_{0}
. The last statement is an easy check that is left to the reader.
∎

Remark 2.1.6
.

One can prove that the action of
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
in (
15
) is the unique semi-linear action on
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
which commutes with
\varphi_{q}
and is such that
a(e_{i})\in e_{i}+T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1}\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1}\rrbracket e_{j}
for all
i
. The argument is the same as in the proof of Lemma
2.2.2
below.

As a special case of Lemma
2.1.5
we have:

Lemma 2.1.7
.

Let
\chi:\Gal(\overline{K}/K)\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
be a continuous character and write
\chi=\omega_{f}^{h_{\chi}}{\rm unr}(\lambda_{\chi})
for
h_{\chi}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
\lambda_{\chi}\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
, then
(
for
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
)
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cll}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\chi)^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}&=&\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)e_{\chi}\\
\varphi_{q}(e_{\chi})&=&\displaystyle{\frac{\lambda_{\chi}}{T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{h_{\chi}(q-1)}}e_{\chi}}\\
a(e_{\chi})&=&({f_{a}^{\rm LT}})^{h_{\chi}}e_{\chi}\end{array}\right.

If
\overline{\rho}
is any finite-dimensional continuous representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
, write
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})(\chi)
for
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\chi)
with tensor product structures. Then we have
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}\otimes\chi)\cong D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})(\chi)
as follows from the compatibility of
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(-)
with tensor products.

2.2
The
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
of a semi-simple Galois representation

To an arbitrary semi-simple
\overline{\rho}
we associate by an elementary recipe an étale
(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
over
A
(depending on the fixed choice of the embedding
\sigma_{0}
).

Let
N_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&{\mathcal{O}}_{K}\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\subseteq\GL_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
and
\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}
the maximal ideal of
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
. Recall that
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket Y_{0},\dots,Y_{{f-1}}\rrbracket
and
\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}=(Y_{0},\dots,Y_{{f-1}})
, where

Y_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\lambda^{-p^{i}}\begin{pmatrix}1&[\lambda]\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket.

(Namely, as
N_{0}
is a uniform pro-
p
-group isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{f}
, it
follows from
[
DdSMS99
, Thm. 7.23(i)]
that
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
is
isomorphic to a power series ring in
f
variables over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. This is a local ring. We easily check that the images of
Y_{0},\dots,Y_{f-1}

in
\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}/\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}^{2}
form a basis of this
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector
space, so that
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket Y_{0},\dots,Y_{f-1}\rrbracket
.)

As in
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
consider the multiplicative system

S\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\{(Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1})^{k},\ k\geq 0\}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket

and
A_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\widehat{\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{S}}
the completion of the localization
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{S}
with respect to the ascending filtration (
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
):

F_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{1}{(Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1})^{k}}\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}^{kf-n}=\bigcup_{k\geq 0}\frac{1}{(Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1})^{k}}\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}^{kf-n}

(16)

where
\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
if
m\leq 0
(see
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
). We denote by
F_{n}A_{q}
(
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
) the induced ascending filtration on
A_{q}
and endow
A_{q}
with the associated topology (
[
LvO96
, §I.3]
). The ring
A_{q}
contains
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
and the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-linear action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
(induced by the multiplication on
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}\cong N_{0}
) canonically extends by continuity to
A_{q}
(but not to
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{S}
as it does not preserve
S
). We will write this action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
and
A_{q}
as
a(x)
for
(a,x)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}\times A_{q}
. In fact using
a-[\overline{a}]\in p{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
one has for
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
and
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
:

a(Y_{i})\in\overline{a}^{p^{i}}Y_{i}+\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}^{p}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}

which implies

a(Y_{i})\in\overline{a}^{p^{i}}Y_{i}\big(1+\frac{1}{Y_{i}}\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}^{p}\big)\subseteq\overline{a}^{p^{i}}Y_{i}(1+F_{1-p}A_{q})\subseteq A_{q}^{\times}.

(17)

We define
\varphi
as the Frobenius endomorphism of
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
,
i.e. by
\varphi(f)=f^{p}
for
f\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
. It canonically extends by continuity to
A_{q}
and obviously commutes with the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
, hence on
A_{q}
.

Let
A
be the complete filtered ring in
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
. Recall that
A
is defined similarly to
A_{q}
replacing
\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
by
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket

except
that the Frobenius
\varphi
on
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
is now
\mathbb{F}
-linear. As in (
7
), we have an isomorphism
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A_{q}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\underbrace{A\times A\times\cdots\times A}_{f\textrm{ times}}
which sends
\lambda\otimes\sum_{\underline{n}}c_{\underline{n}}Y_{0}^{n_{0}}\cdots Y_{f-1}^{n_{f-1}}\in\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A_{q}
to:

\big(\lambda\sum_{\underline{n}}\sigma_{0}(c_{\underline{n}})Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{n_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{n_{f-1}},\lambda\sum_{\underline{n}}\sigma_{1}(c_{\underline{n}})Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{n_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{n_{f-1}},\dots,\lambda\sum_{\underline{n}}\sigma_{f-1}(c_{\underline{n}})Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{n_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-2}}^{n_{f-1}}\big)

where we set for
\sigma:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
:

Y_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\sigma(\lambda)^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}1&[\lambda]\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket\subseteq A.

(18)

It induces an analogous decomposition for any
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A_{q}
-module
D_{A_{q}}
:

D_{A_{q}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A,\sigma_{0}}\times\cdots\times D_{A,\sigma_{f-1}}.

We extend
\mathbb{F}
-linearly the Frobenius
\varphi
and the action of

{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
from
A_{q}
to
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A_{q}
. Note that we have

\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{i}})=Y_{\sigma_{i-1}}^{p}
for
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
(see
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
, where
\varphi
on
A
is denoted by
\phi
). We let

\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi^{f}
on
A
. As in §
2.1
, the functor

D_{A_{q}}\mapsto D_{A,\sigma_{0}}
induces an equivalence of categories
between the category of étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A_{q}
and the category of étale

(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
.

The embedding of
\mathbb{F}
-algebras

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow A,\ \ \ \sum_{n\gg-\infty}c_{n}T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{n(q-1)}\longmapsto\sum_{n\gg-\infty}c_{n}\Big(\frac{\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}\Big)^{n}

(19)

trivially commutes with
\varphi_{q}
and
[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]
(the latter acting trivially on both sides). When
\overline{\rho}
is a direct sum of absolutely irreducible finite-dimensional continuous representations of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
, we define:

D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}A\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}

(20)

where
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
is as in Lemma

2.1.4
. It follows from its definition that

D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
is an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over

A
if it is endowed with the endomorphism

\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi_{q}\otimes\varphi_{q}
.

Remark 2.2.1
.

Definition (
20
) does not need the semi-simplicity of

\overline{\rho}
, but we will only use it in that case, see also Remark

2.9.5
below.

For
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
, we set (see (
17
)):

f_{a,\sigma_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}f_{a,\sigma_{0}}(Y_{\sigma_{0}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\frac{\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{a(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}\in 1+F_{1-p}A.

(21)

Lemma 2.2.2
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be an absolutely irreducible continuous representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
and
(e_{0},\dots,e_{d-1})
a basis of
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
as in Lemma
2.1.5
. Then we have:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cll}D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})&=&\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d-1}A(1\otimes e_{i})\\
\varphi_{q}(1\otimes e_{i})&=&1\otimes e_{i+1},\ i<d-1\\
\varphi_{q}(1\otimes e_{d-1})&=&\displaystyle{\lambda^{d}\Big(\frac{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{h}(1\otimes e_{0}).}\end{array}\right.

Moreover there is a unique structure of

(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
on
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
such that

a(1\otimes e_{i})\in 1\otimes e_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}(F_{1-p}A)(1\otimes e_{j})\textrm{ for all $i$ and $a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}$.}

This action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
is explicitly given by
(
i\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
,
