a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
)
:

a(1\otimes e_{i})=\displaystyle{\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{\frac{hq^{i}}{1-q^{d}}}(1\otimes e_{i})\in(1+F_{q^{i}(1-p)}A)(1\otimes e_{i})}

(22)

and does not depend
(
up to isomorphism
)
on the choice of the basis
(e_{i})_{i}
of
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
.

Proof.

The first part of the statement follows from the definition of
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
in (
20
). Fix
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
and write
a(1\otimes e_{0})=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}C_{i}(1\otimes e_{i})
for some
C_{0}\in 1+F_{1-p}A
and
C_{i}\in F_{1-p}A
if
i\neq 0
. Assume
C_{i}\neq 0
for some
i\neq 0
and let
m_{i}\geq p-1
be the maximal integer such that
C_{i}\in F_{-m_{i}}A\setminus F_{-(m_{i}+1)}A
. Since
a(1\otimes e_{0})
and the
1\otimes e_{j}
are fixed by
[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]
, the constants
C_{j}
are also fixed by
[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]
in
A
for all
j
, and thus in particular by
[\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}]
, from which it is an exercise to deduce that we must have
(p-1)|m_{i}
. Since
\varphi_{q}^{d}(1\otimes e_{j})=\lambda^{d}\Big(\frac{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{q^{j}h}\!(1\otimes e_{j})
for all
j
, the equality
a(\varphi_{q}^{d}(e_{0}))=\varphi_{q}^{d}(a(e_{0}))
yields for
j\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
(using
\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})^{q-1}=1
):

C_{j}=\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{h}\Big(\frac{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{(q^{j}-1)h}\varphi_{q}^{d}(C_{j})

(23)

which implies in particular
-m_{i}=(q^{i}-1)h(p-1)-q^{d}m_{i}
, i.e.
(q^{i}-1)h(p-1)=(q^{d}-1)m_{i}
, i.e.
h=\frac{q^{d}-1}{q^{i}-1}\frac{m_{i}}{p-1}
, which contradicts the
assumption on
h
since
\frac{m_{i}}{p-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Hence we must have

C_{i}=0
if
i\neq 0
. When
i=0
, (
23
) is just

C_{0}=\big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\big)^{h}\varphi_{q}^{d}(C_{0})
. The
equation
C_{0}=\big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\big)^{h}\varphi_{q}^{d}(C_{0})
has a solution in
1+F_{1-p}A
given by

C_{0}=\prod_{n=0}^{+\infty}\varphi_{q}^{nd}\Big(\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{h}\Big)=\prod_{n=0}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{q^{nd}h}=\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{h(1+q^{d}+q^{2d}+\cdots)}\\
=\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{\frac{h}{1-q^{d}}},

where the second equality uses
x^{q^{nd}}=x
if
x\in\mathbb{F}_{q}
. This
solution is unique in
1+F_{1-p}A(\subseteq A^{\times})
: the quotient of two
solutions is an element of
1+F_{1-p}A
fixed by
\varphi_{q}^{d}
and the
map
1-\varphi_{q}^{d}
induces an automorphism of
F_{1-p}A
(with inverse

\sum_{n\geq 0}\varphi_{q}^{dn}
) so that only
1
is fixed by

\varphi_{q}^{d}
in
1+F_{1-p}A
. Then (
22
) immediately
follows, from which the continuity of the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
is
clear (as it is continuous on
A
). If one changes the basis

(e_{i})_{i}
, or equivalently by (the last statement in) Lemma
2.1.5

changes the integer
h
, the last statement easily follows from the
last statement of Lemma
2.1.5
.
∎

Remark 2.2.3
.

The uniqueness of the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
in the proof of Lemma
2.2.2
works just assuming
a(1\otimes e_{i})\in 1\otimes e_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}(F_{-1}A)(1\otimes e_{j})
(and lands automatically in
1\otimes e_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}(F_{1-p}A)(1\otimes e_{j})
).

Let us finally make twists explicit. Let
\chi:\Gal(\overline{K}/K)\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
be a continuous character and write
\chi=\omega_{f}^{h_{\chi}}{\rm unr}(\lambda_{\chi})
for
h_{\chi}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
\lambda_{\chi}\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
, then (using Lemma
2.1.7
) the étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\chi)
is explicitly given by (
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
):

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cll}D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\chi)&=&A(1\otimes e_{\chi})\\
\varphi_{q}(1\otimes e_{\chi})&=&\displaystyle{\lambda_{\chi}\Big(\frac{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{h_{\chi}}(1\otimes e_{\chi})}\\
a(1\otimes e_{\chi})&=&\displaystyle{\Big(\frac{f_{a,\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{\frac{h_{\chi}}{1-q}}(1\otimes e_{\chi}).}\end{array}\right.

(24)

One has an action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
on
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}\otimes\chi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\otimes_{A}D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\chi)
by taking the tensor product action. We leave to the reader the exercise to check that, when
\overline{\rho}\otimes\chi\cong\overline{\rho}^{\prime}\otimes\chi^{\prime}
, then
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}\otimes\chi)\cong D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime}\otimes\chi^{\prime})
as
(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
.

2.3
A reminder on
p
-divisible groups and
K
-vector spaces

We review some results on constructions of Fargues and Fontaine (
[
FF18
]
) related to
p
-divisible groups (in a relative context, see for example
[
LB18
, §5.1]
) and we define the important perfectoid spaces
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
and
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
over
\mathbb{F}
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid

\mathbb{F}
-algebra and
\varpi
a
pseudo-uniformizer of
R
. As usual we denote by
R^{\circ}
the subring of power-bounded elements in
R
and by
R^{\circ\circ}\subseteq R^{\circ}
the subset of topologically nilpotent elements (i.e. those
a\in R
such that
{a}^{n}
converges to
0
in
R
). We fix a power-multiplicative norm

\lvert\cdot\rvert
on
R
defining the topology of
R
. Such a norm exists and can
be explicitly given by

\lvert a\rvert=\inf\{2^{\frac{m}{n}},(m,n)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0},\,\varpi^{m}a^{n}\in R^{\circ}\}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

(25)

(so
\lvert a\rvert\leq 1\Leftrightarrow a\in R^{\circ}
). We endow the Witt vectors
W(R^{\circ})
with the

(p,[\varpi])
-adic topology (where
[\cdot]
is the multiplicative representative). Let
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
be the Fréchet
K
-algebra defined as the completion
of
W(R^{\circ})[1/p]
for the family of norms
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{\rho}
,

0<\rho<1
given by

\left\lvert\sum_{n>\!>-\infty}[x_{n}]p^{n}\right\rvert_{\rho}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lvert x_{n}\rvert\rho^{n}).

(26)

It is endowed with a continuous
K
-semi-linear endomorphism defined by

\varphi\left(\sum_{n}[x_{n}]p^{n}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{n}[x_{n}^{p}]p^{n}

and we define
\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi^{f}
which is
K
-linear.

Let
\mathbb{F}\llbracket x_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{d-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket
be the
completion of the perfection of
\mathbb{F}[x_{0},\dots,x_{d-1}]
for the

(x_{0},\dots,x_{d-1})
-topology. If
R
is a perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
and
(r_{0},\dots,r_{d-1})\in(R^{\circ\circ})^{d}
, let

F(r_{0},\dots,r_{d-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}[r_{i}^{p^{-i-nf}}]p^{i+nd}\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}

then we have:

Lemma 2.3.1
.

Let
1\leq d\leq f
. For each perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
, the
following functorial map is a bijection:

\begin{array}[]{rcc}\Hom_{\mathbb{F}-\alg}^{\cont}(\mathbb{F}\llbracket x_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{d-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket,R)\cong(R^{\circ\circ})^{d}&\longrightarrow&\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}\\
(r_{0},\dots,r_{d-1})&\longmapsto&F(r_{0},\dots,r_{d-1}).\end{array}

Proof.

This follows from
[
FS
, Prop. II.2.5(iv)]
. See also

[
FF18
, Prop. 4.2.1]
for the case where
R
is an algebraically closed
perfectoid field.
∎

Remark 2.3.2
.

If
R
is a Huber ring over
\mathbb{F}
and
R^{+}\subseteq R^{\circ}
is an open and integrally closed subring (
(R,R^{+})
is then called a
Huber pair
), we have

R^{\circ\circ}\subseteq R^{+}
so that, by
[
Hub94
, Prop. 2.1(i)]

\Hom_{\Spa(\mathbb{F})}\big(\Spa(R,R^{+}),\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket x_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{d-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)\big)\\
\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{F}-\alg}^{\cont}(\mathbb{F}\llbracket x_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{d-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket,R).

Thus Lemma
2.3.1
and
[
SW20
, Lemma 18.1.1]
imply
that the functor
(R,R^{+})\!\mapsto\!\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}
can be
extended to a sheaf on the site
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
of perfectoid spaces
over
\mathbb{F}
endowed with either the pro-étale topology or the

v
-topology.

Remark 2.3.3
.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a perfectoid Huber pair over
\mathbb{F}
. If

x\in\Spa(R,R^{+})
, then its residue field
k(x)
is a perfectoid
field containing
\mathbb{F}
(see for example

[
Sch12
, Cor. 6.7(ii)]
). If

z\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}
, we let
z_{x}
be its image in

\mathbf{B}^{+}(k(x))^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}
. Then the functorial bijection of Lemma

2.3.1
induces a functorial bijection:

\big(\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket x_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{d-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)\setminus V(x_{0},\dots,x_{d-1})\big)(R,R^{+})\!\cong\!\{z\in B^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}\!,\ z_{x}\neq 0\ \forall\ x\}.

The following remark will be used in §
2.9
.

Remark 2.3.4
.

There exists a norm
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{1}
on
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
which induces
on
W(R^{\circ})[1/p]
the norm

\left\lvert\sum_{n>\!>-\infty}[x_{n}]p^{n}\right\rvert_{1}=\sup\{\lvert x_{n}\rvert,\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}\in[0,1]\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

and is such that

\lvert x\rvert_{1}=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho<1\\
\rho\rightarrow 1\end{subarray}}\lvert x\rvert_{\rho}
(see

[
FF18
, Prop. 1.10.5 & Prop. 1.6.16]
). Equivalently, there exists a valuation

v_{0}:\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)\rightarrow[0,+\infty]
such that

\forall\ x=\sum_{n>\!>-\infty}[x_{n}]p^{n}\in W(R^{\circ})[1/p],\quad v_{0}(x)=\inf\{v(x_{n}),\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\},

where
v
is the valuation

-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert
on
R
. This description implies that if
(x_{-n})_{n\geq 0}
is a sequence
of elements of
R^{\circ\circ}
such that
\sum_{n\leq 0}[x_{n}]p^{n}\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
and such that there exists
0\leq c<1
with

\lvert x_{-n}\rvert\leq c
for all
n\geq 0
, then

\left\lvert\sum_{n\leq 0}[x_{n}]p^{n}\right\rvert_{1}\leq c.

Note that
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{1}:\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)\rightarrow[0,1]
is not continuous since, for instance,
\lvert p^{n}\rvert_{1}=1

for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
although
p^{n}\rightarrow 0
in
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
when
n\rightarrow+\infty

(in fact
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{1}
induces the discrete topology on
K\subseteq\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
).

Now we review the interpretation of
\mathbf{B}^{+}(-)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{d}}
in
terms of
p
-divisible groups in the two extreme cases
d=1
and
d=f
.

The case
d=1

Let
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
be the Lubin–Tate formal group of §
2.1
. As at the end of
loc. cit.
we choose an isomorphism

G_{\mathrm{LT}}\cong\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket T_{K}\rrbracket)
such that the logarithm map

\log_{G_{\mathrm{LT}}}:G_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a,K}^{\mathrm{rig}}
(where
\mathbb{G}_{a,K}
is the additive formal group over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and “rig” the rigid analytic generic fiber) is given by the series
\sum_{n\geq 0}p^{-n}T_{K}^{q^{n}}
. Let
\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{p}(G_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})}\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q}))\cong\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q}\llbracket T_{K}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)
be the universal
cover of
G_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})}\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q})
(see for instance
[
SW13
, §3.1]
). The action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
on
G_{\mathrm{LT}}
extends to an
action of
K
on
\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}
. Note that if
R
is a perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra, we have
\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}(R)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}(G_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})}\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q}))(R)\cong G_{\mathrm{LT}}(R)
(see for example
[
SW13
, Prop. 3.1.3(iii)]
) so that
G_{\mathrm{LT}}(R)
already has a
structure of a
K
-vector space. We also have
\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}(R^{\circ})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}(R)
. By
[
FF18
, Prop. 4.4.5]
or
[
FS
, Prop. II.2.2]
,
for each perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
, we have an isomorphism of

K
-vector spaces

\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}(R^{\circ})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p}
given by

r\in R^{\circ\circ}\cong\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}(R^{\circ})\longmapsto F(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[r^{q^{-n}}]p^{n}\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p}

(27)

(this is the isomorphism of Lemma
2.3.1
when
d=1
, where the variable
x_{0}
in
loc. cit.
is denoted by
T_{K}
). Note that on the left-hand side, the
K
-linear structure is given by (for
r\in R^{\circ\circ}
)

\begin{cases}\forall\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}},&p^{n}(r)=r^{q^{n}},\\
\forall\ a\in\mathcal{O}_{K},&a(r)=a_{\mathrm{LT}}(r),\end{cases}

(28)

where we view the coefficients of the power series
a_{\mathrm{LT}}
in
\mathbb{F}

via
\mathcal{O}_{K}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hookrightarrow}\limits^{\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{F}
. We let

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}((\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\Spf(\mathbb{F}))^{\ad}\setminus\{0\})^{f},

where
(\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\Spf(\mathbb{F}))^{\ad}
is the adic space associated to the
formal scheme

\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\Spf(\mathbb{F})
and
\{0\}
is
the closed analytic subspace image of the
0
-section, i.e.
f
-times the fiber product of
(\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\Spf(\mathbb{F}))^{\ad}\setminus\{0\}
over
\Spa(\mathbb{F})
(still using
\sigma_{0}
). Using obvious notation, we have an
isomorphism of adic spaces

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\cong\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,T_{K,f-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)\setminus V(T_{K,0}\cdots T_{K,f-1})\cong\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,i}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,i}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)

and there is an action of
(K^{\times})^{f}
on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
given by

\forall\ \underline{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in(K^{\times})^{f},\quad\underline{a}(T_{K,i})=a_{i,\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,i}).

The case
d=f

Let
\mathcal{G}_{f,f}
be the
p
-divisible group over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
defined
in
[
FF18
, §4.3.2]
(with
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
) as the kernel of
V^{f}-1
on the group scheme
of Witt covectors
CW
(we use without comment the notation of
loc. cit.
, for instance
V
is the Verschiebung,
F
is the Frobenius, see
[
FF18
, §1.10.2]
for
CW
, etc.). The base change of
\mathcal{G}_{f,f}
to
\mathbb{F}
is endowed with an additional structure of functor in

\mathcal{O}_{K}
-modules. Namely if
R
is an
\mathbb{F}
-algebra, then
CW(R)
is an

\mathcal{O}_{K}=W(\mathbb{F}_{q})
-module via
\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
and the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
on
CW(R)
commutes
with
V^{f}
and
F^{f}
(but not with
V
and
F
).

As
\ker(V-1)\subseteq\ker(V^{f}-1)
, there is a natural injection of

p
-divisible groups

\mathcal{G}_{1,1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{f,f}
which induces a
morphism of
p
-divisible groups over
\mathbb{F}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-action

\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{G}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}.

(29)

Lemma 2.3.5
.

The map (
29
) is an isomorphism of
p
-divisible
groups over
\mathbb{F}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-action.

Proof.

In this proof we will use (contravariant) Dieudonné Theory
\mathbb{D}(-)
for
p
-divisible groups over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Recall that it yields free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules, and that when the
p
-divisible group is over
\mathbb{F}
it yields free
W(\mathbb{F})
-modules. The map (
29
) corresponds to a nonzero map of Dieudonné modules which is both
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-linear and
W(\mathbb{F})
-linear:

\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{G}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}})\cong\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}))\\
\cong\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},W(\mathbb{F}))\otimes_{W(\mathbb{F})}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}})

(30)

where
\mathcal{O}_{K}
acts on the right-hand side via its natural action on
\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},W(\mathbb{F}))
. Note that
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}})=W(\mathbb{F})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f})
, where the Dieudonné module
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f})
has a

{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-basis
(e_{0},e_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}V(e_{0}),\dots,e_{f-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}V^{f-1}(e_{0}))
such that
F(e_{i})=pe_{i-1}

for all
0\leq i\leq f-1
(see
[
FF18
, §4.3.2]
, we write
e_{0}:\mathcal{G}_{f,f}\hookrightarrow CW
for the canonical embedding
e
of
loc. cit.
and we use the convention that

i=i+f
). Moreover the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
on
\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}

induces an action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
on
\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}})=W(\mathbb{F})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f})

such that
a(1\otimes e_{i})=\varphi^{-i}(a)\otimes e_{i}
for
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
, where
\varphi
is the absolute Frobenius on
W(\mathbb{F})
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is seen in
W(\mathbb{F})
via
\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
. Using the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
- and
W(\mathbb{F})
-linearities, and the commutativity with
F
, one checks that there is an isomorphism
W(\mathbb{F})\cong\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}})
such that the map (
30
) is given by

\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}\lambda_{i}\otimes e_{i}\longmapsto\left(a\longmapsto\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}\lambda_{i}\varphi^{-i}(a)\right)\in\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},W(\mathbb{F}))

(in particular,
e_{0}
maps to the inclusion
\mathcal{O}_{K}\hookrightarrow W(\mathbb{F})
). To conclude the proof we need to show that the elements
a\mapsto\varphi^{-i}(a)
,
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
, generate the
W(\mathbb{F})
-module
\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},W(\mathbb{F}))
. This can be
checked after reduction mod
p
and we have to prove that the
elements
a\mapsto a^{p^{i}}
,
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
, generate the
\mathbb{F}
-vector space

\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{p}-{\rm vs}}(\mathbb{F}_{q},\mathbb{F})
, which is a consequence of the linear
independence of characters.
∎

By
[
FF18
, Prop 4.4.5]
(replacing
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
by
\mathbb{F}
, the field
F
by a perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
and where the variable
x_{i}
of
loc. cit.
is reindexed
x_{f-i}
here for
i\in\{1,\dots,f-1\}
,
x_{0}
being unchanged) there exists a coordinate
z
(resp. coordinates
x_{0},\dots,x_{f-1}
) on
the formal group
\mathcal{G}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}
) such that the following map is an isomorphism
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces (resp.
K
-vector spaces) for any perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
:

\begin{gathered}\gamma_{1}:\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}(R)&\xrightarrow{\sim}&\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi=p}\\
z&\xrightarrow{\sim}&\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[z^{p^{-n}}]p^{n}\end{array}\\
\left(\textrm{resp. }\gamma_{f}:\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}(R)&\xrightarrow{\sim}&\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}\\
(x_{0},\dots,x_{f-1})&\xrightarrow{\sim}&\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[x_{i}^{p^{-i-nf}}]p^{i+nf}\end{array}\right)\end{gathered}

(31)

(we use
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}(R)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}(R)
by
[
SW13
, Prop. 3.1.3(iii)]
for the structure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space on
{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}(R)
, likewise with
{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}(R)
).
Moreover these isomorphisms are given by the composition of the isomorphisms in
the following diagram (we only give
\gamma_{f}
and refer to
loc. cit.
for the notation):

\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}(R)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{W(\mathbb{F})[F]}(\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}),CW(R))\xleftarrow{\sim}\Hom_{W(\mathbb{F})[F]}(\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}),BW(R))\\
\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{W(\mathbb{F})[F]}(\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{G}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}),\mathbf{B}^{+}(R))=\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}},

(32)

where the second isomorphism is a consequence of

[
FF18
, Prop. 4.4.2]
and the third a
consequence of
[
FF18
, Prop. 4.2.1]
. We deduce from (
32
) the commutativity of the following
diagram of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi=p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{f}}

and thus the commutativity of the following diagram of
K
-vector spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi=p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Id_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\!\otimes\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{f}}

(33)

Let
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
be the multiplicative formal group over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\mathbb{F}}
its base change over
\mathbb{F}
, we have
\mathcal{G}_{1,1}\cong\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
(see

[
FF18
, Ex. 4.4.7]
) and isomorphisms of
p
-divisible groups over
\mathbb{F}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-action

\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\mathbb{F}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1,1,\mathbb{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}

(34)

using the isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-modules

\mathcal{O}_{K}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),\ a\mapsto\Tr_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(a\cdot)

(35)

and Lemma
2.3.5
. Here, the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-action on the left-hand side of (
34
) is via the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
on
\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
given by
a(\lambda)\!=\!\lambda(a-)
(
a\!\in\!\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
\lambda\!\in\!\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
). Using

\Hom_{\mathbb{F}-\alg}^{\cont}(\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket,A)\cong\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},A^{\circ\circ})\cong\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}-{\rm mod}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}A^{\circ\circ}

for any complete topological
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
A
, we deduce from (
34
) an isomorphism of formal modules over
\mathbb{F}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-action

{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}\cong\Spf(\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket),

(36)

where
\mathcal{O}_{K}
acts (continuously) on
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
by multiplication on itself. It follows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{p}{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}
is represented by the formal scheme
\Spf(\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket)
, where
\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket
is the
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
-adic completion of
\mathbb{F}[K]\otimes_{\mathbb{F}[\mathcal{O}_{K}]}\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket

(
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
being the maximal ideal of
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
). It also follows from the formula for
\gamma_{f}
in (
31
) that there exist elements
X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
satisfying

\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\rrbracket
such that we have isomorphisms
{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}(R)\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{F}-\alg}^{\cont}(\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket,R)\cong\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
for any perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
, where the second isomorphism is given by (where
X_{i}\mapsto r_{i}\in R^{\circ\circ}
)

(r_{0},\dots,r_{f-1})\in(R^{\circ\circ})^{f}\longmapsto F(r_{0},\dots,r_{f-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[r_{i}^{p^{-i-nf}}]p^{i+nf}\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}.

(37)

We then easily check that, in the coordinates
X_{i}
, the action of
K^{\times}
on
\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket
has
the following properties

\begin{cases}\forall\ 0\leq i\leq f-1,\,\forall\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}},&p^{n}(X_{i})=X_{i-n}^{p^{n}}\\
\forall\ 0\leq i\leq f-1,\,\forall\ a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times},&[a](X_{i})=\sigma_{0}(a)^{p^{i}}X_{i}\end{cases}

(38)

(with the usual convention that
X_{i+f}=X_{i}
). Finally, we let

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}^{\ad}\setminus\{0\},

where

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}^{\ad}
is the adic space over
\mathbb{F}
associated to the formal scheme

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{f,f,\mathbb{F}}
. We have an isomorphism

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\cong\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,X_{f-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)\setminus V(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}).

Note that the adic spaces
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
and
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
are both in

\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
.

We fix now
C
a perfectoid field containig
\mathbb{F}
and
v
a continuous
rank
1
valuation on
F
. If
x\in\mathbf{B}(C)
,
the
Newton polygon
of
x
, defined in
[
FF18
, Déf. 1.5.2, Déf. 1.6.18,
Déf. 1.6.21]
is a decreasing convex function from

\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\cup\{+\infty\}
. From
[
FF18
, Ex. 1.6.22]
, it can
be computed as the inverse Legendre transform of the function

\lambda\mapsto v_{\lambda}(x)
,
\lambda\in\left[0,+\infty\right[

(from which we remove the zero slope if it appears), where
v_{\lambda}
is the continuous
extension to
\mathbf{B}(C)
of the valuation
v_{\lambda}
defined in

[
FF18
, Déf. 1.4.1]
.

Lemma 2.3.6
.

Let
(x_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
be a family of nonzero elements of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
such
that, for any
\lambda\in]0,+\infty[
,

v(x_{n})+\lambda n\rightarrow+\infty
when
n\rightarrow-\infty
and

{v}(x_{n})\rightarrow 0
when
n\rightarrow+\infty
. Let

x{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[x_{n}]p^{n}\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)
.

(i)

Assume that

x\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
. Then the set of slopes of the
Newton polygon of
x
is of the form
ap^{{\mathbb{Z}}}

for some
a>0
if
and only if
v(x_{n})=v(x_{0})p^{-n}
for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. In this case we can choose
a=(p-1)v(x_{0})
.

(ii)

Assume that

x\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p}
. Then the set of slopes of the Newton
polygon of
x
is
(q-1)v(x_{0})q^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

Proof.

We prove
(i)
,
(ii)

being similar and simpler. For
\lambda\in]0,+\infty[
we
define
f_{x}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}v_{\lambda}(x)
. By
[
FF18
, §1.5.1]
, the
set of slopes of the Newton polygon of
x
is the set of breakpoints
of
f_{x}
. For any compact interval
[a,b]\subseteq]0,+\infty[
,
there exists an integer
N>0
such that

\forall\lambda\in[a,b],\qquad f_{x}(\lambda)=v_{\lambda}(x)=\inf_{n\in[-N,N]\cap{\mathbb{Z}}}(v(x_{n})+n\lambda).

This shows that the function
f_{x}

has only finitely many slopes in the interval
[a,b]
and that these
slopes are in the set
[-N,N]\cap{\mathbb{Z}}
. Therefore the function
f_{x}

has integral slopes. Moreover, as the breakpoints of
f_{x}
in

[a,b]
are coordinates of the intersection points of finitely many
lines,
f_{x}
has finitely many breakpoints in
[a,b]
. The
relation
\varphi_{q}(x)=p^{f}x
implies

qv_{\lambda/q}(x)=v_{\lambda}(x)+f\lambda
for all
\lambda>0
. As a
consequence, the set of breakpoints of the function
f_{x}
is
stable under multiplication by
q
and
q^{-1}
. Moreover, if

\lambda
is a regular point of
f_{x}
, so is
q\lambda
and

f^{\prime}_{x}(q\lambda)=f^{\prime}_{x}(\lambda)-f
. Let us fix
\lambda_{0}>0
some
regular point of
f_{x}
and let
s{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}}f^{\prime}_{x}(\lambda_{0})\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. As

f^{\prime}_{x}(q\lambda_{0})=s-f
, between
\lambda_{0}
and
q\lambda_{0}
,
f_{x}

can have at most
f+1
different slopes and so at most
f
breakpoints.
These breakpoints are representatives of the quotient of
the set of breakpoints of
f_{x}
by
q^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
. Therefore if the
set of breakpoints of
f_{x}
is of the form
ap^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
, for some

a>0
, then
f_{x}
has exactly
f
breakpoints in

[\lambda_{0},q\lambda_{0}]
which are of the form

\lambda_{1},p\lambda_{1},\dots,p^{f-1}\lambda_{1}
and the successive
slopes in this interval are
s,s-1,\dots,s-(f-1),s-f
. This implies that

\lambda_{1}
is the coordinate of the intersection point of the
graphs of the functions
\lambda\mapsto v(x_{s})+s\lambda
and

\lambda\mapsto v(x_{s-1})+(s-1)\lambda
, that is

\lambda_{1}=v(x_{s-1})-v(x_{s})
. Similarly, we have

p^{j}\lambda_{1}=v(x_{s-j-1})-v(x_{s-j})
for all
0\leq j\leq f-1
. Thus we have the relation

v(x_{n-1})-v(x_{n})=p(v(x_{n})-v(x_{n+1}))
for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. As the
sequence
(v(x_{n}))_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
is not constant and has limit
0
as
n\to+\infty
, we easily deduce that
v(x_{n})=v(x_{0})p^{-n}
for all

n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Moreover, as the computation shows,
v(x_{0})-v(x_{1})
is the
coordinate of a breakpoint up to some power of
p
, hence we can
choose
a=p(v(x_{0})-v(x_{1}))=(p-1)v(x_{0})
. A direct computation
following the same lines shows, conversely, that if

v(x_{n})=p^{-n}v(x_{0})
for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, then the set of breakpoints
of
f_{x}
is
(p-1)v(x_{0})p^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
∎

Recall the series
F(r)\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p}
from (
27
) and
F(x_{0},\dots,x_{f-1})\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
from (
37
)

Corollary 2.3.7
.

(i)

Let
t_{0},\dots,t_{f-1}\in C
such
that
v(t_{0}),\dots,v(t_{f-1})>0
. The set of slopes of the Newton
polygon of
F(t_{0},\dots,t_{f-1})
is of the form
ap^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
for
some
a>0
if and only if
v(t_{0})=v(t_{1})=\cdots=v(t_{f-1})
.
In this case we can choose
a=(p-1)v(t_{0})
.

(ii)

Let
t\in C
. The set of slopes
of the Newton polygon of
F(t)
is
(q-1)v(t_{0})q^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
2.3.6
.
∎

2.4
A “sum of divisors” map

We define and study certain open subspaces of the perfectoid spaces
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
and
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
of §
2.3
, as well as a canonical map
m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\longrightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
preserving these subspaces.

For any perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
, the product in the ring
\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)

induces a functorial map:

m_{R}:\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p})^{f}&\longrightarrow&\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}\\
(z_{1},\dots,z_{f})&\longmapsto&z_{1}\cdots z_{f}.\end{array}

(39)

Using Remark
2.3.3
, the fact that each
\mathbf{B}^{+}(k)
is a
domain for
k
a perfectoid field (see
[
FF18
, Thm. 6.2.1 & Thm. 3.6.1]
) and
[
SW20
, Prop. 8.2.8(2)]
, the family of maps

(m_{R})
induces a morphism of perfectoid spaces over
\mathbb{F}

m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\longrightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}.

(40)

The map
m_{R}
being compatible with the
actions of
(K^{\times})^{f}
(on the source) and
K^{\times}
(on the target), we deduce that

m
is compatible with the
actions of
(K^{\times})^{f}
and
K^{\times}
on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
and
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
, i.e.
m\circ(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})=(\prod_{i}a_{i})\circ m
. For
0\leq i\leq f-1
let
j_{i}
be the morphism
K^{\times}\rightarrow(K^{\times})^{f}
sending
a
to the
f
-uple with
1
at all entries
except at the
i
-th entry where it is
a
, then for all

a\in K^{\times}
and
0\leq i\leq f-1
, we have in particular

m\circ j_{i}(a)=a\circ m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}.

(41)

Remark 2.4.1
.

The map
m
can be reinterpreted using the Abel–Jacobi map (cf.
[
Far20
]
). Namely the sheaf on the
pro-étale site of
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
associated to the quotient presheaf

(\mathbf{B}^{+}(-)^{\varphi_{q}=p}\setminus\{0\})/K^{\times}
is isomorphic to the pro-étale sheaf

\Div_{\mathbb{F}}^{1}
of degree
1
divisors on the relative
Fargues–Fontaine curve over
\mathbb{F}
and likewise

(\mathbf{B}^{+}(-)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}\setminus\{0\})/K^{\times}
is isomorphic to the pro-étale sheaf

\Div^{f}_{\mathbb{F}}
of degree
f
divisors. The map
m
induces a morphism
of pro-étale sheaves
(\Div_{\mathbb{F}}^{1})^{f}\rightarrow\Div^{f}_{\mathbb{F}}
which
is given by the sum of divisors, cf.
[
Far20
, §2.4]
.

The group
\mathfrak{S}_{f}
acts on the left on
(K^{\times})^{f}
by permutation of
coordinates:

\forall\ \sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f},\,\forall\ (a_{i})_{0\leq i\leq f-1}\in(K^{\times})^{f},\quad\sigma(a_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(a_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}).

The group
\mathfrak{S}_{f}
acts likewise on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
by permuting the factors

(\widetilde{G}_{\mathrm{LT}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})}\Spf(\mathbb{F})\setminus\{0\})^{\ad}
so that the action
of
(K^{\times})^{f}
on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
extends to an action of the semi-direct product

(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
. Let
\Delta
be the kernel of the
multiplication
(K^{\times})^{f}\rightarrow K^{\times}
and

\Delta_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\Delta\cap(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}
. Then

\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
is a subgroup of

(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
and the map
m
is invariant under
the action of
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
. By

[
Far20
, Lemme 7.6]
2
2

2

Note that
[
Far20
, Lemme 7.6]
extends scalars to
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, however the proof works the same without extending scalars as it is based on the proof of
[
Far20
, Prop. 2.18]
where one does not extend scalars.
, the map
m
induces an isomorphism of
pro-étale sheaves on
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}

\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}\backslash Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}.

(42)

We let
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
be the open subspace of
\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket)
defined by the
relations

\lvert X_{0}\rvert=\dots=\lvert X_{f-1}\rvert\neq 0

(it is open as it is the intersection over
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
of the rational open subsets
U(\frac{X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}}{X_{i}})
of
\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket)
).
Note that we have
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
. We also define

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}m^{-1}(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}})
, an open subspace of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
, so that
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
are both in
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
. We now give explicit descriptions of
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
.

We start with
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
. We denote by
A_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}}(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}})
the ring of global
sections on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
.

Lemma 2.4.2
.

The following statements hold.

(i)

The ring
A_{\infty}
is the perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\left\langle\left(\frac{X_{i}}{X_{0}}\right)^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\!\!,\ 1\leq i\leq f-1\right\rangle.

(ii)

We have
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}=\Spa(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ})
, in particular
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is affinoid perfectoid.

(iii)

There exists a multiplicative
norm
\lvert\cdot\rvert
on
A_{\infty}
such that
\lvert X_{0}\rvert=p^{-1}
inducing
the topology of
A_{\infty}
.

(iv)

Any quasi-compact open subset of
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
whose points of rank
1
are exactly the points of
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
of rank
1
is necessarily
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
itself.

Proof.

Define the adic spaces

T^{\mathrm{gen}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\{\lvert X_{0}\rvert=\cdots=\lvert X_{f-1}\rvert\neq 0\}\subseteq T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\rrbracket).

It is enough to prove (i), (ii) and (iii) replacing everywhere

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket)
by
T^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq T

(i.e. completed perfection will not change the arguments in the proof
below). Moreover, as the map

T=\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\rrbracket)\rightarrow\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,X_{f-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)
is a
homeomorphism, it is also enough to prove (iv) with
T^{\mathrm{gen}}
and

T\setminus V(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})
.

We first show the analogue (iii). Let

S\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{0})\mkern-3.0mu)\left\langle\left(\frac{X_{i}}{X_{0}}\right)^{\pm 1},\ 1\leq i\leq f-1\right\rangle
that we endow with the
X_{0}
-adic topology (it is a Tate algebra), then the norm in (iii) is the unique multiplicative extension to
S
of the Gauss norm on the restricted power series
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{0})\mkern-3.0mu)\left\langle\left(\frac{X_{i}}{X_{0}}\right),\ 1\leq i\leq f-1\right\rangle
(which is well-known to be multiplicative). Note that
S^{\circ}=\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0}\rrbracket\left\langle\left(\frac{X_{i}}{X_{0}}\right)^{\pm 1},\ 1\leq i\leq f-1\right\rangle
is the unit ball for this norm.

Let us prove (the analogues of) (i) and (ii). Looking at continuous valuations, it is clear that the morphism of adic spaces
\Spa(S,S^{\circ})\rightarrow T
factors as
\Spa(S,S^{\circ})\rightarrow T^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq T
. In order to prove that the morphism of adic spaces
\Spa(S,S^{\circ})\rightarrow T^{\mathrm{gen}}
is an isomorphism, it is enough to prove that it induces an isomorphism
\Spa(S,S^{\circ})(W)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}T^{\mathrm{gen}}(W)
for any analytic adic space
W
over
\mathbb{F}
, and it is enough to take
W=\Spa(R,R^{+})
for an arbitrary complete analytic Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
\mathbb{F}
(the case
R
Tate would be enough). Then this easily follows from the definitions of
T
and
S
.

Let us finally prove (the analogue of) (iv). First note that
T\setminus V(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})

is the analytic locus of the adic space
T
, the only non-analytic
point of
T
being the unique (rank
0
) valuation with kernel the
maximal ideal of the local ring
\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\rrbracket
. Let

U
be a quasi-compact open subset of
T\setminus V(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})
whose points of rank
1
are the points of
T^{\mathrm{gen}}
of rank
1
. For
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
consider the open subset
U_{i}
of
T
defined by
|X_{j}|\leq|X_{i}|\neq 0
for all
j
, or equivalently (by the same argument as for the proof of (i))

U_{i}=\mathrm{Spa}\Big(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{i})\mkern-3.0mu)\left\langle\frac{X_{j}}{X_{i}},j\neq i\right\rangle,\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{i}\rrbracket\left\langle\frac{X_{j}}{X_{i}},j\neq i\right\rangle\Big)\subseteq T\setminus V(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}).

Then
U\cap U_{i}
and
T^{\mathrm{gen}}=\bigcap_{j}U_{j}
are two open subsets of
U_{i}
with the same points of rank
1
, and thus
a fortiori
with the same points with residue field being a finite extension of
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{i})\mkern-3.0mu)
. Let
U_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}\subseteq U_{i}
(resp.
(T^{\mathrm{gen}})^{\mathrm{rig}}\subseteq T^{\mathrm{gen}}
) be the subset of points of
U_{i}
(resp.
T^{\mathrm{gen}}
) with residue field being a finite extension of
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{i})\mkern-3.0mu)
, then
U_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}
(resp.
(T^{\mathrm{gen}})^{\mathrm{rig}}
) can be identified with the affinoid rigid analytic space over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(X_{i})\mkern-3.0mu)
corresponding to
U_{i}
(resp.
T^{\mathrm{gen}}
) by
[
Hub96
, (1.1.11)(a)]
, and we have
U\cap U_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}=(T^{\mathrm{gen}})^{\mathrm{rig}}
. Note that
U
,
U_{i}
and
T^{\mathrm{gen}}
are quasi-compact (
U
by assumption,
U_{i}
,
T^{\mathrm{gen}}
as they are affinoid). As
T
is a quasi-separated adic space (being spectral as the adic space associated to a Huber pair, see for instance
[
Mor
, Cor. III.2.4]
), the open subset
U\cap U_{i}
is still quasi-compact. As
U_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}
is quasi-separated, we deduce
U\cap U_{i}=T^{\mathrm{gen}}
from
U\cap U_{i}^{\mathrm{rig}}=(T^{\mathrm{gen}})^{\mathrm{rig}}
by
[
Hub96
, (1.1.11)]
(see also
[
Sch12
, Thm. 2.21]
). Since
U=\bigcup_{i}(U\cap U_{i})
(as
U\subseteq T\setminus V(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})
), we finally obtain
U=T^{\mathrm{gen}}
in
T
.
∎

Lemma 2.4.3
.

The following statements hold.

(i)

The open subset
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
of

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
is stable under the action of
K^{\times}
.

(ii)

The open subset
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
is stable
under the action of
(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
.

Proof.

(ii) can be easily deduced from (i) and
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}m^{-1}(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}})
, so we only prove (i).

The fact that

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is stable under the actions of
p
and
p^{-1}
on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}

is a direct computation on
\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,X_{f-1}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket\hookrightarrow A_{\infty}^{\circ}
using (
38
). Let us show that
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is stable under the action of

\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
. It follows from Lemma
2.4.2
(iv)
that it is sufficient to check that
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
is
stable under the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket)(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})=\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
for
C
a perfectoid field containing
\mathbb{F}
(using
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}(C,C^{+})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
for any open bounded valuation subring
C^{+}\subseteq C
). Recall that
\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
is the set of converging power series in
\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)
:

F(x_{0},\dots,x_{f-1})=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}[x_{i}^{p^{-i-nf}}]p^{i+nf}

where
\lvert x_{i}\rvert<1
for all
i
with
\lvert\cdot\rvert
a fixed power-multiplicative norm on
C
(e.g. as in (
25
)). A point
x\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
is in
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
if and
only if
0\neq\lvert x_{0}\rvert=\cdots=\lvert x_{f-1}\rvert<1
, equivalently
if and only if its Newton polygon has slopes
\{cp^{n},\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}

for some
c>0
by
Corollary
2.3.7
. As the Newton polygon of
x
only depends on the norms
\lvert x\rvert_{\rho}
for
0<\rho<1
(see
[
FF18
, Ex. 1.6.22]
and (
26
) for
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{\rho}
), it is enough to show that

\lvert x\rvert_{\rho}
does not change if we multiply
x
by an element of

\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
. This follows from the multiplicativity of

\lvert\cdot\rvert_{\rho}
(see
[
FF18
, Prop. 1.4.9]
) and the fact that

\lvert\cdot\rvert_{\rho}
induces the
p
-adic norm on
K
.
∎

From Lemma
2.4.3
we deduce a continuous action of

K^{\times}
on the topological
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
A_{\infty}
. We denote by

\varphi
the endomorphism of
A_{\infty}
induced by the action of

p\in K^{\times}
. It is
\mathbb{F}
-linear and satisfies (see (
38
))

\varphi(X_{i})=X_{i-1}^{p}\ {\rm for}\ 0\leq i\leq f-1

(43)

(with
X_{-1}=X_{f-1}
as usual). We also note
\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi^{f}
(which coincides with
x\mapsto x^{q}
on
A_{\infty}
when
\mathbb{F}_{q}=\mathbb{F}
).

We now give an explicit description of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
.

Recall first that if a locally profinite group
H
acts continuously on a perfectoid space
X^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{F}
, a morphism
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
in
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
(
H
acting trivially on
X
) is a pro-étale
H
-torsor if there exists a pro-étale cover
Y\rightarrow X
in
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
such that there is an isomorphism
X^{\prime}\times_{X}Y\cong\underline{H}\times Y
in
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
, where
\underline{H}
is the sheaf on
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
defined by
\underline{H}(T)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}{\rm Cont}(|T|,H)
,
|T|
being the underlying topological space of the perfectoid space
T
(note that
\underline{H}\times Y
is perfectoid by
[
Sch
, Lemma 10.13]
).

Let
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
be the additive group quotient of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}
by the diagonal embedding of
{\mathbb{Z}}

into
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}
. If
\underline{n}=(n_{0},\dots,n_{f-1})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
we let

U_{\underline{n}}
be the open affinoid perfectoid subspace of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\subseteq\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,0}{{}^{1/p^{\infty}}},\dots,T_{K,f-1}{{}^{1/p^{\infty}}}\rrbracket)
defined
by the relations

\lvert T_{K,i}\rvert^{p^{n_{j}}}=\lvert T_{K,j}\rvert^{p^{n_{i}}}\neq 0,\qquad\forall\ 0\leq i,j\leq f-1

or equivalently
\lvert T_{K,i}\rvert=\lvert T_{K,0}\rvert^{p^{n_{i}-n_{0}}}
for
0\leq i\leq f-1
. Note that
U_{\underline{n}}
is well-defined as it only depends on the class of
\underline{n}
in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
, and that
U_{\underline{n}}
is disjoint from
U_{\underline{n}^{\prime}}
if
\underline{n}\neq\underline{n}^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. The group
\mathfrak{S}_{f}
acts on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
by
permutation, for
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
and

\underline{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\sigma(\underline{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(n_{\sigma^{-1}(i)})_{0\leq i\leq f-1}

and
we check that

\sigma(U_{\underline{n}})=U_{\sigma(\underline{n})}
. Moreover, if

\underline{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in(K^{\times})^{f}
, we also easily check that (where
v_{p}
is the unique valuation on
K
with
v_{p}(p)=1
):

\underline{a}(U_{\underline{n}})=U_{\underline{n}+fv_{p}(\underline{a})}.

Proposition 2.4.4
.

Let
\underline{n}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(0,1,\dots,f-1)
and let
({\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}})_{0}
be the image in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of the subgroup of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}
of
\underline{m}=(m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1})
such that
\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}m_{i}=0
. We have in
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\bigcup_{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}}U_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}=\coprod_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\coprod_{\underline{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}})_{0}}U_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}=\!\!\coprod_{\gamma\in(\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f})/\Delta_{1}}\gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}}).

(44)

Moreover for each
U_{\underline{n}}
in (
44
) the map
m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
restricts to a pro-étale
\Delta_{1}
-torsor
m|_{U_{\underline{n}}}:U_{\underline{n}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
.

Proof.

One first easily checks that any element in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of the form
\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}
can uniquely be written (in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
) as
\sigma^{\prime}(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m^{\prime}}
for a unique
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
and a unique
\underline{m^{\prime}}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}})_{0}
. Assuming
\gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})=U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
when
\gamma\in\Delta_{1}
this gives the last two equalities in (
44
) (recall that
\Delta_{1}
is normal in
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
).

We check that
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and

\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\bigcup_{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}}U_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}

have the same rank
1
points, i.e. the same
(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
-points, where
C
is a perfectoid field containing
\mathbb{F}
(recall
that
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}(C,C^{+})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}Z_{\mathrm{LT}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})

for any open bounded valuation subring
C^{+}\subseteq C
). We use Newton
polygons and notation as in the proof of Lemma

2.4.3
. If

(F(t_{0}),\dots,F(t_{f-1}))\in(\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p})^{f}
, the element

F(t_{i})
has slopes
\{(q-1)v(t_{i})q^{n},\ \!n\!\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
by Corollary

2.3.7
, where
v
is the valuation of
C
such
that
\lvert\cdot\rvert=q^{-v(\cdot)}
, and recall that

(F(t_{0}),\dots,F(t_{f-1}))\in Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
if and only if

F(t_{0})\cdots F(t_{f-1})\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)^{\varphi_{q}=p^{f}}
lies in

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
. As the slopes of the Newton polygon of a
product
ab
in
\mathbf{B}^{+}(C)
is the union of the slopes of the Newton
polygons of
a
and
b
(see
[
FF18
, Prop. 1.6.20]
for instance), we
see that
F(t_{0})\cdots F(t_{f-1})\in Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
if and
only if there exists
c>0
such that

\bigcup_{i}\{(q-1)v(t_{i})q^{n},\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}=\{cp^{n},\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
(see the proof of
Lemma
2.4.3
). Equivalently

F(t_{0})\cdots F(t_{f-1})\in Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
if and only if
there exist
c>0
,
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
and

m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
v(t_{i})=cp^{\sigma^{-1}(i)+fm_{i}}

for
0\leq i\leq f-1
if and only if there exist

\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
and
m_{0},\dots,m_{f-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that

v(t_{i})=p^{(\sigma^{-1}(i)+fm_{i})-(\sigma^{-1}(0)+fm_{0})}v(t_{0})
for

0\leq i\leq f-1
if and only if

F(t_{0})\cdots F(t_{f-1})\in U_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}
. This proves our statement on rank
1
points.

For a point
x
of the analytic adic space
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
define
\tilde{x}\in Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
as its maximal generization, then the corresponding valuation
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{\tilde{x}}
is of rank
1
, i.e. real valued (see for instance
[
Hub96
, Lemma 1.1.10]
or
[
Mor
, Cor. II.2.4.8]
). Thus one can define continuous maps as in
[
SW20
, proof of Prop. 4.2.6]
:

\kappa_{i,j}:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\rightarrow]0,+\infty[,\ x\mapsto\kappa_{i,j}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\tfrac{\log(\lvert T_{K,i}\rvert_{\tilde{x}})}{\log(\lvert T_{K,j}\rvert_{\tilde{x}})}.

For
\underline{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
, define the closed subset of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}

V_{\underline{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\kappa^{-1}(p^{n_{0}-n_{1}},\dots,p^{n_{0}-n_{f-1}}),

where
\kappa=(\kappa_{0,1},\dots,\kappa_{0,f-1})
. For
x\in U_{\underline{n}}
, we still have
\tilde{x}\in U_{\underline{n}}
by
[
Hub96
, Lemma 1.1.10(v)]
applied to
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}U_{\underline{n}}\hookrightarrow Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
, hence we have an inclusion of topological spaces
U_{\underline{n}}\subseteq V_{\underline{n}}
. Let us prove that the open subspace
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
is contained in
V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\bigcup_{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}}V_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}
. Let
x\in Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
of rank
1
, then
x\in U_{\underline{n}}\subseteq V_{\underline{n}}
for some
\underline{n}
of the form
\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}
by the second paragraph. As
V_{\underline{n}}
is closed, we have
\overline{\{x\}}\subseteq V_{\underline{n}}
. Now let
x\in Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
be any point and
\tilde{x}
its maximal generization (which is in
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
by
[
Hub96
, Lemma 1.1.10(v)]
applied to
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\hookrightarrow Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
), then
\tilde{x}
is of rank
1
and
x\in\overline{\{\tilde{x}\}}
, which implies
x\in V_{\underline{n}}
for some
\underline{n}
, i.e.
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq V
. As
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is open in
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
, we have
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq\mathring{V}\subseteq V
, where
\mathring{V}
is the interior of the topological space
V
in
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
(
\mathring{V}
is then open in the perfectoid space
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
, hence itself a perfectoid space). Let
x\in\mathring{V}
, then
x\in V_{\underline{n}}
for some
{\underline{n}}
. But
V_{\underline{n}}
is open in
V
as
V
is the inverse image by
\kappa
of a discrete set and
V_{\underline{n}}
is the inverse image of a single, hence open, element in this discrete set. Hence there exists an open subset
U
of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
such that
V_{\underline{n}}=U\cap V
. As
x\in U\cap\mathring{V}
which is open in
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
, we deduce
x\in\mathring{V_{\underline{n}}}
which proves that
\mathring{V}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\bigcup_{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathring{V}_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}
. Thus we finally have
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\subseteq\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\bigcup_{\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathring{V}_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}}
which implies (using the first sentence of the proof)

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}=\coprod_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\coprod_{\underline{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}})_{0}}(Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}})

(45)

as open (perfectoid) subspaces of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
.

Now we go into group actions. It is not hard to check that
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
stabilizes
V
(inside
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
), more precisely
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
sends
V_{\underline{n}}
to
V_{\sigma(\underline{n})}
,
(p^{d_{0}},\dots,p^{d_{f-1}})\in\Delta\cap(p^{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{f}
sends
V_{\underline{n}}
to
V_{\underline{n}+f(d_{0},\dots,d_{f-1})}
and
\Delta_{1}
preserves each
V_{\underline{n}}
(indeed, using that
f(\widetilde{x})=\widetilde{f(x)}
for any
x\in Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
and any endomorphism
f
of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
by
[
Hub96
, Lemma 1.1.10(iv)&(v)]
, it is enough to check this for rank
1
points, i.e.
(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
-points for perfectoid fields
C
containing
\mathbb{F}
, which is an easy exercise left to the reader). Then by continuity of the action of
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
the same holds for the interiors
\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
, and thus also for
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
by Lemma
2.4.3
(ii). In particular, the group
(\Delta\cap(p^{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{f})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
permutes transitively the perfectoid spaces
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
for
\underline{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of the form
\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}
as in (
45
), and the group
\Delta_{1}
preserves each
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
. Thus the associated sheaf
\underline{\Delta_{1}}
acts on (the sheaf corresponding to)
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
, and one easily checks that the group
\Delta_{1}
moreover acts freely on the
(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
-points of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
. By the proof of
[
Wei17
, Prop. 4.3.2]
,
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
is a pro-étale
\Delta_{1}
-torsor over
\Delta_{1}\backslash(Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}})
, seen as a pro-étale sheaf on
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
. Since
\Delta_{1}
is a normal subgroup in
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
, we deduce with (
45
) that
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is a pro-étale
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-torsor over

\displaystyle\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}\backslash Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle((\Delta\cap(p^{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{f})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f})\backslash(\Delta_{1}\backslash Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle((\Delta\cap(p^{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{f})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f})\backslash\big(\coprod_{\sigma,\underline{m}}\Delta_{1}\backslash(Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}})\big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}\backslash(Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}})

for each
\underline{n}=\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}
as in (
45
). Now, it follows from (
42
) (and Lemma
2.4.3
) that we have an isomorphism
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}\backslash Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
of pro-étale sheaves, hence
\Delta_{1}\backslash(Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}})\cong Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
for each
\underline{n}
as above.

We now finish the proof. As
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is affinoid perfectoid by Lemma
2.4.2
(ii), each
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
is affinoid perfectoid by
[
SW20
, Prop. 9.3.1]
, in particular is a quasi-compact open subset of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
. The quasi-compact open subspaces
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
and
U_{\underline{n}}
of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\subseteq\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket)\setminus V(T_{K,0})
have the same points of rank
1
by the second paragraph of this proof, and we can then argue in a similar way as for the proof of Lemma
2.4.2
(iv), applying the results in
[
Hub96
, (1.1.11)]
(or
[
Sch12
, Thm. 2.21]
) to the affinoid rigid analytic space over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,0})\mkern-3.0mu)
associated to
\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,0},\dots,T_{K,{f-1}}\rrbracket)\setminus V(T_{K,0})
(recalling that
\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,0},\dots,T_{K,{f-1}}\rrbracket)\rightarrow\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,0}^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,T_{K,{f-1}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)=Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
is a homeomorphism). In particular, we obtain
U_{\underline{n}}=Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\cap\mathring{V}_{\underline{n}}
for all
\underline{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of the form
\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})+f\underline{m}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

As a consequence of the above proof and of
[
Sch
, Lemma 10.13]
, we also obtain:

Corollary 2.4.5
.

The map
m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is a pro-étale
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-torsor, in particular is a pro-étale cover.

Remark 2.4.6
.

Note that
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is not affinoid (contrary to
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
) as it is not quasi-compact.

Let us denote by
A^{\prime}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})
the ring of global
sections on
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
. The following result on
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
can be proved exactly as Lemma
2.4.2
, and we leave the details to the reader.

Lemma 2.4.7
.

The following statements hold.

(i)

The ring
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
is the perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,0}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\left\langle\left(\frac{T_{K,i}}{T_{K,0}^{p^{i}}}\right)^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\!\!,\ 1\leq i\leq f-1\right\rangle.

(ii)

We have
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}=\Spa(A^{\prime}_{\infty},(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ})
.

(iii)

There exists a multiplicative norm

\lvert\cdot\rvert
on
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
such that
\lvert T_{K,0}\rvert=p^{-1}
inducing
the topology of
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
.

(iv)

Any quasi-compact open subset of
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
whose points of rank
1
are exactly the points of
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
of rank
1
is necessarily
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
itself.

2.5
Equivariant vector bundles on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}

We show that continuous
(\!K^{\times}\!)^{f}\!\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundles on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A_{\infty}
are the same thing.

Recall first that if
X
is an adic space with a left action of a group
H
,
an
H
-equivariant vector bundle on
X
is a locally finite free
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{V}
with a collection of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear isomorphisms
(c_{h}:h^{*}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V})_{h\in H}
satisfying
the relation
c_{h_{2}h_{1}}=c_{h_{1}}\circ h_{1}^{*}(c_{h_{2}})
for all

h_{1},h_{2}\in H
. This induces a
right
action of
H
on

\Gamma(X,\mathcal{V})
given by

c_{h}^{*}:\Gamma(X,\mathcal{V})=\Gamma(X,h^{*}\mathcal{V})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\Gamma(X,\mathcal{V}).

Now assume that
X
is perfectoid space (the only case we will use) and that
H
is a locally profinite topological group acting continuously on
X
. Let
\mathcal{V}
be a vector bundle on
X
, for an open affinoid perfectoid subspace
U=\Spa(A,A^{+})\subseteq X
, the finite projective
A
-module
\mathcal{V}(U)
is endowed with the Banach topology given by the quotient topology of any surjection of
A
-modules
A^{\oplus d}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{V}(U)
. If
U\subseteq X
is any open subspace, we endow
\mathcal{V}(U)\cong\displaystyle{\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longleftarrow\\
U^{\prime}\subseteq U\end{subarray}}\mathcal{V}(U^{\prime})}
with the projective limit topology, where
U^{\prime}
ranges over open affinoid subspaces of
U
, and we define
H_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\{h\in H,\ h(U)=U\}
, which is a closed subgroup of
H
by continuity of the action of
H
on
X
. We then define a
continuous

H
-equivariant vector bundle on
X
as an
H
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
on
X
such that for any open subspace
U\subseteq X
the natural map
H_{U}\times\mathcal{V}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{V}(U)
,
(h,s)\mapsto c_{h}^{*}(s)
is continuous (for the product topology on the left).

By Lemma
2.4.2
(i),(ii) and
[
KL15
, Thm. 2.7.7]
or
[
KL
, Thm. 3.5.8]
, the functor of global sections induces an
equivalence of categories from the category of vector bundles on

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
to the category of finite projective

A_{\infty}
-modules. This equivalence is exact, rank preserving and compatible
with tensor products. As a finite projective
A_{\infty}
-module is in fact always free
(see
[
DH21
, Thm. 2.19]
) and as the action of
K^{\times}
on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is continuous, we see that the functor of global sections induces a rank-preserving
\otimes
-equivalence of categories from the category of continuous
K^{\times}
-equivariant vector bundles on

Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
to the category of étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
over
A_{\infty}
, where
\varphi
on
A_{\infty}
is given by (
43
).

As
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is perfectoid and as the fibered category of vector bundles on
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{F}}
is a
v
-stack by
[
SW20
, Lemma 17.1.8]
, we easily deduce from Corollary
2.4.5
an equivalence of categories between the category of (continuous)
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundles on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and the category of vector bundles on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
(the continuity condition is then automatic in that case, as
\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
acts continuously on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
), hence also between the category of continuous
(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundles on

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and the category of continuous
K^{\times}
-equivariant vector
bundles on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
. In both cases this equivalence is given by the two
functors

\mathcal{V}\mapsto(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}

and
\mathcal{W}\mapsto m^{*}\mathcal{W}
, where
m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
. If
\mathcal{V}
is

(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant, the

K^{\times}
-equivariant structure on

(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}
can be made explicit as follows. For
a\in K^{\times}
and any
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
we have an isomorphism using the notation in (
41
)

a^{*}m_{*}\mathcal{V}\cong(a^{-1})_{*}m_{*}\mathcal{V}\cong(a^{-1}m)_{*}\mathcal{V}\cong(mj_{i}(a)^{-1})_{*}\mathcal{V}\cong m_{*}(j_{i}(a)^{-1})_{*}\mathcal{V}\cong m_{*}j_{i}(a)^{*}\mathcal{V}

(where the first isomorphism is
\id\in\Hom(m_{*}\mathcal{V},m_{*}\mathcal{V})=\Hom((a^{-1})^{*}a^{*}m_{*}\mathcal{V},m_{*}\mathcal{V})\cong\Hom(a^{*}m_{*}\mathcal{V},(a^{-1})_{*}m_{*}\mathcal{V})
, the third comes from (
41
) and the last is analogous to the first). We then obtain an isomorphism of sheaves for
a\in K^{\times}
and any
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
:

m_{*}(c_{j_{i}(a)}):a^{*}m_{*}\mathcal{V}\cong m_{*}j_{i}(a)^{*}\mathcal{V}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}m_{*}\mathcal{V}

which preserves the subsheaf
(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}
(as

\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
is a normal subgroup of

(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
) and induces an isomorphism
m_{*}(c_{j_{i}(a)}):a^{*}(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}
which does not depend on
i
.

We deduce from Proposition
2.4.4
that we have an
isomorphism of perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebras

A_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\sim}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\Delta_{1}}
, and as above using

[
SW20
, Lemma 17.1.8]
that there is also an equivalence of
categories between the category of
\Delta_{1}
-equivariant vector
bundles on
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
and the category of vector bundles
on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
. Using again
[
KL15
, Thm. 2.7.7]
(or
[
KL
, Thm. 3.5.8]
) and

[
DH21
, Thm. 2.19]
, we deduce:

Theorem 2.5.1
.

The functor

D_{A_{\infty}}\mapsto A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}
induces
an exact rank-preserving
\otimes
-equivalence of categories from the category of finite free

A_{\infty}
-modules to the category of finite free
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
-modules
with a semi-linear action of
\Delta_{1}
. A quasi-inverse is
given by
D_{A^{\prime}_{\infty}}\mapsto D_{A^{\prime}_{\infty}}^{\Delta_{1}}
.

Let
\delta\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
be the cyclic permutation
i\mapsto i+1
(with
f-1\mapsto 0
). If

\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
, let

p_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(1,\dots,p,\dots,1)\in(K^{\times})^{f}
with
p
at the

\sigma(0)
-th entry. From the discussion before Proposition
2.4.4
we get

(p_{\sigma}\circ\sigma)(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})=p_{\sigma}(U_{\sigma(\underline{n}_{0})})=U_{\sigma\delta(\underline{n}_{0})}.

In particular,
p_{\delta^{-1}}\circ\delta^{-1}:U_{\underline{n}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
and we define an
\mathbb{F}
-linear continuous automorphism
\varphi
of

A^{\prime}_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})
by

\varphi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(p_{\delta^{-1}}\circ\delta^{-1})^{*}=(\delta^{-1})^{*}\circ p_{\delta^{-1}}^{*}.

(46)

Using (
28
) and since
\delta^{-1}(0)=f-1
this automorphism
is easily checked to satisfy

\varphi(T_{K,i})=T_{K,i+1}\ {\rm for\ }i\neq f-1\ {\rm and}\ \varphi(T_{K,f-1})=T_{K,0}^{q}.

(47)

In particular,
\varphi^{f}
on
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
is
\mathbb{F}
-linear and such that
\varphi^{f}(T_{K,i})=T_{K,i}^{q}
for all
i
. Moreover if

\underline{a}\in(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}
, we have
\varphi\circ\underline{a}=\delta(\underline{a})\circ\varphi
, where

\delta(\underline{a})=(a_{i-1})_{0\leq i\leq f-1}
(with
a_{-1}=a_{f-1}
), in particular
\varphi^{f}
commutes with
(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}
. As
m:Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is
K^{\times}
-equivariant and
\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant, the action of
K^{\times}
on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
being through
j_{i}
for any
0\leq i\leq f-1
and the action of
\mathfrak{S}_{f}
on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
being trivial, the isomorphism
A_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\sim}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\Delta_{1}}

commutes with the actions of
\varphi
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on both sides (see (
43
) for
\varphi
on
A_{\infty}
).

The following result sums up the previous discussion and gives a more explicit way to compute
the
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
associated to a
continuous
(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundle on

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
.

Corollary 2.5.2
.

There is an equivalence of categories between the category of continuous
(\!K^{\times}\!)^{f}\!\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundles on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and the category of étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A_{\infty}
. If
\mathcal{V}
is a
continuous
(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundle on

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
, its associated
A_{\infty}
-module is

\Gamma(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}},(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}})
which is
isomorphic to

\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})^{\Delta_{1}}
. The action of

a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on

\Gamma(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}},(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}})
is
induced by the action of
(a,1\dots,1)=j_{0}(a)
on

\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})
and the action of

\varphi
on
\Gamma(\!Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}},(m_{*}\mathcal{V})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\!)
is induced by

(\delta^{-1})^{*}\circ p_{\delta^{-1}}^{*}:\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})=\Gamma(U_{\delta^{-1}(\underline{n}_{0})},(p_{\delta^{-1}}^{*}\mathcal{V})|_{U_{\delta^{-1}(\underline{n}_{0})}})\\
\cong\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},((p_{\delta^{-1}}\circ\delta^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{V})|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}}).

2.6
The
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
of an arbitrary Galois representation

To an arbitrary
\overline{\rho}
we functorially associate an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
over
A
for
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be a continuous representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
on a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}
-vector space and
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
its
Lubin–Tate
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module (see §
2.1
). The étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})

is the space of global sections of a continuous
K^{\times}
-equivariant vector
bundle
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
on

\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)
, where
p
acts by
\varphi^{f}\otimes\varphi_{q}
. For
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
we define

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{i}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}),

where
\iota_{i}
denotes the
\mathbb{F}
-linear embedding
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}
corresponding to
T_{K,\sigma_{0}}\mapsto T_{K,i}
. Each
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}
is a
\Delta
-equivariant vector bundle on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
with
(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in\Delta\subseteq(K^{\times})^{f}
acting on
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
via
a_{i}
. In particular,
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}}
is a
\Delta_{1}
-equivariant vector
bundle on
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
and
\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})=A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{i}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
. We define for
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}

D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})^{\Delta_{1}}=(A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{i}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}))^{\Delta_{1}}

(48)

which is a finite free
A_{\infty}
-module of rank

\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}
by Theorem
2.5.1
.

The endomorphism

\varphi^{f}\otimes\varphi_{q}
on

A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{i}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
(see below (
47
) for
\varphi^{f}
on
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
) commutes with the action of
\Delta_{1}
and
induces a
\varphi_{q}
-semi-linear automorphism of

D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
, which is thus naturally a

\varphi_{q}
-module (see below (
43
) for
\varphi_{q}
on
A_{\infty}
). The action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on

A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{i}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
defined by

a(x\otimes v)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}j_{i}(a)(x)\otimes a(v)
induces a continuous semi-linear
action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
(with respect to the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
A_{\infty}
) which commutes with
\varphi_{q}
. In particular,
D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
is naturally an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
. Note that the functor

\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
from continuous representations of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
on finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces to étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A_{\infty}
is exact and
\mathbb{F}
-linear. We also have isomorphisms of functors for
0\leq i\leq f-1
:

\phi_{i}:D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(-)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i+1)}(-),\qquad\phi_{i}=\begin{cases}\varphi\otimes\Id&\text{if }i<f-1\\
\varphi\otimes\varphi_{q}&\text{if }i=f-1.\end{cases}

(49)

We now show that étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\infty}
, and hence étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A_{\infty}
, canonically descend to the ring
A
of §
2.2
. First we need an easy lemma.

Lemma 2.6.1
.

The ring
A
of §
2.2
can be identified with the ring of global sections of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}
on the
rational open subset of the adic space
\Spa(\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket)
defined by the
relations

\lvert Y_{\sigma_{0}}\rvert=\cdots=\lvert Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}\rvert\neq 0,

where the variables
Y_{\sigma_{i}}\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket
are defined in (
18
).

Proof.

Recall that
A
is by definition the completed localization
(\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})})^{\wedge}=(\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}\rrbracket_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})})^{\wedge}
, where the completion is for the
(Y_{\sigma_{0}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})
-adic topology. Then using (for instance)
[
BHH+
, Rk. 3.1.1.3(iii)]
one easily checks that

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}[(Y_{\sigma_{1}}/Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{\pm 1},\dots,(Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}/Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{\pm 1}]\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}}\rrbracket[1/Y_{\sigma_{0}}]

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(Y_{\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)\langle(Y_{\sigma_{1}}/Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{\pm 1},\dots,(Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}/Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{\pm 1}\rangle,

where
\langle\ \rangle
means, as usual, the corresponding Tate algebra with respect to the non-archimedean local field
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(Y_{\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)
. This is exactly the Tate algebra of the statement.
∎

Note that the open subset of Lemma
2.6.1
is stable under the endomorphisms deduced from the actions of
p
and

\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\mathcal{O}_{K}
by multiplication, in particular the
\mathbb{F}
-linear endomorphism
\varphi
on
A
sending
Y_{\sigma_{i}}
to
Y_{\sigma_{i-1}}^{p}
(see §
2.2
) is the one deduced from the action of
p
.

Remark 2.6.2
.

It follows from Lemma
2.6.1
and
[
Lüt77
, Satz 3, p. 131]
(we thank Ofer Gabber for pointing out this reference to us) that any projective
A
-module of finite type is actually free.

Let
X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}
be as at the end of §
2.3
, we have
\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}\rrbracket
, and from the equalities in (
38
) we deduce that there is
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
such that for
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}

X_{i}={\sigma_{0}(\lambda)^{p^{i}}}Y_{\sigma_{i}}+({\rm degree}\geq 2\ {\rm in\ the\ variables}\ Y_{\sigma_{j}}).

(50)

This easily implies an isomorphism of completed localized rings

\big(\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}\rrbracket_{(X_{0}\cdots X_{f-1})}\big)^{\wedge}\cong\big(\mathbb{F}\llbracket Y_{\sigma_{0}},\dots,Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}\rrbracket_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})}\big)^{\wedge}=A,

where the completion on the left-hand side is for the
(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})
-adic topology. In other words we can use the variables
X_{i}
defined in §
2.3
instead of the variables
Y_{\sigma_{i}}
to define the ring
A
. In particular, the perfectoid Tate algebra
A_{\infty}
in Lemma
2.4.2
is the completion of the perfection of
A
and the action of
\varphi
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
A_{\infty}
are compatible with the corresponding actions on
A
.

We will use the following result:

Proposition 2.6.3
.

For
R
a perfect
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra there is an equivalence of categories between the category of locally constant sheaves
L
of finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector spaces on
\Spec(R)_{\acute{e}t}
and the
category of pairs
(M,\phi)
where
M
is a finite projective
R
-module and
\phi
is an isomorphism

\varphi_{q}^{*}M\xrightarrow{\sim}M

(
where
\varphi_{q}(-)=(-)^{q}
)
. This equivalence is given by the two inverse functors
L\mapsto(\Gamma(L\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\mathcal{O}_{\Spec(R)}),\mathrm{Id}\otimes\varphi_{q})
and
(M,\phi)\mapsto(S\mapsto(M\otimes_{R}S)^{\phi\otimes\varphi_{q}=1}
,
S
étale
R
-algebra).

Proof.

This is (a trivial variant of)
[
KL15
, Prop. 3.2.7]
.
∎

We let
\displaystyle{A^{1/p^{\infty}}=\varinjlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}A}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\big(\mathbb{F}\llbracket X_{0}^{1/p^{n}},\dots,X_{f-1}^{1/p^{n}}\rrbracket_{(X_{0}\cdots X_{f-1})}\big)
be the perfection of the ring
A
. It is used in the next proof.

Theorem 2.6.4
.

The functor
D_{A}\mapsto A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}
induces an exact equivalence of categories
from the category of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A
to the
category of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Note first that
D_{A}\mapsto A^{1/p^{\infty}}\otimes_{A}D_{A}
induces an exact equivalence of categories
from the category of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A
to the
category of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
(use that any étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
comes by extension of scalars from an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A^{1/q^{N}}
for some
N\gg 0
and apply
\varphi_{q}^{N}
). Hence we can replace
A
by
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
. It follows from

[
SW20
, Thm. 7.4.8]
(more precisely the discussion following
loc. cit.
) that there is an equivalence of categories between the
category of finite étale
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
-algebras and the category
of finite étale
