A_{\infty}
-algebras. Hence, when
\mathbb{F}=\mathbb{F}_{q}
, the result follows from Proposition
2.6.3
applied to both
R=A^{1/p^{\infty}}
and
R=A_{\infty}
.
In general, let
A_{q}
be the ring of §
2.2
, i.e.
A_{q}
is
A
but with
\mathbb{F}_{q}
instead of
\mathbb{F}
,
A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}
its perfection and
A_{q,\infty}
the completion of
A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}
. Then one can see an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
(resp.
A_{\infty}
) as an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}
(resp.
A_{q,\infty}
) together with the structure of an
\mathbb{F}
-vector space compatible with the action of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(seen in
\mathbb{F}
via
\sigma_{0}
). We only prove essential surjectivity (full faithfulness being easy). Let
D_{A_{\infty}}
be an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\infty}
. By the equivalence of categories for
\mathbb{F}=\mathbb{F}_{q}
, there is an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}
over
A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}
, which is also an
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}=A^{1/p^{\infty}}
-module, such that

A_{q,\infty}\otimes_{A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}}D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}\cong(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}}D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}=A_{\infty}\otimes_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A_{\infty}}.

We need to prove that
D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}
is finite projective over
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
(or equivalently free by faithfully flat descent with Remark
2.6.2
). The following argument is due to Ch. Du. Let
0\rightarrow N^{\prime}\rightarrow N\rightarrow N^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0
be a short exact sequence of
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
-modules. Since
D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}
is free over
A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}
there is a short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\Hom_{A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}},N^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow\Hom_{A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}},N)\rightarrow\Hom_{A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}},N^{\prime})\rightarrow 0
. Making
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
act on
\Hom_{A_{q}^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}},(-))
by
(\lambda f)(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\lambda f(\lambda^{-1}x)
and taking
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
-invariants we deduce a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\Hom_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{\!A^{1/p^{\infty}}},N^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow\Hom_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{\!A^{1/p^{\infty}}},N)\rightarrow\Hom_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}(D_{\!A^{1/p^{\infty}}},N^{\prime})\rightarrow 0.

Hence
D_{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}
is finite projective over
A^{1/p^{\infty}}
.
∎

Remark 2.6.5
.

We thank Laurent Berger for a discussion around Theorem
2.6.4
, and Laurent Fargues for suggesting to use Proposition
2.6.3
in its proof. Note that one can characterize the subspace
\displaystyle{A^{1/p^{\infty}}}\otimes_{A}D_{A}
of an étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
D_{A_{\infty}}
over
A_{\infty}
as the
A
-submodule of
D_{A_{\infty}}
of elements
d\in D_{A_{\infty}}
such that
\sum_{n\geq 0}A\varphi_{q}^{n}(d)
is a finite type
A
-module.

Corollary 2.6.6
.

The functor
D_{A}\mapsto A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}
induces a rank-preserving
\otimes
-equivalence between the category of étale

(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
(
resp. étale

(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
)
over
A
and the
category of étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules

(
resp. étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
)

over
A_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Let
D_{A_{\infty}}
be an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
. Any
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
gives an isomorphism of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules
\id\otimes a:a^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A_{\infty}}
which canonically descends to an isomorphism of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules
a^{*}D_{A}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A}
by Theorem
2.6.4
. Now let
D_{A_{\infty}}
be an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
, then replacing
\varphi
by
\varphi_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi^{f}
, it is also an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
. Let
\varphi^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}A_{\infty}\otimes_{\varphi,A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}
, then
\id\otimes\varphi
induces an isomorphism of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules
\varphi^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A_{\infty}}
which canonically descends to an isomorphism
\varphi^{*}D_{A}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A}
by Theorem
2.6.4
, giving the endomorphism
\varphi
on
D_{A}
. The action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
canonically descends too by the first case of the proof and commutes with
\varphi
(using Theorem
2.6.4
again). The rest of the statement is easy and left to the reader.
∎

From (
48
), (
49
) and Corollary
2.6.6
, we deduce:

Corollary 2.6.7
.

For
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
there is a covariant exact
\mathbb{F}
-linear
functor
\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
compatible with tensor
products from
\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
to étale

(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
and an isomorphism

A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{(i)}(-)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(-)
between
functors from
\Rep_{\mathbb{F}}{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)
to the category of étale

(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A_{\infty}
. These functors are related by functorial
A
-linear isomorphisms
\phi_{i}:A\otimes_{\varphi,A}D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A}^{(i+1)}(\overline{\rho})
which commute with
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
and are such that
\phi_{f-1}\circ\phi_{f-2}\circ\cdots\circ\phi_{0}:A\otimes_{\varphi^{f},A}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
is
\id\otimes\varphi_{q}
.

Remark 2.6.8
.

One can check that
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\times D_{A}^{(f-1)}(\overline{\rho})\times D_{A}^{(f-2)}(\overline{\rho})\times\cdots\times D_{A}^{(1)}(\overline{\rho})
can be given the structure of an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A_{q}
in the sense of §
2.2
.

2.7
The
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
associated to a Galois representation

To an arbitrary
\overline{\rho}
we associate an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
(which will be particularly important when
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}=2
).

Keep the notation of §
2.6
and let

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
be
the
f
-th “exterior tensor product” of
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
on

Z_{\mathrm{LT}}\!=\!(\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket))^{f},

where

\mathrm{pr}_{i}:(\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket))^{\!f}\twoheadrightarrow\Spa(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu),\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket)

is the
i
-th projection (so
\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
is the sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}
of §
2.6
). As

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
is a continuous
K^{\times}
-equivariant vector bundle,

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}
is naturally a
continuous
(K^{\times})^{f}
-equivariant vector bundle. We promote it to a
(continuous)
(K^{\times})^{f}\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}
-equivariant vector bundle using
the commutativity of the tensor product (where
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
):

c_{\sigma}:\sigma^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}=\sigma^{*}\Big(\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}\Big)\cong\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\sigma^{*}\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}\cong\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\mathrm{pr}_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sigma\atop\sim}}\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}=\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}.

We define

D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
as the
A_{\infty}
-module with a
continuous semi-linear action of
K^{\times}
obtained as the global
sections of the continuous
K^{\times}
-equivariant vector bundle on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}

corresponding to
\mathcal{V}^{\boxtimes f}|_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}}
, more concretely (see §
2.5
):

D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\Gamma\big(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}},(m_{*}(\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}|_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}}}))^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}\big)=\Gamma(Z_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\mathrm{gen}},\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f})^{\Delta\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{f}}.

This is an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\infty}
(recall
\varphi
is bijective).

Using Corollary
2.5.2
and §
2.6
, we can give a more explicit description of
D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
. Note that we have:

D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})=\Gamma(U_{\underline{n}_{0}},\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}|_{U_{\underline{n}_{0}}})^{\Delta_{1}}

and that the vector bundle
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}
is isomorphic to the tensor
product

\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(0)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(f-1)}.

As the equivalence with vector bundles on
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
, i.e. finite free

A_{\infty}
-modules, is compatible with tensor products (see §
2.5
), we deduce an isomorphism of

A_{\infty}
-modules

D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\cong(A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{0}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}))^{\Delta_{1}}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}\cdots\otimes_{A_{\infty}}(A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{f-1}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}))^{\Delta_{1}}.

Lemma 2.7.1
.

There is a functorial isomorphism of étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over

A_{\infty}

D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho}),

where the automorphism
\varphi
on the right-hand side is given by
(
see (
49
) for
\phi_{i}
)

\varphi(v_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{f-1})=\phi_{f-1}(v_{f-1})\otimes\phi_{0}(v_{0})\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{f-2}(v_{f-2})

(
and the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
is as defined in §
2.6
on each factor
D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
)
.

Proof.

Recall that
\delta\in\mathfrak{S}_{f}
sends
i
to
i+1
. Let

\alpha_{i}:(\delta^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i-1)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}

be the tautological isomorphism deduced from the identifications

(\delta^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i-1)}=(\delta^{-1})^{*}\mathrm{pr}_{i-1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}\cong(\mathrm{pr}_{i-1}\circ\delta^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}=\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}=\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}.

Recall that
p_{\delta^{-1}}\in(K^{\times})^{f}
is defined in §
2.5
and let

\beta_{i}:p_{\delta^{-1}}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}

be the isomorphism of sheaves on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
defined by (
f\in\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\mathrm{LT}}}
,
v\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}
and compare with (
47
)):

f\otimes v\longmapsto\begin{cases}f(p_{\delta^{-1}}(-))\otimes v&\text{if }i\neq f-1\\
f(p_{\delta^{-1}}(-))\otimes\varphi_{q}(v)&\text{if }i=f-1.\end{cases}

We obtain isomorphisms of sheaves on
Z_{\mathrm{LT}}
for
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}

\alpha_{i}\circ(\delta^{-1})^{*}(\beta_{i-1}):\varphi^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i-1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\cong}\limits^{(\ref{frob'})}}((\delta^{-1})^{*}\circ p_{\delta^{-1}}^{*})\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i-1)}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\delta^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i-1)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}.

The isomorphism
c_{p_{\delta^{-1}}\circ\delta^{-1}}:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}
(with the notation as at the beginning of §
2.5
) is easily checked to decompose as a tensor product

\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\big(\alpha_{i}\circ(\delta^{-1})^{*}(\beta_{i})\big):\varphi^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{\boxtimes f}\cong\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\varphi^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i-1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}\mathcal{V}_{\overline{\rho}}^{(i)}.

Taking global sections on
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}
and
\Delta_{1}
-invariants, we obtain the desired formula.
∎

From Lemma
2.7.1
and Corollary
2.6.6
we deduce
D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\cong A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
for a unique étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
over
A
such that

D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\cong\bigotimes_{i=0}^{f-1}D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})

(51)

with the same
\varphi
and action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on the right-hand side as in Lemma
2.7.1
.

Remark 2.7.2
.

Note that, for
0\leq i<f-1
, the isomorphism
\phi_{i}
in (
49
) is induced by the natural
A_{\infty}
-linear isomorphism
\varphi^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i)}(-)\cong D_{A_{\infty}}^{(i+1)}(-)
, whereas
\phi_{f-1}
coincides with the

A_{\infty}
-linear isomorphism

\varphi^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(f-1)}(-)\cong\varphi^{*}((\varphi^{f-1})^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(-))=\varphi_{q}^{*}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(-)\longrightarrow D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(-)

induced by the
\varphi_{q}
-semi-linear
automorphism
\varphi_{q}
of
D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(-)
. Therefore the
isomorphism class of the
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module

D_{A_{\infty}}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
(equivalently of
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
) is completely characterized by the
isomorphism class of the
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module

D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
(equivalently of
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
).

2.8
Relation to classical
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

We show that the étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
is related in a simple way to the (usual) étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module
D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
of §
2.1
and derive some consequences.

As in
[
BHH+
, §3.1.3]
, let
\tr:A\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
be the ring surjection induced by the trace
\tr:\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathcal{O}_{K}\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rrbracket\cong\mathbb{F}\llbracket T\rrbracket
. Since the map
\tr
commutes with
\varphi
(hence
\varphi_{q}
) and the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, we deduce that
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
is an étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module.

Proposition 2.8.1
.

There is
d\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
such that we have a functorial isomorphism of
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-modules

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\cong D_{\sigma_{d}}(\overline{\rho}),

where
D_{\sigma_{d}}(\overline{\rho})
is as above (
9
) choosing the embedding
\sigma_{d}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
instead of
\sigma_{0}
.

Proof.

The trace
\tr:\mathbb{F}\llbracket K\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathbb{F}\llbracket\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rrbracket\cong\mathbb{F}\llbracket T^{p^{-\infty}}\rrbracket
induces a ring surjection
\tr:A_{\infty}\!\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
commuting (in an obvious way) with
\tr:A\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
. Using Corollary
2.6.6
it is enough to prove
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\cong\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{d}}(\overline{\rho})
.

For any perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra
R
we have a commutative diagram

(53)

where the top horizontal map is

on the coordinates and the right vertical map is the map

in (

). From the discussion above, the map

, where

), (

), (

) and (

deduced from the trace

. Hence we deduce from (

) a commutative diagram of perfectoid rings over

:

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

(54)

where the top horizontal surjection sends
T_{K,i}^{p^{-n}}
to
T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{i-n}}
for
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
. The right vertical injection commutes with
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
(acting on
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
via the norm
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
), hence we deduce from Theorem
2.1.1
(and (
5
)) that it induces an injection of perfectoid fields
\iota:\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)^{\Gal(K_{\infty}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))}\cong\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)
.
Since
\iota
commutes with the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, one easily checks that it must be an isomorphism, as any continuous
\mathbb{F}
-algebra homomorphism
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mkern-3.0mu)\rightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mkern-3.0mu)
commuting with the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
sends
[1]\in\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mkern-3.0mu)
to
[\lambda]\in\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mkern-3.0mu)
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
by
[
BR22
, Thm. 3.1]
(and continuity).

Now let
\overline{\rho}
be a continuous representation of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
on a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}
-vector space, using the isomorphism
A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{0}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\cong A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
from Theorem
2.5.1
we deduce from (
54
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A^{\prime}_{\infty}}\big(A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{0}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A^{\prime}_{\infty}}\big(A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\iota,\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)}\big(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\big).

By Proposition
2.1.2
we also have

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\cong\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)}\big(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\big)\\
\cong\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\iota,\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)}\big(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\iota^{-1},\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)}(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho}))\big).

Since the action of
\Gal(K_{\infty}/K(\!\sqrt[p^{\infty}]{1}))\cong\Gal(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)/\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu))
is trivial on both
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
and
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
, we deduce by Galois descent an isomorphism of
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-modules over
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)

\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A_{\infty}}D_{A_{\infty}}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\!\cong\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\iota^{-1},\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)}(\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T^{p^{-\infty}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})).

This easily gives the statement, using that all the above isomorphisms are functorial in
\overline{\rho}
(note that, if
\iota
is given by
[1]\mapsto[\lambda]
as above, then
d
is the unique integer in
\{0,\dots,f-1\}
congruent to
\mathrm{val}(\lambda)
modulo
f
).
∎

Remark 2.8.2
.

Using Theorem
2.9.4
below together with Lemma
2.1.5
and
[
Bre11
, Prop. 3.5]
, one can compute that
d=f-1
. We won’t need this fact.

We can also consider the tensor product
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
for
\tr:A\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)
. It is obviously an étale
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module.

Corollary 2.8.3
.

The
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
is the
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module of the tensor induction
\ind_{K}^{\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\!\overline{\rho}
.

Proof.

This easily follows from (
51
), Proposition
2.8.1
, Corollary
2.6.7
and the “tensor product version” of
[
Bre11
, Lemma 3.6]
(which we leave to the reader).
∎

Proposition
2.8.1
also enables to prove the following full faithfulness statement.

Corollary 2.8.4
.

For
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}
the functor
\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
from continuous representations of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
on finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}
-vector spaces to étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A
is exact and fully faithful.

Proof.

By Corollary
2.6.7
it is enough to prove the full faithfulness for
i=0
. We have morphisms:

\Hom_{{\Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/K)}(\overline{\rho},\overline{\rho}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Hom_{(\varphi_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})}(D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}),D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime}))\longrightarrow\Hom_{(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})}(D_{\sigma_{d}}(\overline{\rho}),D_{\sigma_{d}}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime}))

​​where we use Proposition
2.8.1
for the second. By the theory of
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-modules (see e.g. §
2.1
), we know that the composition of the two morphisms is bijective. Hence the first morphism is injective. It is enough to prove that the second morphism is also injective. Let
f:D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})\rightarrow D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})
mapping to
0
, i.e.
f(D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}))\subseteq{\mathfrak{p}}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})
, where
{\mathfrak{p}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Ker}(\tr:A\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu))
(a maximal ideal of the noetherian domain
A
). Using the fact that
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
is étale and that
f
commutes with
\varphi_{q}
, we derive
f(D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}))\subseteq\varphi_{q}^{n}({\mathfrak{p}})D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})
for any
n\geq 0
. For those
n
such that
x\mapsto x^{q^{n}}
is
\mathbb{F}
-linear, the map
\varphi_{q}^{n}
on
A
is just
x\mapsto x^{q^{n}}
, hence
\varphi_{q}^{n}({\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq{\mathfrak{p}}^{q^{n}}
for those
n
, and thus
f(D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}))\subseteq(\bigcap_{m\geq 0}{\mathfrak{p}}^{m})D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})=0
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.8.5
.

(i)

We do not expect the functor
\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})
to be essentially surjective (for any
i
). It is probably an interesting question to characterize its essential image.

(ii)

It is
not true
that the functor
\overline{\rho}\mapsto D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
is fully faithful, as in general the
isomorphism class of the
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
does not determine the one of the Galois
representation
\overline{\rho}
. For instance, one can check by an explicit computation using Theorem
2.9.4
below that,
if
f=2
and
\overline{\rho}\cong(\ind\omega_{4}^{h})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda)
is irreducible,
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
only
sees
\lambda^{4}
, i.e. does not distinguish
\overline{\rho}
and
(\ind\omega_{4}^{h})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda^{\prime})
, where

\lambda^{\prime 2}=-\lambda^{2}
. However, one can also check (again using Theorem
2.9.4
) that, at least when
\overline{\rho}

is
2
-dimensional and semi-simple,
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
determines
\overline{\rho}
if
\overline{\rho}
is split or if

{\det}(\overline{\rho})(p)=1
.

2.9
An explicit computation in the semi-simple case

When
\overline{\rho}
is semi-simple we show that the explicit étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
defined in §
2.2
is isomorphic to the
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
defined in §
2.6
.

It follows from (
50
) that
for all
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
and
0\leq i\leq f-1
, we
have (using as usual
\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
)
a(X_{i})=\overline{a}^{p^{i}}X_{i}
modulo terms of degree

\geq 2
. Therefore we have the following result:

Lemma 2.9.1
.

For
0\leq i\leq f-1
, we have

a(X_{i})\in\overline{a}^{p^{i}}X_{i}(1+A^{\circ\circ})
.

We define

f^{X}_{a,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\frac{\overline{a}X_{0}}{a(X_{0})}\in 1+F_{-1}A=1+A^{\circ\circ}\subseteq 1+A_{\infty}^{\circ\circ}

(note that by (
50
)
f^{X}_{a,0}
in fact coincides with
f_{a,\sigma_{0}}
in (
21
) up to a factor in
1+F_{-2}A
).

Lemma 2.9.2
.

There exists
u\in\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{(1+p\mathcal{O}_{K})^{f}\cap\Delta_{1}}
such that

u^{q-1}=\frac{X_{f-1}^{p}}{X_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{(\ref{phionainfini})}}\frac{\varphi(X_{0})}{X_{0}}\in A\subseteq A_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{\Delta_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{(1+p\mathcal{O}_{K})^{f}\cap\Delta_{1}}.

Moreover we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\forall\ \underline{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in\Delta_{1}&\quad\underline{a}(u)=\overline{a_{0}}u\\
\forall\ a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}&\quad(a,1,\dots,1)(u)=\overline{a}\left(\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}u\end{array}\right.

noting that
\left(\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}
is well-defined in
1+F_{-1}A\subseteq 1+A_{\infty}^{\circ\circ}
since
\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\in 1+F_{-1}A
.

Proof.

Let
\lvert\cdot\rvert
be a multiplicative norm on
A^{\prime}_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})
such that

\lvert T_{K,i}\rvert=\lvert T_{K,0}^{p^{i}}\rvert=p^{-p^{i}}
for
0\leq i\leq f-1
whose existence comes
from Lemma
2.4.7
(iii)&(i). Let
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{1}
be the associated norm on

\mathbf{B}^{+}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})
defined in Remark
2.3.4
. As
\lvert\cdot\rvert
is
multiplicative, the same proof as in
[
FF18
, Prop. 1.4.9]
shows
that
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{1}
is multiplicative.

By definition of the map
m_{A^{\prime}_{\infty}}
in (
39
), we have the relation in
\mathbf{B}^{+}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})

\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}\left(\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[T_{K,i}^{q^{-n}}]p^{n}\right)=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}[X_{i}^{p^{-nf-i}}]p^{nf+i}=F(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1}).

(55)

For
c\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
let
\mathfrak{p}_{c}
be the ideal of
\mathbf{B}^{+}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})

\mathfrak{p}_{c}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\{x\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(A^{\prime}_{\infty}),\ \lvert x\rvert_{1}<p^{-c}\}\subseteq\mathbf{B}^{+}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})

(note that it is an ideal as
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{1}
is multiplicative and with values in
[0,1]\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
).
Let
c=1+p+\cdots+\cdots+p^{f-1}
. As
\lvert T_{K,i}^{q^{n}}\rvert=p^{-p^{i}q^{n}}\leq p^{-q^{n}}<p^{-c}

for
n\geq 1
, we have
\left\lvert\sum_{n\leq-1}[T_{K,i}^{q^{-n}}]p^{n}\right\rvert_{1}\leq p^{-q}<p^{-c}
, see Remark

2.3.4
, hence we obtain from (
55
)

\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}[T_{K,i}^{q^{-n}}]p^{n}\right)-F(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})\in\mathfrak{p}_{c}

and we deduce from Lemma
2.9.3
below applied to the element

x\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}(\sum_{n\geq 0}[T_{K,i}^{q^{-n}}]p^{n})-F(X_{0},\dots,X_{f-1})=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[x_{n}]p^{n}\in\mathbf{B}^{+}(A^{\prime}_{\infty})

(where
\sum_{n\geq 0}[x_{n}]p^{n}=\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}(\sum_{n\geq 0}[T_{K,i}^{q^{-n}}]p^{n})-\sum_{n\geq 0}\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}[X_{i}^{p^{-nf-i}}]p^{nf+i}
and where
\sum_{n<0}[x_{n}]p^{n}=-\sum_{n<0}\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}[X_{i}^{p^{-nf-i}}]p^{nf+i}
) that we have

\sum_{n\geq 0}[x_{n}]p^{n}=\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}[T_{K,i}^{q^{-n}}]p^{n}\right)-\sum_{n\geq 0}\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}[X_{i}^{p^{-nf-i}}]p^{nf+i}\in\mathfrak{p}_{c}.

Note that the left-hand side is now in
W((A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ})
.
As a consequence, we have

\lvert x_{0}\rvert=\lvert T_{K,0}\cdots T_{K,f-1}-X_{0}\rvert<p^{-c}

so that we can write in
(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ}

X_{0}=T_{K,0}\cdots T_{K,f-1}(1+w_{0})

(56)

with
\lvert w_{0}\rvert<p^{-c+(1+p+\cdots+p^{f-1})}=1
,
i.e.
w_{0}\in(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}
. Applying the automorphism
\varphi
of
A^{\prime}_{\infty}
to (
56
) and since
\varphi
respects
(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ}
and
(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}
(as it is continuous) we obtain in
(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ}

X_{f-1}^{p}=T_{K,1}T_{K,2}\cdots T_{K,f-1}T_{K,0}^{q}(1+w_{1})

with
w_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varphi(w_{0})\in(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}
. We deduce the equality

X_{f-1}^{p}X_{0}^{-1}\in T_{K,0}^{q-1}(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}).

Using that
x\mapsto x^{q-1}
is bijective on
1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}
, we see that there exists a unique
u\in T_{K,0}(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ})
such that
u^{q-1}=X_{f-1}^{p}X_{0}^{-1}
.

As
\Delta_{1}
acts trivially on
A_{\infty}
, we have

\underline{a}(u)^{q-1}=u^{q-1}
for all

\underline{a}\in\Delta_{1}
. Therefore there exists a character

\chi
of
\Delta_{1}
with values in
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hookrightarrow}\limits^{\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{F}
such that

\forall\ \underline{a}\in\Delta_{1},\quad\underline{a}(u)=\chi(\underline{a})u.

Writing
u=T_{K,0}(1+w)
with
w\in(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}
, this gives

\overline{a_{0}}T_{K,0}f_{a_{0}}^{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,0})^{-1}(1+\underline{a}(w))=\chi(\underline{a})u
, where
f_{a}^{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,0})=\overline{a}T_{K,0}(a_{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,0}))^{-1}\in 1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ}
. As
u\in T_{K,0}(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ})
this implies

\chi(\underline{a})\overline{a_{0}}^{-1}\in(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ})\cap\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}=\{1\}

which proves

\chi(\underline{a})=\overline{a_{0}}
.

For the last relation, we have

\left((a,1,\dots,1)(u)\right)^{q-1}=(a,1,\dots,1)(u^{q-1})=a(X_{f-1}^{p}X_{0}^{-1})=\frac{a(\varphi(X_{0}))}{a(X_{0})}=\frac{\varphi(a(X_{0}))}{a(X_{0})}\\
=\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\frac{\varphi(X_{0})}{X_{0}}=\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}u^{q-1}=\left(\left(\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}u\right)^{q-1}

so that as above there is a character
\chi:\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\subseteq\mathbb{F}^{\times}
such that
(a,1,\dots,1)(u)=\chi(a)\left(\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}u
. But
u\in T_{K,0}(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ})
implies
(a,1\dots,1)(u)\in\overline{a}T_{K,0}(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ})
so that
\chi(a)=\overline{a}
since
\left(\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\in 1+A_{\infty}^{\circ\circ}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 2.9.3
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid
\mathbb{F}
-algebra and let
(x_{n})_{\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
a
family of elements of
R^{\circ\circ}
such that the series

\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[x_{n}]p^{n}
converges to an element
x
in

\mathbf{B}^{+}(R)
. Assume that
\lvert x\rvert_{1}<c
for some
c\in[0,1[
. Then we have

\left\lvert\sum_{n<0}[x_{n}]p^{n}\right\rvert_{1}<c.

Proof.

Recall that
\lvert x\rvert_{1}=\displaystyle{\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho<1\\
\rho\rightarrow 1\end{subarray}}\lvert x\rvert_{\rho}}
(see the reference in Remark
2.3.4
). Therefore we can find

0<\rho<1
such that
\lvert x\rvert_{\rho}<c
. This implies

\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\{\lvert x_{n}\rvert\rho^{n}\}<c
and thus for
n\leq-1
,

\lvert x_{n}\rvert\rho^{n}<c
which implies
\lvert x_{n}\rvert<c\rho<c
. The claim then follows from
Remark
2.3.4
applied to
c\rho
.
∎

Let
v\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}uT_{K,0}^{-1}
. We have

v\in 1+\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{\circ\circ}
from the proof of Lemma
2.9.2
, so that, for each

r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
(
={\mathbb{Z}}
localized at the prime ideal
(p)
), the element

v^{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\binom{r}{n}(v-1)^{n}\in 1+\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{\circ\circ}

exists. Writing
\underline{a}(v)=\underline{a}(u)a_{0}(T_{K,0})^{-1}
and using the formula for
\underline{a}(u)
in Lemma
2.9.2
and the fact that
f_{a_{0}}^{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,0})\in 1+\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{\circ\circ}
, we have

\forall\ \underline{a}\in\Delta_{1}\ \forall\ r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)},\quad\underline{a}(v^{r})=f_{a_{0}}^{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,0})^{r}v^{r}.

(57)

We also have
\varphi^{f}(v)/v^{q}\in 1+\mathcal{O}(U_{\underline{n}_{0}})^{\circ\circ}
and

(\varphi^{f}(v)/v^{q})^{q-1}=\varphi^{f}(u^{q-1})/u^{q(q-1)}=1
as

u^{q-1}=\varphi(X_{0})/X_{0}
. It follows that
\varphi^{f}(v)=v^{q}
and

\varphi^{f}(u)=u^{q}.

(58)

Now, let
\overline{\rho}
be an absolutely irreducible continuous representation of

\Gal(\overline{K}/K)
on a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}
-vector space and choose a basis
(e_{0},\dots,e_{d-1})
of the
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)
-module

D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
as in (
15
). We consider the associated étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})=A\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
defined in Lemma
2.2.2
,
where
A
has the structure of
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)
-algebra given by (
19
).

Theorem 2.9.4
.

Assume that
\overline{\rho}
is absolutely irreducible. The
étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
in Corollary
2.6.7
is
isomorphic to
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
.

Proof.

First, replacing the variable
Y_{\sigma_{0}}
by the variable
X_{0}

in Lemma
2.2.2
and using (
50
), it is easily
checked that one obtains an isomorphic étale

(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module. By Corollary
2.6.6
, it
is enough to prove the statement of the theorem after extending
scalars everywhere from
A
to
A_{\infty}
. Recall we have

D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\!=\!\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}

with basis
(1\otimes e_{i})_{0\leq i\leq d-1}
as in Lemma
2.1.5
,
let
u\in T_{K,0}(1+(A^{\prime}_{\infty})^{\circ\circ})
be as in Lemma

2.9.2
and let again
v=uT_{K,0}^{-1}
. Then using (
48
), (
57
) and the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
in (
15
) we obtain

A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})=(A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{0}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]})^{\Delta_{1}}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d-1}A_{\infty}v^{-\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}(1\otimes e_{i}).

Moreover it follows again from the last equality in Lemma
2.9.2
that we have in
A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{0}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
for
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}

a(v^{-\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}(1\otimes e_{i}))=\left(\left(\frac{f^{X}_{a,0}}{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}\right)^{\!\!\frac{1}{q-1}}\!\frac{\overline{a}T_{K,0}}{a(T_{K,0})}v\right)^{\!\!\!\!-\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!f_{a}^{\mathrm{LT}}(T_{K,0})^{\!\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\!(1\otimes e_{i})\\
=\left(\frac{\varphi(f^{X}_{a,0})}{f^{X}_{a,0}}\right)^{\frac{hq^{i}}{q^{d}-1}}v^{-\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}(1\otimes e_{i}).

We define an
A_{\infty}
-linear isomorphism
A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})=A_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}A_{\infty}\otimes_{A}D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
by

1\otimes e_{i}\mapsto v^{-\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes e_{i}
for
i\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
. This
isomorphism commutes with the actions of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on both
sides by the above computation (together with Lemma
2.2.2
). It also commutes with
\varphi_{q}
, namely we have in

A^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu),\iota_{0}}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
(using (
58
)):

\varphi_{q}\big(v^{-\frac{hq^{i}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes e_{i}\big)=v^{-\frac{hq^{i+1}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes e_{i+1}\ \ {\rm for}\ \ i<d-1

and (using the formula for
u^{q-1}
in Lemma
2.9.2
)

\displaystyle\varphi_{q}\big(v^{-\frac{hq^{d-1}(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes e_{d-1}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v^{-h(q-1)}v^{-\frac{h(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes\lambda^{d}T_{K}^{-h(q-1)}e_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{-h(q-1)}\lambda^{d}\big(v^{-\frac{h(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes e_{0}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda^{d}\left(\frac{\varphi(X_{0})}{X_{0}}\right)^{-h}\big(v^{-\frac{h(q-1)}{q^{d}-1}}\otimes e_{0}\big).\qed

Remark 2.9.5
.

Theorem
2.9.4
shows that, when
\overline{\rho}
is a direct sum of absolutely irreducible representations, one can obtain the étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
D_{A}^{(0)}(\overline{\rho})
from the Lubin–Tate
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})=\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}})\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T_{K,\sigma_{0}}^{q-1})\mkern-3.0mu)}D_{K,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})^{[\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}]}
by the simple recipe (
19
). However, we do not expect this recipe to work in general when
\overline{\rho}
is not semi-simple.

Define
D_{A,\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
as
D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})
(see §
2.2
) but using the embedding
\sigma
instead of
\sigma_{0}
. From §
2.2
one easily checks that there are canonical
A
-linear isomorphisms for
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}

\Id\otimes\varphi:A\otimes_{\varphi,A}D_{A,\sigma_{i}}(\overline{\rho})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\longrightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}D_{A,\sigma_{i-1}}(\overline{\rho})

(59)

which commute with
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
and
\varphi_{q}
on both sides. Comparing the isomorphism
\phi_{i}
in Corollary
2.6.7
with the isomorphism (
59
) we see that we have for
i\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}

D_{A}^{(i)}(\overline{\rho})\cong D_{A,\sigma_{f-i}}(\overline{\rho}).

(60)

Using (
60
) and (
51
) we have therefore

D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})\cong D_{A,\sigma_{0}}(\overline{\rho})\otimes_{A}D_{A,\sigma_{1}}(\overline{\rho})\otimes_{A}\cdots\otimes_{A}D_{A,\sigma_{f-1}}(\overline{\rho}).

(61)

When
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{\rho}=1
, i.e. for
\chi:\Gal(\overline{K}/K)\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
a continuous character, we will need in §
3
the (very simple) description of
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\chi)
.

Lemma 2.9.6
.

Viewing
\chi
as a character of
K^{\times}
via the local reciprocity map, we have
(
for
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
)
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cll}D_{A}^{\otimes}(\chi)&=&AF_{\chi}\\
\varphi(F_{\chi})&=&\chi(p)F_{\chi}\\
a(F_{\chi})&=&\chi(a)F_{\chi}.\end{array}\right.

In particular,
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho}\otimes\chi)
equals
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
, but with the action of
\varphi
multiplied by
\chi(p)
and the action of
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
multiplied by
\chi(a)
.

Proof.

By (
59
) and (
61
) replacing
\overline{\rho}
by
\chi
we can describe
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\chi)
as
AE_{\chi}
, where
E_{\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}e_{\chi}\otimes\varphi(e_{\chi})\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi^{f-1}(e_{\chi})
with
\varphi^{j}(e_{\chi})\in D_{A,{\sigma_{f-j}}}(\chi)
(noting
e_{\chi}
instead of
1\otimes e_{\chi}
). Write
\chi=\omega_{f}^{h_{\chi}}{\rm unr}(\lambda_{\chi})
for
h_{\chi}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
\lambda_{\chi}\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
. Set
F_{\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{h_{\chi}}E_{\chi}
, then one computes:

\varphi(F_{\chi})=\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{h_{\chi}}\varphi(E_{\chi})=\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{h_{\chi}}\varphi^{f}(e_{\chi})\otimes\varphi(e_{\chi})\otimes\cdots\otimes\varphi^{f-1}(e_{\chi})\\
=\lambda_{\chi}\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})^{h_{\chi}}\Big(\frac{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{\varphi(Y_{\sigma_{0}})}\Big)^{h_{\chi}}E_{\chi}=\lambda_{\chi}F_{\chi}=\chi(p)F_{\chi}

where the third equality follows from (
24
). An analogous computation using
a(Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{h_{\chi}})=\sigma(\overline{a})^{h_{\chi}}Y_{\sigma_{0}}^{h_{\chi}}f_{a,\sigma_{0}}^{-{h_{\chi}}}
and
a(\varphi^{j}(e_{\chi}))=\big(\frac{\varphi^{j}(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}{\varphi^{j+1}(f_{a,\sigma_{0}})}\big)^{\frac{h_{\chi}}{1-q}}\varphi^{j}(e_{\chi})
(see again (
24
)) gives
a(F_{\chi})=\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})^{h_{\chi}}F_{\chi}
. But
\sigma_{0}(\overline{a})^{h_{\chi}}=\chi(a)
(see (
14
)). The rest of the statement follows from the discussion after (
24
).
∎

3
Étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules and modular representations of
\GL_{2}

In this section we prove that the étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}(\pi)
over
A
associated in
[
BHH+
, §3]
to certain automorphic admissible smooth representations
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(K)
over
\mathbb{F}
is isomorphic to (a certain twist of) the étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho})
of §
2
, where
\overline{\rho}
is the underlying
2
-dimensional representation of
{\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
over
\mathbb{F}
, which is assumed semi-simple and sufficiently generic. We conjecture that an analogous statement holds without these assumptions and for any automorphic admissible smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(K)
over
\mathbb{F}
.

We let
I\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}&{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\\
{p\mathcal{O}_{K}}&{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
be the Iwahori subgroup of
\GL_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
K_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{1+p\mathcal{O}_{K}}&{p\mathcal{O}_{K}}\\
{p\mathcal{O}_{K}}&{1+p\mathcal{O}_{K}}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
the first congruence subgroup,
I_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{1+p\mathcal{O}_{K}}&{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\\
{p\mathcal{O}_{K}}&{1+p\mathcal{O}_{K}}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
the pro-
p
radical of
I
and
Z_{1}
the center of
I_{1}
. We recall from §
2.2
that
N_{0}=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{1}&{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\\
{0}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\subseteq I_{1}
. If
C
is a pro-
p
group we denote by
\mathbb{F}\llbracket C\rrbracket
its Iwasawa algebra over
\mathbb{F}
(a local ring), and
\mathfrak{m}_{C}
the maximal ideal of
\mathbb{F}\llbracket C\rrbracket
. If
M
is a filtered module in the sense of
[
LvO96
, §I.2]
with
(F_{n}M)_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
its ascending filtration, we define
\gr(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\bigoplus_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F_{n}M/F_{n-1}M
. When
R=\mathbb{F}\llbracket C\rrbracket
and
M
is an
R
-module, the filtration
F_{n}M=\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{-n}M
if
n\leq 0
and
F_{n}M=M
if
n\geq 0
is called the
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
-adic filtration on
M
.

3.1
A local-global compatibility conjecture for
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A

We conjecture that any automorphic smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(K)
over
\mathbb{F}
gives rise to an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
which is (up to twist) a direct sum of copies of the module
D_{A}^{\otimes}
in §
2.7
of the corresponding local Galois representation at
p
. We state our main results.

First, we quickly review the construction of the
A
-module
D_{A}(\pi)
associated to certain smooth representations
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(K)
over
\mathbb{F}
in
[
BHH+
, §3.1]
.

Let
\pi
be an admissible smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(K)
over
\mathbb{F}
with a central character and endow the
\mathbb{F}
-linear dual
\pi^{\vee}
with the
\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}
-adic filtration, or equivalently the
\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}/Z_{1}}
-adic filtration (which, in general,
strictly
contains the
\mathfrak{m}_{N_{0}}
-adic filtration). We endow

(\pi^{\vee})_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket}\pi^{\vee}

with the tensor product filtration (where the localization
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})}
is endowed with the
filtration described by (
16
), replacing
\mathbb{F}_{q}
by
\mathbb{F}
)
and define
D_{A}(\pi)
as the completion of
(\pi^{\vee})_{(Y_{\sigma_{0}}\cdots Y_{\sigma_{f-1}})}
for this filtration (
[
LvO96
, §I.3.4]
). Then
D_{A}(\pi)
is a complete filtered
A
-module and the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\pi^{\vee}
extends by continuity to
D_{A}(\pi)
. Moreover the action
f\mapsto f\circ\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{p}&{0}\\
{0}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
on
\pi^{\vee}
gives rise to a continuous
A
-linear morphism (see
[
BHH+
, §3.1.2]
)

\beta:D_{A}(\pi)\longrightarrow A\otimes_{\varphi,A}D_{A}(\pi),

(62)

where
\varphi
on
A
is as in §
2.2
. We let
\mathcal{C}
be the abelian category of those
\pi
such that
\gr(D_{A}(\pi))
is a finitely generated
\gr(A)
-module. Then for any
\pi\in\mathcal{C}
, the
A
-module
D_{A}(\pi)
is finite free (see
[
BHH+
, Cor. 3.1.2.9]
and Remark
2.6.2
).

The following straightforward lemma will be used. For
\chi:K^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
a smooth character, denote by
D_{A}(\chi)
the rank
1
étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
defined by
Ae_{\chi}
with
\varphi(e_{\chi})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\chi(p)e_{\chi}
and
a(e_{\chi})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\chi(a)e_{\chi}
for
a\in{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
.
(Note that this is an ad hoc definition, as
D_{A}(\pi)=0
if
\pi=\chi\circ\det
.)

Lemma 3.1.1
.

Let
\chi:K^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
be a smooth character and
\pi
in the category
\mathcal{C}
, then
D_{A}(\pi\otimes\chi)\cong D_{A}(\pi)\otimes_{A}D_{A}(\chi^{-1})
with diagonal
\varphi
and action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
.

Proof.

This directly follows from the definitions of
D_{A}(\pi)
and of the actions of
\varphi
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times}
on
D_{A}(\pi)
.
∎

For
\pi
in
\mathcal{C}
, when
\beta
is moreover an isomorphism, its inverse
\beta^{-1}=\Id\otimes\varphi
makes
D_{A}(\pi)
an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module.

We now go to the global setting.

We fix a totally real number field
F
that is unramified at
p
. We fix a quaternion algebra
D
of center
F
which is split at all places above
p
and at not more than one infinite place. When
D
is split at one infinite place we say that we are in the
indefinite case
, and in the
definite case
otherwise. For a compact open subgroup
U=\prod U_{w}\subseteq(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty})^{\times}
we let
X_{U}
be the associated smooth projective algebraic Shimura curve over
F
(see
[
BHH+
, §8.1]
and the references therein for more details).

Fix an absolutely irreducible continuous representation
\overline{r}:{\Gal}(\overline{F}/F)\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
and for a finite place
w
of
F
we write
\overline{r}_{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\overline{r}|_{{\Gal}(\overline{F}_{w}/F_{w})}
. We let
S_{D}
be the set of finite places where
D
ramifies,
S_{\overline{r}}
the set of (finite) places where
\overline{r}
is ramified and
S_{p}
the set of (finite) places above
p
. Finally, we fix a place
v\in S_{p}
.
Let
\omega=\omega_{1}
denote the mod
p
cyclotomic character.

For any compact open subgroup
U^{v}=\prod_{w\neq v}U_{w}\subseteq(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty,v})^{\times}
we consider the following admissible smooth representation
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(F_{v})
over
\mathbb{F}
with central character
(\omega{\det}(\overline{r}_{v}))^{-1}
:

\pi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varinjlim_{U_{v}}\Hom_{{\Gal}(\overline{F}/F)}\big(\overline{r},H^{1}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(X_{U^{v}U_{v}}\times_{F}\overline{F},\mathbb{F})\big)

(63)

where the inductive limit runs over the compact open subgroups
U_{v}
of
(D\otimes_{F}F_{v})^{\times}\cong\GL_{2}(F_{v})
.
In the definite case, we replace
\Hom_{{\Gal}(\overline{F}/F)}(\overline{r},H^{1}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(X_{U}\times_{F}\overline{F},\mathbb{F}))
by the Hecke eigenspace
S(U,\mathbb{F})[{\mathfrak{m}}]\subseteq S(U,\mathbb{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\{f:D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty})^{\times}/U\rightarrow\mathbb{F}\}
associated to
\overline{r}
(see
[
BHH+
, §8.1]
) and define analogously

\pi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varinjlim_{U_{v}}S(U^{v}U_{v},\mathbb{F})[{\mathfrak{m}}].

(64)

We also need the “multiplicity
1
” variants of the representations
\pi
. For that, we need to assume that
p\geq 5
, that
\overline{r}|_{G_{F(\!\sqrt[p]{1})}}
is absolutely irreducible, that the image of
\overline{r}(G_{F(\!\sqrt[5]{1})})
in
{\rm PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F})
is not isomorphic to
A_{5}
, that
\overline{r}_{w}
for
w\in S_{p}
is generic in the sense of
[
BP12
, Def. 11.7]
(which implies
S_{p}\subseteq S_{\overline{r}}
) and that
\overline{r}_{w}
is non-scalar if
w\in S_{D}
. Under these assumptions, a so-called “local factor” is defined in
[
BD14
, §3.3]
(in the indefinite case and when
\overline{r}_{w}
is reducible for all
w\in S_{p}
) and in
[
EGS15
, §6.5]
(without these two conditions):

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\pi\!

\displaystyle\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\!\!

\displaystyle\Hom_{U^{v}}\!\!\Big(\overline{M}^{v},\Hom_{{\Gal}(\overline{F}/F)}\big(\overline{r},\varinjlim_{V}H^{1}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(X_{V}\times_{F}\overline{F},\mathbb{F})\big)\Big)[{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]\ \textrm{(indefinite case)}

(65)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\pi\!

\displaystyle\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\!\!

\displaystyle\Hom_{U^{v}}\!\!\Big(\overline{M}^{v},\varinjlim_{V}S(V,\mathbb{F})[{\mathfrak{m}}]\Big)[{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]\ \textrm{(definite case)}

(66)

where the inductive limits run over the compact open subgroups
V
of
(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty})^{\times}
, and where we refer to
loc. cit.
for the definitions of the compact open subgroup
U^{v}\subseteq(D\otimes_{F}{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\infty,v})^{\times}
, of the (finite-dimensional) irreducible smooth representation
\overline{M}^{v}
of
U^{v}
over
\mathbb{F}
and of the maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in a certain Hecke algebra.

Conjecture 3.1.2
.

Let
\pi
be as in (
63
), (
64
), (
65
) or (
66
) and assume
\pi\neq 0
. Then
\pi
is in the category
\mathcal{C}
,
\beta
in (
62
) is a bijection and we have an isomorphism of étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{r}_{v}(1))^{\oplus r}
for some integer
r\geq 1
which is equal to
1
when
\pi
is as in (
65
) or (
66
).

In the sequel, we prove Conjecture
3.1.2
for
\pi
as in (
65
) or (
66
) when
\overline{r}_{v}
is semi-simple and satisfies a strong genericity hypothesis (as defined below). We actually prove a purely local result for certain smooth representations
\pi
, that will ultimately include the representations in (
65
) and (
66
).

Let first
\overline{\rho}:\Gal(\overline{K}/K)\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\mathbb{F})
be a continuous representation satisfying the genericity assumption of
[
BP12
, Def. 11.7]
. Let
\pi
be a smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(K)
over
\mathbb{F}
satisfying the following two conditions:

(i)

there is an isomorphism of diagrams
(\pi^{I_{1}}\hookrightarrow\pi^{K_{1}})\cong D(\overline{\rho})^{\oplus r}
for some
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, where
D(\overline{\rho})
is a diagram associated to
\overline{\rho}
as in
[
BP12
]
or
[
BHH+
, §3.2.1]
with the constants
\nu_{\sigma}
for
\sigma\in W(\overline{\rho})
as in Remark
3.4.9
below;

(ii)

for any character
\chi:I\rightarrow\mathbb{F}^{\times}
appearing in
\pi[{\mathfrak{m}}_{I_{1}}]
there is an equality of multiplicities
[\pi[{\mathfrak{m}}^{3}_{I_{1}}]:\chi]=[\pi[{\mathfrak{m}}_{I_{1}}]:\chi]
.

We moreover assume that
\overline{\rho}
is of the following form
up to twist
:

\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}}\cong\begin{cases}\omega_{f}^{\sum_{j=0}^{f-1}(r_{j}+1)p^{j}}\oplus 1&\text{ if $\overline{\rho}$ is reducible}\\
\omega_{2f}^{\sum_{j=0}^{f-1}(r_{j}+1)p^{j}}\oplus\omega_{2f}^{\sum_{j=0}^{f-1}(r_{j}+1)p^{j+f}}&\text{ if $\overline{\rho}$ is irreducible}\end{cases}

(67)

where the integers
r_{i}
satisfy the following (strong) genericity condition:

\displaystyle\max\{12,2f-1\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!r_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!p-\max\{15,2f+2\}

\displaystyle\text{ if $j>0$ or $\overline{\rho}$ is reducible}

(68)

\displaystyle\max\{13,2f\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!r_{0}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!p-\max\{14,2f+1\}

\displaystyle\text{ if $\overline{\rho}$ is irreducible.}

The following is the main result of §
3
.

Theorem 3.1.3

(See §
3.9
)
.

Assume that
\overline{\rho}
and
\pi
are as above with moreover
(\pi^{I_{1}}\hookrightarrow\pi^{K_{1}})\!\cong D(\overline{\rho})
, i.e.
r=1
. Then
\pi
is in the category
\mathcal{C}
,
\beta
in (
62
) is a bijection and we have an isomorphism of étale
(\varphi,{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\times})
-modules
D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho}^{\vee}(1))
, where
\overline{\rho}^{\vee}(1)
is the Cartier dual of
\overline{\rho}
.

It implies the following special cases of Conjecture
3.1.2
.

Corollary 3.1.4
.

Let
\pi
be as in (
65
) or (
66
) and assume moreover that
\overline{r}_{v}
satisfies (
68
), (
67
), and that the framed deformation ring
R_{\overline{r}_{w}}
of
\overline{r}_{w}
over
W(\mathbb{F})
is formally smooth if
w\in(S_{D}\cup S_{\overline{r}})\setminus S_{p}
. Then Conjecture
3.1.2
is true for
\pi
.

Proof.

By
[
DL21
, Thm. 5.36]
(and the references therein)
\pi
satisfies condition (i) above with
\overline{\rho}=\overline{r}_{v}^{\vee}
and
r=1
. By
[
BHH+23
, Thm. 8.3.14]
,
[
BHH+23
, Thm. 1.5]
and
[
BHH+23
, Rem. 8.4.5]

\pi
satisfies condition (ii). Hence we can apply Theorem
3.1.3
.
∎

Remark 3.1.5
.

(i)

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.1.3
, we already know that
\pi
is in
\mathcal{C}
(see
[
BHH+
, Thm. 3.3.2.1]
) and that
\beta
is a bijection (see
[
BHH+
, Rem. 3.3.5.4(ii)]
noting that we do not need here the assumption (iii) in
[
BHH+
, §3.3.5]
). Hence we only need to prove
D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho}^{\vee}(1))
. In that direction, we already know the étale
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}(\pi)
. Indeed, it follows from
[
BHH+
, Cor. 3.3.2.4]
,
[
BHH+
, Thm. 3.1.3.7]
, Remark
2.6.2
– and some unravelling of the definition of the functor
V_{\GL_{2}}
of
[
BHH+
, §2.1.1]
using Lemma
3.1.1
and Lemma
2.9.6
– that
D_{A}(\pi)
is free of rank
2^{f}
and
\mathbb{F}(\mkern-3.0mu(T)\mkern-3.0mu)\otimes_{A}D_{A}(\pi)
is isomorphic to the
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module of the tensor induction
\ind_{K}^{\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\!(\overline{\rho}^{\vee}(1))
(compare with Corollary
2.8.3
).

(ii)

Under similar hypothesis but assuming that
\overline{\rho}
is reducible non-split with only one Serre weight, Conjecture
3.1.2
is proven in
[
Wana
]
(using the results of
[
Wanb
]
).

The rest of this paper is devoted to the proof of the isomorphism
D_{A}(\pi)\cong D_{A}^{\otimes}(\overline{\rho}^{\vee}(1))
in Theorem
3.1.3
and to the necessary material that needs to be introduced for that.

We fix
\overline{\rho}
and
\pi
as in Theorem
3.1.3
. Twisting both
\overline{\rho}
and
\pi
using Lemma
2.9.6
and Lemma
3.1.1
, we can and do assume
from now on

\overline{\rho}\cong(\ind\omega_{2f}^{h})\otimes{\rm unr}(\lambda)
or
\overline{\rho}\cong\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{f}^{h}{\rm unr}(\lambda_{0})&0\\
0&{\rm unr}(\lambda_{1})\end{pmatrix}
with
h=\sum_{j=0}^{f-1}p^{j}(r_{j}+1)
.

3.2
Duality for étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
A

If
D
is an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
we equip
\Hom_{A}(D,A)
with the structure of an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
.

Fix
D
an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
.

We first equip
D
with a left inverse
\psi:D\to D
of
\varphi
, as follows.
Fix a set of representatives
\{n\}
of
N_{0}/N_{0}^{p}
including 1.
Note that as
D
is étale, every element
x
of
D
can be uniquely written as
x=\sum_{N_{0}/N_{0}^{p}}\delta_{n}\varphi(x_{n})
, where
\delta_{n}
denotes the image of the element
[n]\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
in
A
.
Let
\psi:D\to D
be defined by
\psi(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}x_{1}
. The following easy lemma is left to the reader.

Lemma 3.2.1
.

The map
\psi:D\to D
is a left inverse of
\varphi
that is independent of any choices. We have

x=\sum_{N_{0}/N_{0}^{p}}\delta_{n}\varphi(\psi(\delta_{n}^{-1}x))
for any
x\in D
.
Moreover, the actions of
\psi
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
commute.

To define
\varphi
on
\Hom_{A}(D,A)
recall that we have

\displaystyle\beta:D

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}A\otimes_{\varphi}D

(69)

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{n}\delta_{n}\otimes_{\varphi}\psi(\delta_{n}^{-1}x),

where the sum runs over representatives
\{n\}
of
N_{0}/N_{0}^{p}
. Now if
M
,
N
are
A
-modules with
M
projective, we have for any
A
-algebra
B

a canonical isomorphism
B\otimes_{A}\Hom_{A}(M,N)\cong\Hom_{B}(B\otimes_{A}M,B\otimes_{A}N)
,
hence the
A
-linear dual of (
69
) gives rise to

A\otimes_{\varphi}\Hom_{A}(D,A)\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}\Hom_{A}(D,A),

in other words we get a
\varphi
-linear endomorphism of
\Hom_{A}(D,A)
that we also call
\varphi
(an étale
Frobenius). Explicitly, this endomorphism is given by the formula

\displaystyle\Hom_{A}(D,A)

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{A}(D,A)

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\mapsto\varphi(h)=(x\mapsto\sum_{N_{0}/N_{0}^{p}}\delta_{n}\varphi(h(\psi(\delta_{n}^{-1}x)))).

(70)

By construction, it is independent of the choice of representatives.

Using Lemma
3.2.1
we can rewrite formula (
70
) as follows:

\varphi(h):\sum_{n}\delta_{n}\varphi(x_{n})\mapsto\sum_{n}\delta_{n}\varphi(h(x_{n})).

(71)

We also define the action of
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
by the formula
a(h)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}a\circ h\circ a^{-1}
.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

With the definitions above,
\Hom_{A}(D,A)
is an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module. Moreover, the natural
pairing
D\times\Hom_{A}(D,A)\to A
is equivariant for the actions of
\varphi
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.

Concretely, the
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module structure on

\Hom_{A}(D,A)
is uniquely characterized by the relations, for

x\in D
,
y\in\Hom_{A}(D,A)
,
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
:

\langle\varphi(x),\varphi(y)\rangle=\varphi(\langle x,y\rangle),\qquad\langle a(x),a(y)\rangle=a(\langle x,y\rangle),

(72)

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the natural pairing

D\times\Hom_{A}(D,A)\rightarrow A
.

Fix now a smooth representation
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(K)
in the category
\mathcal{C}
and endow the finite
free
A
-module
D_{A}(\pi)
with its filtration coming from the

\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}
-adic filtration on
\pi^{\vee}
, cf. §
3.1
. If

D
is an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module (endowed with its
natural topology of finite free
A
-module), recall that

\Hom^{\cont}_{\mathbb{F}}(D,\mathbb{F})
is the vector space of continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear morphisms
D\rightarrow\mathbb{F}
, or equivalently (
\mathbb{F}
being endowed with the discrete topology) the
\mathbb{F}
-linear locally constant morphisms
D\rightarrow\mathbb{F}
. We give
\mathbb{F}
the filtration such that
F_{d}\,\mathbb{F}=0
if and only if
d<0
.

We write now
Y_{i}
for
Y_{\sigma_{i}}
(as in
[
BHH+
, §3.1.1]
, note that there will be no confusion with the variables
Y_{i}\in A_{q}
in §
2.2
which are not used here) and
\underline{Y}^{(i_{0},\dots,i_{f-1})}
for
Y_{0}^{i_{0}}\cdots Y_{f-1}^{i_{f-1}}\in A
(as in
[
BHH+
, §3.2.2]
).
We also sometimes use the shorthand
\underline{Y}
for
\underline{Y}^{\underline{1}}=\prod_{j=0}^{f-1}Y_{j}
.

Proposition 3.2.3
.

There is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
-modules between
\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}(D_{A}(\pi),\mathbb{F})
and the set of sequences
(x_{k})_{k\geq 0}
such that
x_{k}\in\pi
and

(i)

\underline{Y}x_{k}=x_{k-1}
for all
k\geq 1
;

(ii)

there exists
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
x_{k}\in\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{fk+d+1}]
for all
k\geq 0

(
where
\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{j}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}0
for
j\leq 0
)
.

A continuous
\mathbb{F}
-linear map
h:D_{A}(\pi)\to\mathbb{F}
corresponds to a sequence
(x_{k})_{k\geq 0}
as above if and only if

h(\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}}y)=\langle x_{k},y\rangle

for all
y\in\pi^{\vee}
,
k\geq 0
and where we denote again by
y
the image
of
y
in
D_{A}(\pi)
. Moreover,
h
is filtered of degree
d
if and only if
x_{k}\in\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{fk+d+1}]
for all
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
S
denote the multiplicative subset of
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
generated by
Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1}
.
Then from the definitions we have

(\pi^{\vee})_{S}\cong\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\geq 0\\
Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1}\end{subarray}}\pi^{\vee}\text{\ \ and\ \ }F_{-d-1}(\pi^{\vee})_{S}\cong\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\geq 0\\
Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{fk+d+1}\pi^{\vee},

so

(\pi^{\vee})_{S}/F_{-d-1}(\pi^{\vee})_{S}\cong\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\geq 0\\
Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1}\end{subarray}}\pi^{\vee}/\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{fk+d+1}\pi^{\vee}.

(Explicitly, the
k
-th map
\pi^{\vee}\to(\pi^{\vee})_{S}
is given by
multiplication by
(Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1})^{-k}
.)
Therefore, we have

\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}(D_{A}(\pi),\mathbb{F})=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}((\pi^{\vee})_{S},\mathbb{F})=\bigcup_{d\geq 0}\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}((\pi^{\vee})_{S}/F_{-d-1}(\pi^{\vee})_{S},\mathbb{F})\\
=\bigcup_{d\geq 0}\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}((\pi^{\vee})_{S}/F_{-d-1}(\pi^{\vee})_{S},\mathbb{F})=\bigcup_{d\geq 0}\varprojlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\geq 0\\
Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1}\end{subarray}}\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}(\pi^{\vee}/\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{fk+d+1}\pi^{\vee},\mathbb{F})\\
=\bigcup_{d\geq 0}\varprojlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\geq 0\\
Y_{0}\cdots Y_{f-1}\end{subarray}}\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{fk+d+1}].

The final claims follow by unravelling these identifications.
∎

We now make explicit the actions of
A
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\Hom_{\mathbb{F}}^{\cont}(D_{A}(\pi),\mathbb{F})
, where the definitions of these actions in the following lemma are a posteriori motivated by Lemma
3.3.5
(namely, the map
\mu_{*}
in (
84
) becomes
A
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
-linear).

Lemma 3.2.4
.

Suppose that
h:D_{A}(\pi)\to\mathbb{F}
is continuous of degree
d
, i.e. sending
F_{-d-1}D_{A}(\pi)
to 0.
Let
h
correspond to the sequence
(x_{k})_{k\geq 0}
as in Proposition
3.2.3
, so
\underline{Y}x_{k+1}=x_{k}
and

x_{k}\in\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{kf+d+1}]
.

(i)

If
a\in A
, then
ah\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}h\circ a
corresponds to the sequence
(y_{k})_{k\geq 0}
, where

y_{k}=\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}-\underline{k}}ax_{\ell}

(73)

for
\ell\gg_{k}0
.

(ii)

If
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
, then
a(h)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}N_{\mathbb{F}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\overline{a})^{-1}(h\circ\diag(a^{-1},1))
corresponds to the sequence
(z_{k})_{k\geq 0}
, where

z_{k}=N_{\mathbb{F}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\overline{a})^{-1}\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{}\\
{}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\frac{\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}}}{a^{-1}(\underline{Y}^{\underline{k}})}x_{\ell}=N_{\mathbb{F}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\overline{a})^{-1}\frac{a(\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}})}{\underline{Y}^{\underline{k}}}\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{}\\
{}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)x_{\ell}

(74)

for
\ell\gg_{k}0
.

Remark 3.2.5
.

To explain the notation in equations (
73
), (
74
) we note that for
x\in\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{e}]
(
e\geq 0
) we
can extend the action of
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
on
x
to an action of the ring
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket+F_{-e}A
such that
F_{-e}A
kills
x

(because
F_{-e}\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket\cap F_{-e}A
kills
x
, by assumption). For (
73
) we note that

\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}}a\in A=\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket_{S}+F_{-d-1}A
(where
S
is generated by
\underline{Y}
), so
\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}-\underline{k}}a\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket+F_{-\ell f-d-1}A
for
\ell\gg_{k}0
and

x_{\ell}\in\pi[\mathfrak{m}_{I_{1}}^{\ell f+d+1}]
. Similarly for (
74
) we note that
\frac{a(\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}})}{\underline{Y}^{\underline{k}}}\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket+F_{-\ell f-d-1}A

for
\ell\gg_{k}0
(and
\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{}\\
{}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
normalizes
I_{1}
).

Proof.

For (i) we first note that
h(F_{-d-1}A\cdot\pi^{\vee})\subseteq h(F_{-d-1}D_{A}(\pi))=0
, so
h\circ a^{\prime}|_{\pi^{\vee}}
only
depends on
a^{\prime}
modulo
F_{-d-1}A
. Writing
\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}}a\in\underline{Y}^{-\underline{\ell}}b+F_{-d-1}A
as above with
b\in\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket

and
\ell\gg_{k}0
, we compute for
k\geq 0
,

h\circ a\circ\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}}|_{\pi^{\vee}}=h\circ\underline{Y}^{-\underline{\ell}}\circ b|_{\pi^{\vee}}=\langle x_{\ell},b(-)\rangle=\langle bx_{\ell},-\rangle=\langle\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}-\underline{k}}ax_{\ell},-\rangle

(75)

as functions
\pi^{\vee}\to\mathbb{F}
, as desired (keeping in mind Remark
3.2.5
).

For (ii), first note that
a(h)\circ\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}}=N_{\mathbb{F}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\overline{a})^{-1}\big(h\circ a^{-1}(\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}})\circ\diag(a^{-1},1)\big)
. By
(
75
) (applied with
k=0
),

h\circ a^{-1}(\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}})|_{\pi^{\vee}}=\langle\underline{Y}^{\underline{\ell}}\cdot a^{-1}(\underline{Y}^{-\underline{k}})x_{\ell},-\rangle
for
\ell\gg_{k}0

and the result follows.
∎

3.3
The continuous morphism
\mu:A\to\mathbb{F}

For
D
an étale
(\varphi,\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A
we relate
\Hom_{A}(D,A)
to
\Hom^{\cont}_{\mathbb{F}}(D,\mathbb{F})
using a certain continuous morphism
\mu:A\to\mathbb{F}
.

Let us write
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket=\mathbb{F}\llbracket T_{0},\dots,T_{f-1}\rrbracket
with
T_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}[\alpha_{j}]-1
, where
(\alpha_{j})_{j\in\{0,\dots,f-1\}}
is a fixed
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-basis of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Recall that
A
is endowed with a map
\psi:A\rightarrow A
defined in §
3.2
, and which is a left inverse of
\varphi:A\rightarrow A
.

Proposition 3.3.1
.

Up to scalar in
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
there exists a unique
\mu\in\Hom^{\cont}_{\mathbb{F}}(A,\mathbb{F})
such that
\mu\circ\psi\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}\mu
,
and we have

\mu\circ\psi=(-1)^{f-1}\mu
.

It will be convenient for the proof to avoid using the variables
Y_{j}
.
To obtain
A
from
\mathbb{F}\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
it suffices to invert elements
Z_{j}
(
0\leq j\leq f-1
) such that
\gr(Z_{j})=\gr(Y_{j})
in the graded ring and
then complete.
We will let
Z_{j}
be the unique linear combination of the
T_{j^{\prime}}
such that
\gr(Z_{j})=\gr(Y_{j})
.
(Note that the
Z_{j}
are not canonical but depend on the choice of
T_{0}
, …,
T_{f-1}
.)
There exists an element of
\GL_{f}(\mathbb{F})
that
relates the
Z_{j}
and the
T_{j}
. Hence we get the same description of
A
as in
[
BHH+
, Rk. 3.1.1.3(iii)]
with
Z_{j}
instead of

Y_{j}
, and also the valuation of an element of
A
is still given by the minimal total degree as a series in
\underline{Z}
. We note that

\varphi(T_{j})=T_{j}^{p}
and
\varphi(Z_{j})=Z_{j-1}^{p}
(because
\varphi(Z_{j})
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
p
in the
T_{j^{\prime}}
and hence in the
Z_{j^{\prime}}
,
and since
\varphi(Y_{j})=Y_{j-1}^{p}
).

Before starting the proof of Proposition
3.3.1
we note that
\mu\circ\psi=c\mu
(with
c\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
) is equivalent to the two conditions

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=c(\mu\circ\varphi),

(76)

\displaystyle\mu(\delta_{n}\varphi(x))

\displaystyle=0\qquad\qquad\forall\ n\in N_{0}\setminus N_{0}^{p},\forall\ x\in A.

(77)

This follows immediately from the definition of
\psi:D\to D
in §
3.2
.

Proof
(
Uniqueness
)
.

Suppose that
\mu\circ\psi=c\mu
for some
c\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
.
For the representatives
\{n\}
of
N_{0}/N_{0}^{p}
we take
n=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{1}&{\sum_{j}i_{j}\alpha_{j}}\\
{}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
(
0\leq i_{j}\leq p-1
), so
\delta_{n}=\prod_{j}(1+T_{j})^{i_{j}}
.
By induction and (
76
)–(
77
) we have
for any
\underline{0}\leq\underline{i}\leq\underline{p-1}
that

\displaystyle\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{i}}\varphi(x))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\|\underline{i}\|}\mu(\varphi(x))

(78)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\|\underline{i}\|}c^{-1}\mu(x).

Take now
x\in F_{f-1}A
. Then by iterating (
78
) we have

\mu(x)=c\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{p-1}}\varphi(x))=\cdots=c^{n}\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{p^{n}-1}}\varphi^{n}(x))=0

for
n\gg 0
, since
\underline{T}^{\underline{p^{n}-1}}\varphi^{n}(x)\to 0
in
A
as
n\to\infty
if
x\in F_{f-1}A
and
\mu
is continuous. Hence

\mu(F_{f-1}A)=0.

(79)

We claim that
\mu(\underline{Z}^{\underline{i}})
for
\underline{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}
is an explicit multiple of
\mu(\underline{Z}^{-\underline{1}})
, only depending on
c
.
To prove the claim, we may suppose that
\|\underline{i}\|\leq-f
by (
79
) and we will argue by descending induction on
\|\underline{i}\|
.
Write
\underline{i}=\underline{r}+p\underline{s}
with

\underline{0}\leq\underline{r}\leq\underline{p-1}
and
\underline{s}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{f}
. Hence
\mu(\underline{Z}^{\underline{i}})=\mu(\underline{Z}^{\underline{r}}\underline{Z}^{p\underline{s}})
and
that can be expressed in terms of various
\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{r^{\prime}}}\underline{Z}^{p\underline{s}})
with
\underline{r^{\prime}}\geq\underline{0}
and

\|\underline{r^{\prime}}\|=\|\underline{r}\|
. Fix now one such term and write
\underline{r^{\prime}}=\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}+p\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}
with

\underline{0}\leq\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}\leq\underline{p-1}
and
\underline{0}\leq\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}
. Then we can express
\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{r^{\prime}}}\underline{Z}^{p\underline{s}})=\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}}\underline{T}^{p\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\underline{Z}^{p\underline{s}})
in
terms of various
\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}}\underline{Z}^{p\underline{t}})
with
\|\underline{t}\|=\|\underline{s}\|+\|\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\|
. By (
78
) we are
reduced to
\pm\mu(\underline{Z}^{p\underline{t}})=\pm c^{-1}\mu(\underline{Z}^{\underline{t^{\prime}}})
, where
\underline{t^{\prime}}
is a cyclic permutation of
\underline{t}

and hence
\|\underline{t^{\prime}}\|=\|\underline{t}\|=\|\underline{s}\|+\|\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\|=(\|\underline{i}\|-\|\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}\|)/p
.

From
\|\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}\|\leq(p-1)f
and
\|\underline{i}\|\leq-f
it follows that
\|\underline{i}\|\leq\|\underline{t^{\prime}}\|
and moreover that equality can only
hold if
\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}=\underline{p-1}
and
\|\underline{i}\|=-f
, in which case
\underline{r}=\underline{r^{\prime}}=\underline{p-1}
and
\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}=\underline{0}
(as
\|\underline{r}\|=\|\underline{r^{\prime\prime}}\|+p\|\underline{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\|\leq(p-1)f
).
Thus
\|\underline{i}\|<\|\underline{t^{\prime}}\|
and we are done by induction, except possibly when
\|\underline{i}\|=-f
and
\underline{i}\equiv-\underline{1}\pmod{p}
.
Applying the same argument to
\mu(\underline{Z}^{\underline{t^{\prime}}})
, we are done in the exceptional case except if
\underline{t^{\prime}}\equiv-\underline{1}\pmod{p}
, which implies
\underline{s}=\underline{t}\equiv-\underline{1}\pmod{p}

and hence
\underline{i}=\underline{p-1}+p\underline{s}\equiv-\underline{1}\pmod{p^{2}}
. By iterating we are left with the case
\underline{i}=-\underline{1}
, which completes the proof of the claim.

Finally we show that
c
is uniquely determined (assuming
\mu\neq 0
). Consider
\underline{i}=-\underline{1}
above. Then

\mu(\underline{Z}^{-\underline{1}})=\mu(\underline{Z}^{\underline{p-1}}\underline{Z}^{-\underline{p}})=c^{\prime}\mu(\underline{T}^{\underline{p-1}}\underline{Z}^{-\underline{p}})=c^{\prime}c^{-1}\mu(\underline{Z}^{-\underline{1}}),

where
c^{\prime}
is the coefficient of
\underline{T}^{\underline{p-1}}
in
\underline{Z}^{\underline{p-1}}
. Here, the second equality follows from the
analysis in the preceding paragraph (the case
\|\underline{i}\|=-f
) that all other intervening terms
\underline{T}^{\underline{r^{\prime}}}\underline{Z}^{-\underline{p}}
with
\underline{r^{\prime}}\geq\underline{0}
and

\|\underline{r^{\prime}}\|=\|\underline{p-1}\|
lie in the kernel of
\mu
(by (
79
)). The third equality follows from (
78
) with
\underline{i}=\underline{p-1}
.
Hence
c=c^{\prime}
is uniquely determined.
∎

Proof
(
Existence
)
.

We define

\mu(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\varepsilon_{-\underline{1}}(x\prod_{j}(1+T_{j})^{-1})

(80)

for
x\in A
, where
\varepsilon_{-\underline{1}}(y)
is the coefficient of
\underline{Z}^{-\underline{1}}
in
y
for
y\in A
(expanded in terms of
the
\underline{Z}^{\underline{i}}
as in
[
BHH+
, Rk. 3.1.1.3(iii)]
. Then
\mu\in\Hom^{\cont}_{\mathbb{F}}(A,\mathbb{F})
, as
\mu(F_{0}A)=\{0\}
.

By (
76
)–(
77
) it suffices to show that for
\underline{1}\leq\underline{i}\leq\underline{p}
we have

\varepsilon_{-\underline{1}}(\prod_{j}(1+T_{j})^{i_{j}-1}\varphi(x))=0\text{\ \ if $\underline{i}\neq\underline{p}$}

(81)

and

\varepsilon_{-\underline{1}}(\prod_{j}(1+T_{j})^{p-1}\varphi(x))=(-1)^{f-1}\varepsilon_{-\underline{1}}(x).

(82)

(This time we take representatives
n=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}{1}&{\sum_{j}i_{j}\alpha_{j}}\\
{}&{1}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
with
1\leq i_{j}\leq p
.)

Recalling that we can write

Z_{j}=\sum_{i}a_{ij}T_{i}\text{\ \ for some $(a_{ij})\in\GL_{f}(\mathbb{F})$},

(83)

we deduce (
81
) and reduce (
82
) to showing that
the coefficient of
\underline{Z}^{\underline{p-1}}
in
\underline{T}^{\underline{p-1}}
equals
(-1)^{f-1}
.
From (
83
), by considering the action of
\varphi
and letting
a_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}a_{i0}
, we obtain that

Z_{j}=\sum_{i}a_{i}^{p^{j}}T_{i}\text{\ \ with $(a_{i}^{p^{j}})\in\GL_{f}(\mathbb{F})$}.

As
a_{i}^{p^{f}}=a_{i}
, the
a_{i}
are in the image of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
in
\mathbb{F}
and in fact they form an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-basis of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
(If not, then
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}a_{i}=0
for some
\lambda_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
that are not all zero. This implies that

\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}a_{i}^{p^{j}}=0
for all
0\leq j\leq f-1
, contradicting that
(a_{i}^{p^{j}})\in\GL_{f}(\mathbb{F})
.)

Let us now work with formal variables
\underline{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}(x_{i})_{0\leq i\leq f-1}
and
b_{i}
(
0\leq i\leq f-1
).

Lemma 3.3.2
.

The coefficient of
\underline{x}^{\underline{p-1}}(=\prod_{j}x_{j}^{p-1})
in
\prod_{j}(\sum_{i}b_{i}^{p^{j}}x_{i})^{p-1}
equals

\prod_{\underline{c}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{f}-\{\underline{0}\})/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}}(\sum_{i}c_{i}b_{i})^{p-1}=(-1)^{(p^{f}-1)/(p-1)}\prod_{\underline{c}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{f}-\{\underline{0}\}}(\sum_{i}c_{i}b_{i}).

(Note that the first product does not depend on the choice of representatives, and for the equality note that
\prod_{x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}}x=-1
.)

This lemma implies what we want: as the
a_{i}
form an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-basis of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, the lemma (applied with
x_{i}=T_{i}
,
b_{i}=a_{i}
) shows
that the coefficient of
\underline{T}^{\underline{p-1}}
in
\underline{Z}^{\underline{p-1}}
equals
-(-1)^{(p^{f}-1)/(p-1)}=(-1)^{f-1}
, as
\prod_{x\in k^{\times}}x=-1
.

To prove Lemma
3.3.2
, we use the following.

Sublemma 3.3.3
.

Suppose
h\in\mathbb{F}[x_{0},\dots,x_{f-1}]
. Then the coefficient of
\underline{x}^{\underline{p-1}}
in
h
is invariant under any linear change of variables over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, i.e. is equal to the coefficient of
\underline{y}^{\underline{p-1}}
in
h
if
\underline{x}
and
\underline{y}
are related by an element
\gamma
of
\GL_{f}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

(This is presumably well known. For the proof we may assume that
h
is a monomial and that
\gamma
is an elementary transformation, in which case
it follows from the facts that
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
is of order
p-1
and that
\binom{r}{p-1}=0
for
p\leq r\leq 2p-2
.)

Let
C
denote the coefficient of
\underline{x}^{\underline{p-1}}(=\prod_{j}x_{j}^{p-1})
in
\prod_{j}(\sum_{i}b_{i}^{p^{j}}x_{i})^{p-1}
.
Then
C\in\mathbb{F}[b_{0},\dots,b_{f-1}]
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
p^{f}-1
, which is clearly divisible by
\underline{b}^{\underline{p-1}}
.
For any linear change of variables
x_{i}=\sum_{i}\lambda_{ij}y_{j}
with
\lambda_{ij}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
Sublemma
3.3.3
then implies that
\prod_{j}(\sum_{i}b_{i}\lambda_{ij})^{p-1}
divides
C
.
In particular,
(\sum_{i}c_{i}b_{i})^{p-1}
divides
C
for each
c\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{f}-\{\underline{0}\})/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
.
But the product of such polynomials is already of degree
p^{f}-1
and they are pairwise relatively prime, hence we are done by
remarking that the coefficient of
\prod_{i}b_{i}^{p^{i}(p-1)}
is the same on both sides.
∎

Remark 3.3.4
.

Fix
\mu\neq 0
as in Proposition
3.3.1
.
By uniqueness we must have
\mu\circ a^{-1}\in\mathbb{F}\mu
for any
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
