p-2-r_{j},p-3-r_{j+1},\cdots,p-1-r_{0},p-3-r_{1},\dots,p-3-r_{j^{\prime}-1},r_{j^{\prime}}+1
for some
0<j^{\prime}<j\leq f-1
, and one checks the following congruence relation mod
q-1
:

\frac{1}{2}\big((p-2-2r_{j})+\sum_{j<i<j^{\prime},i\neq 0}p^{i}(p-3-2r_{i})+(p-1-2r_{0})+p^{j^{\prime}}+(q-1)\big)\equiv\sum_{j\leq i\leq j^{\prime}}p^{i}b_{J,i},

where
\sum_{j<i<j^{\prime}}
means
\sum_{j<i\leq f-1}+\sum_{0\leq i<j^{\prime}}
and similarly for
\sum_{j\leq i\leq j^{\prime}}
.

Together with (
115
), claim (a) is verified in this case.

We check claim (b). Using Lemma
3.4.7
and the definition of
\underline{b_{J}}
one checks that

s_{0}

r_{0}

r_{0}-1

p-2-r_{0}

p-1-r_{0}

\frac{a_{d^{\prime},0}^{\sigma}}{1-p^{d^{\prime}}}+1-b_{J,0}

\frac{h}{1+q}

0

h

\frac{hq}{1+q}

while if
1\leq i\leq f-1
we have

s_{i}

r_{i}

r_{i}+1

p-2-r_{i}

p-3-r_{i}

\frac{a_{d^{\prime},i}^{\sigma}}{1-p^{d^{\prime}}}+1-b_{J,i}

0

-\frac{hp^{f-i}}{1+q}

\frac{hp^{f-i}}{1+q}

0

Then (b) can easily be checked case by case.

(ii) Assume
\overline{\rho}
is (split) reducible.
For
J\subseteq\{0,1,\dots,f-1\}
, with corresponding Serre weight
\sigma\in W(\overline{\rho})
, define

\vartheta:\ E_{\underline{i_{J}}}\longmapsto\alpha_{J}Y^{\underline{b_{J}}-\underline{1}}x_{J}^{*},

where
\alpha_{J}\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}
are suitable constants,
\underline{i_{J}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{{def}}}}}{{=}}\mathbf{1}_{J^{c}}
(i.e.
i_{J,j}=1
if
j\notin J
and
i_{J,j}=0
if
j\in J
), and

•

b_{J,i}=0
if
s_{i}=r_{i}
;

•

b_{J,i}=-h^{[i]}
if
s_{i}=p-2-r_{i}
;

•

b_{J,i}=h^{[i]}+1
if
s_{i}=r_{i}+1
and
i>0
(resp.
b_{J,0}=1
if
i=0
);

•

b_{J,i}=0
if
s_{i}=p-3-r_{i}
and
i>0
(resp.
b_{J,0}=-h^{[0]}
if
i=0
).

Write
J^{\prime}=J_{\delta(\sigma)}
. Then (
111
) remains true, and it is easy to check that

\varphi(E_{\underline{i_{J}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rll}\lambda_{0}(\frac{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}{Y_{\sigma_{f-1}}^{p}})^{h}E_{\underline{i_{J^{\prime}}}}&\text{if $i_{J,0}=0$,}\\
\lambda_{1}E_{\underline{i_{J^{\prime}}}}&\text{if $i_{J,0}=1$}.\end{array}\right.

Thus, to check that
\vartheta
is
\varphi
-equivariant it is equivalent to check

•

if
i_{J,0}=0
then

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\alpha_{J}\cdot\mu_{J}=(-1)^{f-1}\lambda_{0}\alpha_{J^{\prime}},\\
p\delta(\underline{b_{J}})+\underline{1-p}+\underline{c_{J^{\prime}}}=\underline{b_{J^{\prime}}}+(h,0,\dots,-ph);\end{array}\right.

(116)

•

if
i_{J,0}=1
then

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\alpha_{J}\cdot\mu_{J}=(-1)^{f-1}\lambda_{1}\alpha_{J^{\prime}},\\
p\delta(\underline{b_{J}})+\underline{1-p}+\underline{c_{J^{\prime}}}=\underline{b_{J^{\prime}}}.\end{array}\right.

(117)

We leave it as an exercise to check the second equation of (
116
), resp. (
117
), using Lemma
3.4.1
. Thus, to show that the constants
\alpha_{J}
can be compatibly chosen so that
\vartheta
is
\varphi
-equivariant, it suffices to check, for any
J
whose orbit has length
d
,

(-1)^{d(f-1)}\prod_{j=0}^{d-1}\mu_{\delta^{j}(J)}=\lambda_{0}^{|J|\frac{d}{f}}\lambda_{0}^{-|\overline{J}|\frac{d}{f}},

where we have used
{\det}(\overline{\rho})(p)=1
and the fact that

\sharp\big\{0\leq j\leq d-1,0\in\delta^{j}(J)\big\}=|J|\frac{d}{f},\ \ \sharp\big\{0\leq j\leq d-1,0\notin\delta^{j}(J)\big\}=|\overline{J}|\frac{d}{f}.

We conclude by Lemma
3.4.8
and (
97
).

We now check that
\vartheta
is
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
-equivariant. Using (
109
) we know that

a(E_{i_{J}})=\prod_{i:i_{J,i}=0}\varphi^{f-1-i}(f_{a,0}^{h(1-\varphi)/(1-q)})E_{i_{J}}

and by Corollary
3.8.3
we have

a(x_{J}^{*})=N_{\mathbb{F}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\overline{a})\chi_{\sigma}(\overline{a})^{-1}\overline{a}^{\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}p^{i}r_{i}}\bigg(\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}f_{a,i}^{a_{d^{\prime},i}^{\sigma}/(1-p^{d^{\prime}})}\bigg)x_{J}^{*}.

Thus it suffices to show that

a(\underline{Y}^{\underline{b_{J}}-\underline{1}})N_{\mathbb{F}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\overline{a})\chi_{\sigma}(\overline{a})^{-1}\overline{a}^{\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}p^{i}r_{i}}\bigg(\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}f_{a,i}^{a_{d^{\prime},i}^{\sigma}/(1-p^{d^{\prime}})}\bigg)\\
=\prod_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0\leq i\leq f-1}}{{i_{J,i}=0}}}(f_{a,i+1}^{hp^{f-i-1}/(1-q)}f_{a,i}^{-hp^{f-i}/(1-q)})\underline{Y}^{\underline{b_{J}}-\underline{1}},

which is implied by the following claims (where we use that
f\mid d^{\prime}
):

(a)

\chi_{\sigma}(\overline{a})=\overline{a}^{\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}p^{i}b_{J,i}+\sum_{i=0}^{f-1}p^{i}r_{i}}
,

(b)

\displaystyle\frac{a_{d^{\prime},i}^{\sigma}}{1-p^{d^{\prime}}}+1-b_{J,i}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{hp^{f-i}}{1-q}[\mathbf{1}_{J}(i-1)-\mathbf{1}_{J}(i)]&\text{if $1\leq i\leq f-1$,}\\
\displaystyle\frac{h}{1-q}[\mathbf{1}_{J}(f-1)-q\mathbf{1}_{J}(0)]&\text{if $i=0$}.\end{cases}

Both claims are checked as in the irreducible case (we omit the details).
∎
